# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains right-click context menus** ([#1602](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1602), [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §6): right-clicking a tree row opens the action menu at the pointer instead of merely moving the cursor. The machinery was almost all there already — `popup::ActionMenu` filtered by `actions::applicable_actions` was reachable only from the `a` key, and right-button events were already hit-tested to a region — so what this adds is the three missing joins: **classification** (right-button opens a menu), **anchored placement** (`popup::place` is a pure function of anchor, content size and frame size, flipping up at the bottom edge and left at the right edge, clamping only when neither side fits), and **mouse interaction inside a popup**. That last one is the single place Phase 4a's "popups are chrome and chrome is inert" framing had to move, and it moves narrowly: a `popup_mouse_enabled` predicate names the one popup kind that takes the mouse, so the confirm modal's keyboard-only `y`/`n` — a feature, since it gates destructive actions — is untouched. An open menu out-ranks every other region in `RegionMap::hit`, and the first click outside is *consumed* by the dismissal rather than applied to what it landed on. Targeting follows the universal convention: right-clicking inside the marked set acts on the whole set, otherwise the cursor moves to that row and the menu acts on it alone — both end at `tree.targets`, so the action filter is unchanged and **no new capability is reachable from a menu that was not already reachable from `a`**.
  - The row-colour picker is folded in as a *Row Colour ▸* **submenu**, the one level of nesting v1 has. `MenuEntry::Submenu` holds a flat `Vec<MenuItem>` rather than `Vec<MenuEntry>`, which makes "one level only" structural instead of a convention someone can quietly break, and `ActionMenu::activate` is the single method both `Enter` and a click go through, so the key and mouse routes cannot disagree about which rows expand and which dispatch. The submenu is sized to its content and placed beside its parent (a 50-column submenu inheriting the parent's fixed width had nowhere to go but on top of it in an 80-column terminal), hit-tested *before* its parent since it draws on top, and closed rather than left dangling if its parent row scrolls out of view — so what `Enter` dispatches is always what is highlighted. Menus also gained separators between the `0_open`/`1_pr`/`2_claude`/`3_copy`/`4_git`/`9_close` groups, and scrolling with `…` affordances for menus taller than the frame.
  - **The menu is never mouse-only.** `a` still opens it centred, and `alt-m` (or `F10`, with or without `⇧`) opens the *context* menu for whatever has focus, anchored at the cursor. `⇧F10` is deliberately **not** a `keys::chrome_key` chord: `encode_key` already sends `F(10)` to a child and ADR-0072 §5 makes `Alt`-chords the only chrome keys, so claiming it globally would take it from children and punch a hole in the rule that lets this UI avoid a tmux-style prefix — it is handled tree-side, which is exactly where no child is listening. Inside a menu, `↑`/`↓`/`j`/`k`/`Home`/`End`/`Enter`/`Esc` work as before, plus `←`/`→` for the submenu, and one `Esc` closes one level.
  - Two ways a menu could outlive its subject are closed. The tree is live, so a daemon push can delete the worktree a menu is acting on while it is open; `ActionMenu::origin_row` records the row and the menu closes with it, rather than being left offering *Close Worktree* for whatever row slid into its place. And a right-click *outside* an open menu re-targets — the menu closes and the press opens a fresh one where it landed — while any other button dismisses only.
  - *Paste is deliberately excluded.* `clipboard.rs` only ever **writes**; adding a menu item that reads the host clipboard and writes it to a PTY would open a clipboard→child byte path that does not exist today, which is a real capability change and wants its own decision rather than a free ride. The `Event::Paste` route stays what it is: host-initiated bracketed paste, where the user's own terminal decided to send the bytes.
  - **The tab strip and the terminal grid get menus of their own.** The strip offers the conventional tab gestures, none of which existed before — *Close Tab*, *Close Other Tabs*, *Close Tabs to the Right*, *Move to Group Above/Below* (hidden, not greyed, when there is no such group), *Split Below*, *New Shell Tab*, *New Claude Tab* — and right-clicking a tab focuses it first, so the menu's subject is also the tab you can see highlighted. The grid offers *Copy* (disabled without a selection rather than hidden, since it is the reason to open the menu at all), *Select All*, *Clear Selection*, *Find in Scrollback*, *Scroll to Bottom* and *Close Tab*. Contents are built by pure functions in a new `menu.rs` taking small inputs, so "the strip menu hides *Move to Group Above* when there is no group above" is a direct unit test rather than a rendered one. Entries that already have a chord dispatch through that chord's own handler, so a menu item and its shortcut cannot drift apart.
  - **Contract §4 keeps its priority and is not weakened.** A live child that requested mouse reporting still receives the right-button event and no menu opens; `⌥` is how you take it back — exactly the existing rule for the left button, so there is no new modifier and no new precedence. The grid menu is reached only *after* that check.
  - **A measured correction to an older claim.** The Phase 0 spike found that `mouse.rs` §5, [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §6(3) and this guide all described the host's selection escape hatch as *shift*-drag, naming iTerm2 first among the terminals honouring it. Measured on macOS, both halves were wrong: Ghostty keeps shift-drag, **iTerm2 keeps option-drag and forwards shift-drag to the app**, and the VS Code terminal keeps neither and relies on the `⌥`-reclaim. The mechanism stands — the host keeps the gesture and the UI must not detect it — but the modifier is terminal-specific and is not ours to encode. All three places are corrected.
  - **The splitter and the surrounding chrome finish the surface**, and fix a latent bug on the way past: a splitter drag was started without ever checking which button was down, so a **right-drag on a pane boundary resized the pair**. Only the left button resizes now; the right opens a layout menu (*Reset Layout*, *Close Group Above/Below*), and empty tree space or the status bar offers a small global menu (*New Shell Tab*, *Reset Layout*, *Quit*). Menu-invoked *Quit* routes through the same confirm gate `q` uses, so a live child is still asked about rather than reached by a second quit path.
  - **Two guards keep the surface from widening by accident.** A grep guard fails the build if any of `clipboard.rs`/`app.rs`/`menu.rs`/`popup.rs`/`terminal/mod.rs` gains a host-clipboard *read* — the other half of the emulator's `Osc52::OnlyCopy` ([ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §4), so neither a child nor the UI can read the clipboard and *Paste* cannot arrive without an explicit decision. And a structural test asserts every menu command is reachable without a mouse: each is an `ActionKind` (the `a` menu and `:` palette), a `ChromeKey` (its own chord), or one of an enumerated menu-only set — so that set cannot grow silently. A companion check pins that no menu opens or closes with a separator or shows two in a row, the classic bug when entries are hidden conditionally.
- **`omni-dev drive sheets info` / `drive sheets read` — reading the *cells* of a Google Sheet** ([#1589](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1589), [ADR-0073](docs/adrs/adr-0073.md)): the Drive API cannot do this at all — it treats a Sheet as an opaque native document with no notion of a range, a row or a cell, which is why `drive read --content` on a Sheet exports **the first sheet only** (Drive's export API has no multi-sheet CSV format). `drive sheets read` closes that gap: with no `--range`/`--sheet` it reads every tab (one `spreadsheets.get` for the titles, then `values.batchGet`), and narrows to a range or a named tab on request. Needs no new login flag — the Sheets API accepts the `drive.readonly` scope every account already has. `-o table` (the default) emits CSV, since a grid of cells is what a spreadsheet range *is*; multi-sheet output separates each block with a `# <title>` line. Two rendering rules are deliberate and documented: CSV **pads** rows to the widest row (the API truncates trailing empty cells, so raw rows are ragged and a ragged CSV is malformed) while `-o json`/`-o yaml` preserve the raggedness as the truthful shape; and cell values are emitted **verbatim** rather than stripped of control characters, because they are content — a multi-line cell survives intact as a quoted CSV field — while sheet *titles*, rendered as chrome, are sanitised. `--render formatted|unformatted|formula` selects locale-formatted strings, raw typed numbers, or formula text. Sheet titles are always quoted internally, so spaces, apostrophes and `!` need no special handling and a sheet literally titled `A1` is unambiguous; A1 *grammar* is deliberately left to the server, so unbounded forms (`A:A`, `1:2`, a bare defined name) pass through rather than being rejected by a local guess. Writing cells (`sheets write`/`append`/`clear`/`create`) is the next phase.
  - Under the hood this adds the integration's **first second-host Google API**. Sheets lives on `sheets.googleapis.com`, which no base-URL tweak to `DriveClient` (built around `/drive/v3/...` on `www.googleapis.com`) could reach, so the host-agnostic half of that client — OAuth session, one-shot 401 refresh-and-retry, quota backoff, request logging, error-envelope parsing — moved into a shared `GoogleApiClient` that both clients wrap. They stay **distinct types** so a Sheets-hosted client cannot be handed to `FilesApi` and silently issue `/drive/v3/files` against the wrong host, and they **share one OAuth session**, so a command touching both APIs refreshes the token once rather than twice. `DriveClient`'s public API is unchanged.
  - Fixes a latent bug the second host exposed: Drive v3 reports error codes at `error.errors[0].reason`, but newer Google services — Sheets v4 among them — return the `google.rpc` envelope with `error.status` and **no `errors[]` array at all**. Reading only the legacy shape yields no reason code, which silently disables both the `--write-file`/`--write-full` scope hint and the quota retry — neither fails loudly, they simply never fire. Both envelope shapes are now understood.
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` remembers your layout, labels sessions readably, and clears all row colours (Phase 5 — completes issue [#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585))**: the pane layout now survives a restart. Quitting records the shape of the workspace — how many pane groups, their relative sizes, which worktree each tab was opened in and which was active — to `~/.omni-dev/worktrees-ui-layout.yaml` (`0600` under a `0700` directory, atomic rename, beside the existing row-colour store), and the next run reopens it. What is restored is the *shape*, never a live process: PTYs are hosted in this process ([ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §2), so each restored tab is a **new** child in the same place. Restoration is best-effort and silent by design — a worktree deleted since, a shell that no longer exists, a file from a newer version: each is dropped rather than turned into a startup error, and a tab that will not spawn costs you that tab rather than the session (the count is reported in the status bar). Quitting with no tabs removes the file, so the next run starts clean. The Move/Copy Claude Session picker now labels each session with the **first user prompt from its transcript** instead of a bare UUID, which is what the VS Code companion's `readPreview` does and for the same reason: a UUID tells you nothing about which session you are about to move. The reader is deliberately tolerant of Claude's transcript schema — it scans only the head of the file for the first user message and gives up quietly on anything unexpected, rather than parsing a structure that is not ours to depend on — and no transcript content is logged or persisted. Finally `alt-⇧c` clears every row colour at once, retiring the last `#[allow(dead_code)]` placeholder in the module (`HubCommand::ClearAllRowColors` has been wired since Phase 1 with no key to reach it).
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains the batch git actions (Phase 4d — completes Phase 4)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585), [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §9): **Rebase on main**, **Push (force-with-lease)** and **Add to Merge Queue** join the action menu's `4_git` group, completing the VS Code tree view's parity surface. Each drives the **daemon's** `rebase`/`push`/`merge-queue` op — the same op the companion extension calls, running the same engine — never the CLI's local-only path, and each is two-phase like `close`: the daemon plans, the plan is rendered verbatim into the confirm modal, and phase 2 re-plans from scratch so what was confirmed is advisory rather than a replayable token. The plans are specific: a rebase names each worktree, its branch and how far behind it is, and warns that a conflict leaves that worktree mid-rebase to resolve in place; a push separates plain fast-forwards from leased forces; a merge-queue check lists the eligible PRs and why each skipped one was skipped. **No force option exists anywhere in the UI** — every force the daemon issues is `--force-with-lease --force-if-includes`, a repository's default branch is never force-pushed, and a refused lease is reported as such with the fix named (fetch and rebase), since there is no harder push to reach for. A guard test greps the UI's action, client and wire modules so a `force` field cannot be added to a request without failing the build; the client also sends no `remote` or `onto` override, each of which would be a way around a guard that lives in the daemon.
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains the glyph table, badge columns, scrollback search and a command palette (Phase 4c)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585)): every row cue now comes from one table (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/glyph.rs`) with a unicode and an ASCII form, and **every form is asserted to be exactly one cell wide in both modes** — a glyph that measures two cells misaligns every column after it on that row alone, so the East-Asian *Ambiguous* characters the issue's mockups used (`✔ ⟳ ⚠ ▌ ⇊ ↑ ↓`) are deliberately excluded in favour of confirmed-Narrow substitutes. The new `--ascii` flag (or `OMNI_DEV_UI_ASCII=1`), resolved once at startup, switches the whole table over for terminals without a unicode font. Each worktree row gains a two-cell badge column — PR-check state and Claude-session state, one cell each — which is where a TUI is simply better than the VS Code companion: the extension negotiates two characters of `FileDecoration` between its two providers ([#1406](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1406)), while a TUI owns every cell and gives each dimension its own. Long branch names are elided in the middle and padded to a fixed column, so paths and fields line up down the pane. `alt-f` searches the focused tab's scrollback case-insensitively and scrolls the match into view, stepping further back on each `Enter`; `:` opens a command palette that filters the same action set the menu uses, and opens the menu at the match rather than firing it, so destructive actions keep their confirm. The tree also now reports which rows are actually on screen (`SetVisibleRows`), so the per-worktree ahead/behind fetch — the dominant cost ([#1306](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1306)) — covers the visible rows instead of every row in the snapshot, and is sent only when the set changes rather than every frame.

## [0.43.0] - 2026-09-05

### Added
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` — a full-screen terminal UI for the worktrees tree (Phase 1: read-only view)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585)): a `ratatui`-based app that supersedes `worktrees tree --follow` for interactive use, redrawing on every live `worktrees`/`sessions` push from the daemon rather than one static snapshot. It reuses the daemon's existing `subscribe` push protocol behind a new reconnect-with-full-jitter-backoff supervisor (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/supervisor.rs`) that falls back to one-shot polling against an older daemon that doesn't know the `subscribe` op, and joins the tree/sessions feeds with a local ahead/behind cache, row-colour store (`~/.omni-dev/worktrees-ui-row-colors.yaml`), and open-tab state into one merged view model (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/{hub,view_model}.rs`). Each row's colour follows the same severity ranking the tree view's `rowColorId` uses (red > yellow > green > muted from PR-check/session state), with an in-flight git operation or a user's own row-colour tag taking precedence over it. `q`/`Esc`/Ctrl-C quits and always restores the terminal. Actions, embedded terminals and tabs, and the rest of the tree view's VS Code-parity surface are later phases. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains actions (Phase 2)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585)): row navigation and multi-select (`↑`/`↓`/`j`/`k`, `space`), an action menu (`a`) filtered to the current selection and grouped in the tree view's own `0_open`/`1_pr`/`2_claude`/`3_copy`/`9_close` order (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/{actions,tree,popup}.rs`), the row-colour write path (`c`/`C`, wired onto Phase 1's already-working store), and five daemon-free parity commands ported from the VS Code companion: Open GitHub Repository, Copy Directory, Copy Pull Request URL(s) (built entirely from the already-live snapshot, needing no `gh` lookup unlike the companion's version), Move/Copy Claude Session Here (`src/sessions/relocate.rs` — pure `~/.claude/projects/` filesystem manipulation; the cursor row is always the relocation's *source*, the one adaptation a TUI needs since it has no "current window" to default a source to), and Focus/Open Worktree (wraps the daemon's `open` op, identical to `worktrees focus`). Close Worktree/Close Window are the two-phase check→confirm→execute flow, fanned out client-side across every marked target concurrently since the daemon's `close` op isn't batched on the wire. `WorktreesClient`'s new `open`/`close_check`/`close_execute` ops (and the existing `fetch_ahead_behind`) now share the crate's `call_service` request/origin-stamping helper instead of hand-rolling their own. `arboard` (clipboard writes) moves from an optional, macOS-only, `menu-bar`-feature-gated dependency to a plain unconditional one, since the UI needs it on both macOS and Linux in the default build.

- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains tabs and splits (Phase 4b)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585), [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §7): the terminal side becomes a vertical stack of tab groups instead of a single tab. `alt-t` opens a tab in the focused group, `alt-s` splits a new group below it, `alt-[`/`alt-]` cycle tabs, `alt-1`…`alt-9` select one, `alt-↑`/`alt-↓` move focus between groups, `alt-⇧↑`/`alt-⇧↓` move the active tab between them, and `alt-0` resets every group to an equal share. Each group carries a tab strip: clicking a tab activates it, middle-clicking closes it, and the wheel cycles. The boundary between two groups is a draggable splitter with a 3-row hit box, clamped so neither side goes below four rows and touching only the pair either side. The stack is a `Vec<PaneGroup>` with relative weights (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/{layout,panes}.rs`), not a recursive split tree — top-vs-bottom is what the issue asked for, and a nested variant stays additive. Layout maths and tab lifecycle are separate modules so the geometry is unit-tested with no PTY in sight. Opening a worktree that already has a live tab focuses it rather than duplicating, but an explicit split always makes a new group; an exited tab now stays visible with its exit status until closed instead of being silently replaced; and the tree's `here` cue is ref-counted, clearing only when the last tab on that worktree closes. `unicode-width` becomes a direct dependency: the tab strip's click targets are computed from the same display widths ratatui renders at, so a click cannot resolve to a different tab than the one under the pointer.
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` gains mouse support (Phase 4a — the selection contract)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585), [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §6): the mouse now drives the tree and the terminal grid (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/mouse.rs`). In the tree, a click moves the cursor, `^`-click toggles a mark, `⇧`-click marks the range, a drag range-marks, a double-click opens a shell tab, and the wheel scrolls the cursor; in the terminal, a drag selects (word on a double-click, line on a triple), the wheel scrolls scrollback (or sends arrow keys to an alt-screen child, or forwards to a child that asked for the mouse), and `alt-c` copies and clears the selection. The contract is structural, not a runtime filter: a `RegionMap` rebuilt from the drawn frame hit-tests each event to one region and clamps a drag to the region it began in, and **only the terminal grid has a selection model** — the tree, borders, tab strip and status bar cannot be selected because no code path would produce a string from them. A child that has itself requested mouse reporting (`claude` does) receives the encoded event instead of starting a selection, with `⌥` held taking it back; the host terminal's shift-drag escape hatch keeps working because nothing here inspects `Shift` on a drag. No new logging path touches PTY or selection content (the grep-guard test now covers `mouse.rs`).
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` hosts an embedded terminal tab (Phase 3)** ([#1585](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1585), [ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md)): `enter`/`alt-t` on a worktree row opens the user's shell in it, `alt-⇧t` opens `claude` there through `omni-dev claude-wrap` so the session reports *authoritative* state to the daemon ([ADR-0057](docs/adrs/adr-0057.md)), and the tree's `here` cue lights for that worktree — computed locally, with no daemon registration ([ADR-0072](docs/adrs/adr-0072.md) §1). Emulation is `alacritty_terminal` 0.26 in-process (`src/cli/worktrees/ui/terminal/`), one native PTY-reader thread per tab feeding the async loop over one shared channel; the loop coalesces PTY output into at most one redraw per 16 ms. Two spike-proven invariants are enforced and regression-tested against real children rather than mocks: `TERM`/`COLORTERM` are set explicitly per spawn (a parent without `TERM` otherwise spawns a child that silently degrades to dumb-terminal rendering — `vim` never enters the alt screen), and every `Event::PtyWrite` (the emulator's own reply to a child's cursor-position/device-attribute query) is written back to the child, without which a real interactive program hangs. A focused terminal takes every key verbatim (including `Esc`) except `Alt`-chords — `alt-e`/`alt-l` focus, `alt-w` close, `alt-c` copy (`arboard`, with an OSC 52 fallback for SSH/no-display sessions), `⇧PgUp`/`⇧PgDn` scrollback — so the chrome needs no tmux-style prefix; keys are encoded legacy-xterm and the kitty keyboard protocol is deliberately not advertised this phase. A child's own OSC 52 clipboard write is passed through, but the emulator stays at `Osc52::OnlyCopy` so no clipboard→child path exists. **PTY contents are never logged**, enforced by a grep-guard test rather than convention. `q` asks before quitting while a child is live. One tab and no tab strip this phase; tabs/splits and the mouse/selection contract are Phase 4. The event loop moved out of `mod.rs` into `src/cli/worktrees/ui/app.rs`.

- **`coverage diff` gains source comment markers to ignore or tolerate a *region* of a file** ([#1593](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1593)): the only fine-grained noise control until now was `diff.ignore-filename-regex` ([#1398](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1398)), which drops a **whole file** — but the noise it exists to silence is almost always one function. A region gated on a *runtime* CPU-feature check is compiled into the denominator on every run yet executed only on a host that has the instruction, so it flips whenever the baseline and head runs draw different runner CPUs; excluding its file hides far more real coverage than noise. Naming the region in config cannot work either — line numbers are invalidated by every edit above them, and function *extents* are absent from the lcov `FN:` records (start line and mangled symbol, nothing more). So the region is delimited in the source, with a comment that travels with the code: `// omni-dev: coverage tolerate reason="…"` … `// omni-dev: coverage end`, plus `ignore` and single-line `ignore-line`/`tolerate-line` forms. See [docs/coverage.md](docs/coverage.md).
  - **`ignore` removes the region's lines from both reports** before analysis — the scoped twin of `ignore-filename-regex`. **`tolerate` is the new capability**: the lines stay, so the reported percentage remains the true measured number, and only the *delta signals* are masked, by scoring each tolerated head line with its baseline hit status. The motivating case renders `Total: 92.79% ⚪ 0 pp` instead of `🔴 -0.01 pp`, with `92.79%` still the real figure.
  - **Each revision's own source is scanned** — head from the working tree, base from the base revision's blob — so **no line number is ever stored**, and a region that moves, grows, or disappears between base and head is handled by construction (a region that merely moved is still reported as one region, not one per revision). Only paths present in that side's report are read, bounding the cost by the report rather than the repository. An explicit `--head-ref`, or a repository with no working tree, reads that revision's tree instead of the checkout.
  - **Matching is a plain substring**, so any comment syntax works and nothing parses the host language — which also means a marker inside a string literal is matched. `reason="…"` is **mandatory**, and a malformed marker — missing or empty reason, unterminated quote, nested region, stray `end`, or a region left open at end-of-file — is a **hard error naming `path:line`**, never a silent no-op: an unterminated region must never widen to EOF, silencing an unbounded amount of code nobody looked at.
  - **Silencing is never invisible.** Every applied region is listed in a collapsed markdown note (path, kind, span as observed at head, revision, reason) and in a `markers` array in the YAML/JSON views. Displayed percentages are always the real measured values; `after_effective` / `total_after_effective` appear only where masking changed them.
  - Added lines inside a `tolerate` region **stay** in the patch-coverage denominator — new code should still be tested even if it flaps later — while lines inside an `ignore` region leave it. Without a baseline, `ignore` still applies and `tolerate` is inert. File-level exclusion wins: markers in an excluded file are never read. No new CLI flags.

### Changed
- **MSRV raised from 1.80.0 to 1.88.0**: required by `ratatui` 0.30 (added for `worktrees ui` above, chosen because it resolves to the same `crossterm` 0.29 already pinned here — `ratatui` 0.29 would have pulled in a second, non-overlapping `crossterm` 0.28 instead). `clippy.toml`'s `msrv` is synced to match, which also surfaced newer idioms clippy previously held back (`Option::is_none_or`, `usize::midpoint`, `iter::repeat_n`, `&raw mut`) across a handful of unrelated files.

### Fixed
- **`omni-dev worktrees ui` could hang on quit instead of exiting** ([#1605](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1605)): `tty::Pty`'s `Drop` sends the direct child `SIGHUP` and then blocks in `child.wait()` with no deadline, so a shell that ignores the signal — or one still waiting on a foreground job — hung it forever. Moving the join onto a blocking task kept that off the event loop but did not bound it: dropping the `tokio` runtime waits for blocking tasks, so the UI could restore the terminal, hand back the prompt, and then never exit. The child's whole **process group** is now reaped before the join (`setsid()` in the PTY spawn makes the child a group leader, so its own children go too), escalating from `SIGHUP` to `SIGKILL` after a 250ms grace — a well-behaved child still exits cleanly and keeps its `drain_on_exit` output. Needs no `unsafe`: `nix` was already a unix-gated dependency. Note this bounds the *wait*, which is the quit hang; a separate and rarer stall in the PTY reader thread's own join can still wedge a parallel `cargo test` run, so `--test-threads=1` remains the local workaround (tracked on #1605).
- **The `coverage diff` headline no longer paints a red arrow on a move every other section of the same comment hides** ([#1591](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1591), [#1592](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1592)): coverage is two independent instrumented runs, and [#973](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/973) deliberately suppresses their run-to-run variance in the per-file sections — delta-table rows need `|Δ| >= 0.05` pp and an untouched file needs a net move of 10 covered lines — but the headline total had **no** equivalent gate, so every flip those sections hid still accumulated there and was painted 🔴. Two independent defects, both visible on a docs-only PR in `rust-works/succinctly` whose total moved -0.0088 pp because one CPU-conditional dispatch function flipped between two runner CPUs: the direction emoji was derived from the **unrounded** delta while the **rounded** value was printed beside it (so any move in `(-0.005, 0)` rendered as `🔴 0 pp` — an alarm colour next to a number saying nothing moved), and the headline lacked the per-file tolerance entirely. `arrow` now rounds internally, which fixes the pairing at all three call sites at once — including the "unchanged files also moved" table, which is gated on *covered lines* rather than percentage points and so was never shielded by the `EPS` row filter the delta table has. The headline additionally treats a sub-0.05 pp total move as neutral while still printing the real number, and annotates itself `_(not attributable to this diff)_` when the total moved but no per-file row and no notable-unchanged entry accounts for it. The reported percentage is unchanged and still the real measured value.

## [0.42.0] - 2026-09-04

### Added
- **Google Drive gains real write capability: `drive create`, `drive upload`, and `drive edit`, bounded by folder-scoped write permissions** ([#1574](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1574), [ADR-0071](docs/adrs/adr-0071.md)): the Drive integration could read ([ADR-0069](docs/adrs/adr-0069.md)) and mutate *metadata* ([ADR-0070](docs/adrs/adr-0070.md) — `rename`/`move`), but never a file's content. The three new verbs close that gap, and land alongside a second, **local** safety gate so an OAuth grant is no longer the only thing standing between a mistyped command and a user's whole Drive. Purely additive: `read`/`search`/`dedupe`/`rename`/`move` behave exactly as before. See [docs/drive.md](docs/drive.md).
  - **A pure, default-deny folder-permission engine** (`src/drive/write_gate.rs`): `resolve` decides whether an operation is permitted against a target's **ancestor folder chain** and a configured rule set — closest ancestor wins, deny beats allow at equal depth, and a write with no matching rule is **denied**. Zero `DriveClient`/network dependency, mirroring `visibility.rs`'s pure/testable contract; named `write_gate` rather than `permission(s)` to avoid confusion with the unrelated `permissions_api.rs` (Google's own sharing/ACL wrapper). Its impure sibling `src/drive/folder_ancestry.rs` walks the chain via repeated `files.get` calls, short-circuiting the moment some depth decides the verdict — cutting a deep hierarchy with an explicit rule from up to 100 calls down to as few as necessary. Any fetch failure, or a chain exceeding the defensive depth cap, is a hard `Err` — **never** a silently truncated `Ok` chain, the same invariant ADR-0070 §3 established for `permissions.list` failures.
  - **A per-account `write_permissions.rules` block in `settings.json`** (`DriveAccountSettings`), following the same `#[serde(default)]` round-trip convention every other settings field uses. Per-account rather than top-level, since a folder id is only meaningful inside the one Drive it was minted in.
  - **`DriveScope` becomes `DriveGrantedScopes`, a combinable capability set.** `--write`/`--write-file`/`--write-full` are independent, additive opt-ins (Google simply grants the union), not one of two tiers, so the two mutually-exclusive variants become a four-flag set. `drive auth login` gains **`--write-file`** (`drive.file` — create/upload, and editing files omni-dev itself created) and **`--write-full`** (the unrestricted `drive` scope — editing arbitrary pre-existing content) alongside the existing `--write`. The one behaviour change is `load_credentials_with`'s stale-scope fallback, which now degrades to an all-false set instead of `ReadOnly` — strictly more conservative, and display-only either way, since scope never gates a real API call client-side.
  - **`omni-dev drive permissions {show, lookup-folder, check}`** — read-only, zero-mutation-risk diagnostics for the gate. `show` prints the active account's configured rules straight from `settings.json` (no network call, mirroring `drive account list`); `lookup-folder` searches folders by name and resolves each hit's full root-to-leaf path, for pasting an id into config; `check` evaluates **the real gate** — the exact `folder_ancestry` + `write_gate::resolve` functions the mutating verbs call — against a real target, so it cannot drift from actual enforcement. A file target's chain starts at its *current* parent(s), combined with `combine_across_parents` (deny wins) for a legacy multi-parent file.
  - **`omni-dev drive create --name --parent [--folder|--mime-type] [--dry-run]`** makes a new file or folder, with the gate consulted **before** any `files.create` call is attempted; `--dry-run` and a real run share the same gate classification by construction. **`drive upload <LOCAL_PATH> --parent <FOLDER_ID>`** creates a file from local content through a hand-assembled `multipart/related` body (Drive's upload endpoint rejects the `multipart/form-data` that `reqwest::multipart` produces), with Drive's 5 MB simple-upload cap checked by a `stat` before the file is ever read — so an oversized upload is refused identically under `--dry-run`. **`drive edit <FILE_ID> --content <PATH|->`** replaces an existing file's content; it is the structurally distinct one, since its gate chain starts at the target's *current* parent(s) rather than a caller-given `--parent`. A Google-native document (Docs/Sheets/Slides) is refused client-side before the gate even runs — a raw media `PATCH` has no meaningful content to replace for one — and `--content -` bounds stdin at the cap + 1 byte, so an unbounded pipe can never be buffered past the limit before being refused.
  - **A blocked attempt is still logged, and is queryable.** `DriveMutationOutcome` gains `resolved_folder_id`/`decided_by_folder_id`/`decided_by_depth`, so a folder-gate refusal is inspectable via `omni-dev log` through the existing `kind: "drivemutation"` record rather than a new record kind (see [docs/log.md](docs/log.md)). A 403 from Drive now names the *right* flag via a generalized `append_write_scope_hint` (`--write-file`/`--write-full`), instead of hardcoding the old rename/move-only `--write` hint.
  - **The gate cannot be bypassed by construction.** `FilesApi::create`/`upload`/`edit_content` are restricted to `pub(in crate::drive)`, so a future direct caller (a new CLI command, an MCP tool) that skips `write_gate::resolve` **fails to compile** rather than silently skipping both the gate and the request-log write. Separately, a `Content-Type` containing CR/LF is rejected before it reaches the hand-assembled multipart body — unlike a real HTTP header (which `reqwest`'s own `header()` already rejects those bytes for), that inner `Content-Type` line is spliced in by hand and could otherwise inject an extra boundary/part into the request Drive receives.
- **`claude-wrap` rewrites the terminal tab title with a colour-coded model identity** ([#1445](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1445), [ADR-0057](docs/adrs/adr-0057.md) amendment): concurrent Claude sessions in separate VS Code terminal tabs were visually indistinguishable — Claude asserts an OSC title sequence carrying only its own version string, and there is no other way to tell tabs apart: the terminal API only lets the *creating* extension set a tab's `iconPath`/`color`, only at creation time, with no way to change either afterward (anthropics/claude-code#56925, closed not-planned by Anthropic itself). `omni-dev claude-wrap` now rewrites that sequence in flight, in the `FromClaude` direction only, prepending a colour-circle-emoji-and-family prefix — 🟠 Fable, 🟡 Opus, 🟢 Sonnet, 🔵 Haiku, or ⚪ Claude for anything else — classified from the session's model id via a new `ModelRegistry::get_model_family` (`src/claude/model_config.rs`), which normalizes Bedrock/region-prefixed and versioned identifiers the same way every other registry lookup does. It updates live on a mid-session `/model` switch: `StreamTracker::absorb_identity` no longer latches `model` after its first sighting the way `session_id`/`cwd` still do. Measured against a real `claude` binary, the title is asserted once, at startup, well before the model can be known — not "continuously," as first assumed — so the pump also races `watch::Receiver::changed()` against the read and proactively re-emits a corrected title (`TitleRewriter::resync`) the moment the model becomes known, rather than waiting on a reassertion that may never come; that same testing also surfaced a pre-existing defect where the tee's newline-based line framing could corrupt the `system`/`init` line when Claude's raw terminal-init bytes land in the same `read()` (now recovered by skipping to the line's first `{` before parsing). The rewrite is a deliberate, narrow exception to ADR-0057's fail-open/forward-verbatim guarantees — the pump gains a dependency on a value the parser produced (over a `tokio::sync::watch` channel, read via non-blocking `borrow()`/`changed()`, never an `.await`) and mutates one tightly-matched byte span — but stays fail-open the same way the rest of the wrapper is: a small state machine recognizes exactly OSC `0`/`2` sequences terminated by `BEL` or the two-byte ST form, bounded to 8 KiB of buffering across any number of `read()` calls, and forwards anything else (a different OSC code, malformed input, non-UTF-8, an unterminated sequence, or a title asserted before any model is known) byte-for-byte unchanged. Set `OMNI_DEV_CLAUDE_WRAP_NO_TITLE_REWRITE=1` to disable the rewrite outright, if it ever misrenders in a given terminal or font. No new trust boundary: the model id already crossed the daemon's existing `0600` socket before this change. See [docs/sessions-service.md](docs/sessions-service.md).

### Changed
- **`async` removed from functions that never `.await`** (`src/atlassian`, `src/browser`, `src/cli`, `src/coverage`, `src/daemon`, `src/mcp`): a `clippy::unused_async` sweep across six modules. No behaviour change — callers that already `.await`ed these are unaffected, and the futures they no longer allocate were empty.

### Fixed
- **`coverage diff` no longer misattributes huge line ranges as new on refactor-shaped diffs** ([#1580](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1580)): `DiffModel::between` (`src/coverage/diff.rs`) built its `git2::DiffOptions` with only `context_lines(3)` set, leaving libgit2's default Myers algorithm active — which misaligns on files with long runs of near-duplicate lines (e.g. hand-expanded match arms), spuriously reporting nearly an entire file as "new" when a diff really just deletes one repetitive block. Adding `.patience(true)` and `.indent_heuristic(true)` fixes it; `patience` is libgit2's analogue of git's `--diff-algorithm=histogram`, confirmed correct against the real reproduction commit.
- **The Claude session feed no longer loses a session's identity when terminal control bytes share a `read()` with its JSON** ([#1445](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1445)): `claude-wrap`'s `tee_chunk` frames lines on `\n`, which has no notion of terminal control sequences — Claude's raw terminal-init bytes (title-set, mouse tracking, alt-screen; none newline-terminated on their own) can land in the same `read()` as the following `stream-json` line and get accumulated onto its front before the first `\n`, corrupting the parse. It was lost most consequentially for the `system`/`init` line, the one carrying `session_id`, `cwd`, and `model`. Since every real line here is a JSON object, the parser now skips to the first `{` — recovering the line without needing to understand escape-sequence structure at all. Surfaced while testing the title rewrite above against a real `claude` binary.

### CI/CD
- **Dependency bumps**: `taiki-e/install-action` 2.85.13 → 2.86.5 ([#1571](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/pull/1571)), and the `rust-minor-patch` cargo group ([#1572](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/pull/1572)) — `rmcp` 3.1.2 → 3.1.4, `similar` 3.1.2 → 3.2.0, and `mail-parser` 0.11.6 → 0.11.7. Manifest + lockfile bumps; no source or behaviour changes.

## [0.41.0] - 2026-08-20

### Added
- **Gmail integration: typed API client, OAuth2 login, and CLI/MCP surfaces (Phase 1)** ([#1465](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1465)): omni-dev can now search, read, and label Gmail messages and threads, both from the CLI and from an MCP-connected assistant — the first integration in this project authenticated via OAuth2 rather than a static API token. See [ADR-0063](docs/adrs/adr-0063.md) and [docs/gmail.md](docs/gmail.md).
  - **Authorization-code + PKCE login over a loopback listener** (`src/gmail/auth.rs`), modelled on Snowflake's external-browser SSO shape (bind the callback port first, open the browser, capture the redirect with a raw socket) but extended with what a static-token/SSO flow doesn't need: a PKCE `code_verifier`/`code_challenge` pair, a `state` nonce, and an explicit `error=` branch on the callback. `omni-dev gmail auth login [--modify]` is interactive-only — there is deliberately no MCP tool for it.
  - **Refresh token on disk, access token in memory only** — the refresh token persists to `~/.omni-dev/settings.json` through the same `Settings::upsert_env_vars_in` path every other backend's `auth login` uses (profile-aware for free); the access token is held only for one process's lifetime behind a `GmailSession`, refreshed proactively (60s before expiry) and reactively (retried once on an HTTP 401), with a single-flight guard so concurrent callers can't stampede the token endpoint. No new persistence machinery — a deliberate choice recorded in ADR-0063, not a shortcut.
  - **Bring-your-own Google Cloud OAuth2 client**: `GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`/`GMAIL_CLIENT_SECRET` are supplied by the user, never shipped by omni-dev — Gmail read scopes are Google *restricted scopes*, and distributing a verified app that requests them requires an annual CASA security assessment disproportionate to an open-source tool. A documented consequence: an OAuth client left in Google's "Testing" publishing status (the default) issues refresh tokens that expire after 7 days; hitting Google's `invalid_grant` names this as the likely cause rather than surfacing the bare OAuth error string.
  - **`omni-dev gmail {search, read, thread, label list/add/remove, auth login/logout/status}`** (`src/cli/gmail/`), structured like `src/cli/datadog.rs`: the client is resolved once before dispatch, every leaf takes `&GmailClient`. `search` returns Gmail's bare `{id, threadId}` list hits by default — the quota-safe choice — with `--enrich` opting into hydrating each hit with From/Subject/Date/snippet via one extra `messages.get` request per hit, fan-out bounded by `--concurrency` (default 4). `read`'s `--detail minimal|metadata|full|raw` controls how much of a message is fetched, named `--detail` rather than `--format` since `-o/--output` already owns that word for this project's rendering axis (per [ADR-0046](docs/adrs/adr-0046.md)). `label remove` is guarded per [ADR-0027](docs/adrs/adr-0027.md) (confirm by default, `--force`, `--dry-run`); `label add` is unconditional, matching the existing Confluence label add/remove asymmetry. `read --out-file` is the only leaf that writes to disk, per [ADR-0046](docs/adrs/adr-0046.md).
  - **Five `gmail_*` MCP tools** (`src/mcp/gmail_tools.rs`): `gmail_auth_status` (presence flags only, never calls the API), `gmail_search` (ids-only by default, same `enrich`/`concurrency` params as the CLI), `gmail_message_read` (with an `output_file` param mirroring `jira_read`'s off-context-write pattern), `gmail_thread_read` (always truncation-guarded — a thread's messages with attachments is the single highest payload-size risk on the surface), and `gmail_label_list`. A mutating `gmail_label_modify` tool is deferred to a fast-follow issue.
  - `src/gmail/{messages,threads,labels}_api.rs`: cursor-paginated façades over the Gmail v1 REST API, following the same contract as `src/datadog/logs_api.rs` — auto-pagination terminates only on an absent `nextPageToken`, never a short page, since a filtered Gmail list can return zero results on a page while still signalling more exist. The MIME payload stays `serde_json::Value` rather than being modelled, the same escape hatch `Dashboard.widgets` uses. A `history_api.rs` façade for Phase 2's incremental sync was deliberately not added in this issue — no Phase 1 consumer needs it yet.
  - `src/cli/atlassian/confirm.rs` promoted to `src/cli/confirm.rs` (no behavior change) since `gmail label remove` is a second, non-Atlassian consumer of the same destructive-confirm guard.
- **`omni-dev gmail sync`: a durable, incrementally-updated local mail archive (Phase 2)** ([#1467](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1467)): a new subcommand maintains `.eml` files plus a `manifest.jsonl` index for a mailbox, so the archive survives account loss and is analysable with ordinary tools (`grep`, `mutt`, a mail indexer) without omni-dev. See [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/adr-0064.md) and [docs/gmail.md](docs/gmail.md#sync).
  - **Presence-on-disk, not the `historyId` watermark, is the correctness mechanism.** A first run (or `--full`) lists the whole mailbox and fetches whatever's missing on disk (`messages/<year>/<month>/<day>/<id>.eml`, sharded by the message's `internal_date` — an earlier id-prefix scheme was measured against 5,824 real ids and found not to distribute at all, since Gmail ids are time-ordered hex); a `history.list` 404 on a stale watermark — Google guarantees history for roughly a week, not the ~30 days the parent issue assumed — falls back to that exact same listing pass rather than a gap or a blind re-download. An interrupted or killed run needs no special resume logic: the next run simply recomputes what's missing.
  - **`.eml` is immutable; `manifest.jsonl` is the only mutable state.** Gmail labels aren't part of the RFC 2822 body, so a `labelsAdded`/`labelsRemoved` history event updates only the manifest record — never rewrites or re-fetches the message. A corrupt manifest is a hard error (it's the sole record of a message's label state); a corrupt or missing `state.json`, by contrast, is fully disposable and just triggers the same full-listing fallback as a 404.
  - **Threading headers and attachment metadata in the manifest.** `to`/`in_reply_to`/`references` (alongside the existing `subject`/`from`/`rfc822_msgid`) let a conversation be reconstructed from the manifest alone, and `attachment_count`/`attachment_filenames` (including RFC 2231 percent-encoded filenames) make attachments discoverable without re-parsing every `.eml` — measured at 24% of a real corpus. All parsed from the already-decoded raw bytes; no second fetch. Metadata only — attachments stay inline regardless of whether they're also extracted to separate files (see [#1487](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1487) below).
  - **A regression test for the Facebook harvester's data-loss bug** (`src/browser/harvest/facebook.rs`'s `preload_prior`/`open_sink`, which silently truncates an existing archive when resume state is missing): `gmail sync` is structurally incapable of it — presence-on-disk is checked before every fetch decision, and nothing ever opens an existing `.eml` or the manifest in a mode that discards prior content first.
  - **A proactive token-bucket rate limiter** (`src/utils/rate_limit.rs`, generic — no Gmail-specific knowledge) paces `messages.get` calls against Gmail's documented 250-quota-units/second budget, composed with a `--concurrency`-bounded fan-out (default 20, clamped to `1..=50`) so a multi-thousand-message sync is throttled proactively rather than reactively backing off on 429s.
  - **`retry_429` (`src/utils/http.rs`) now also retries Gmail's real quota-exhaustion signal** — HTTP 403 with `reason: rateLimitExceeded`/`userRateLimitExceeded`, which Gmail sends instead of a literal 429. The new `retry_if` driver takes a caller-supplied retryability predicate and reconstructs the response from its buffered parts on give-up, so Atlassian and Datadog's call sites (still only retrying literal 429s) needed zero changes.
  - Per-message failures accumulate into a `SyncReport { actions, errors }` (`src/cli/gmail/sync/report.rs`, modelled on `src/cli/ai/claude/history/sync.rs`) rather than aborting the run — one unfetchable message never discards an hour of otherwise-successful work. The watermark only advances when a run recorded zero errors.
  - **Report output ends with an aggregate summary** ([#1488](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1488)): a dry run over a large label used to print one line per action with no total — 5,824 lines and never "5,824". `SyncReport::summary()` tallies `actions` by variant (`fetched`/`would_fetch`/`labels_updated`/`deleted`/`undeleted`/`would_delete`/`would_undelete`) plus the error count, recomputed on demand rather than accumulated inline so it can never drift from the underlying `actions`/`errors`. Table/text output gets a trailing line (e.g. `5,794 fetched, 30 deleted, 0 errors`); `-o json`/`-o yaml`/`-o yamls`/`-o jsonl` gain an explicit `summary` field alongside `actions`/`errors`, via a borrowing `SyncReportOutput` wrapper (mirroring `SyncOutput` in `src/cli/ai/claude/history.rs`) rather than a stored field on `SyncReport` itself.
  - `src/gmail/history_api.rs` (`users.history.list`, recovered and adapted from its Phase 1 YAGNI removal) and `src/gmail/profile_api.rs` (`users.getProfile`, extracted out of `gmail auth status`) back the new sync engine; `decode_raw_message` moved from `gmail read` into a shared `src/gmail/raw_message.rs`, which also gains `extract_headers` for pulling `Subject`/`From`/`Message-Id` out of an already-decoded raw message without a second network round-trip.
  - No MCP equivalent — a bulk, potentially long-running filesystem operation is a poor fit for a synchronous MCP tool call.
- **`gmail sync --extract-attachments`: write attachment MIME parts to disk** ([#1487](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1487)): an opt-in flag that additionally writes each fetched message's `Content-Disposition: attachment` parts to `messages/<year>/<month>/<day>/<id>/attachments/<filename>`, a sibling directory of the message's `.eml`. Off by default; the `.eml` stays the lossless source of truth either way. See [ADR-0065](docs/adrs/adr-0065.md) and [docs/gmail.md](docs/gmail.md#sync).
  - New `src/gmail/attachments.rs` pulls in the `mail-parser` crate for real multipart/MIME parsing (boundary parsing, Content-Transfer-Encoding decoding, RFC 2231 continuation parameters) — isolated to this one opt-in path; the existing `attachment_count`/`attachment_filenames` manifest fields keep coming from `raw_message.rs`'s cheap heuristic scan unchanged, regardless of the flag.
  - Filenames are sanitised against path traversal (reusing `crate::utils::path::attachment_filename`) and de-duplicated within a message with a `-N` suffix on collision.
  - Because `sync` only fetches messages missing on disk, turning the flag on does not retroactively extract attachments for already-archived messages, even under `--full` — delete the affected `.eml` files (or the archive) and re-run `--full --extract-attachments` to force re-extraction.
- **Self-verifying Gmail auth setup: `auth import`, a prompt fallback, and real scope validation** ([#1498](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1498)): end-to-end setup against a real account surfaced two silent failure modes and one avoidable manual step, all fixed without any change to the OAuth flow itself.
  - **`omni-dev gmail auth import [PATH]`** (`src/gmail/import.rs`) reads the `client_secret.json` Google Cloud Console already hands out and saves its client id/secret straight to `~/.omni-dev/settings.json` — the secret never has to transit a shell, an env var, or an agent's context. `PATH` is optional: discovery tries `$GMAIL_CLIENT_SECRET_FILE`, then `~/.config/gws/client_secret.json`, then the most-recently-modified `~/Downloads/client_secret_*.apps.googleusercontent.com.json` (the console's default download name). A "Web application" client (`web` key) is rejected with a message naming the "Desktop app" requirement instead of surfacing as a baffling `redirect_uri_mismatch` later at login.
  - **`gmail auth login` now prompts for a missing client id/secret instead of hard-erroring.** It first falls back to `settings.json` (closing a gap where it read only the raw process environment, unlike every other Gmail credential path); only when both are still absent does it prompt — the id echoes normally, the secret does not (`crossterm` raw-mode input, printing nothing per keystroke, no new dependency).
  - **A login that Google grants no Gmail scope for is now a rejected login, not a silent success.** `GmailScope::from_granted` returns `Option<GmailScope>` instead of defaulting an unrecognised grant to `ReadOnly`; `auth login` fails before persisting anything when the token response's `scope` carries no `gmail.*` scope (including when the field is absent entirely), naming the scopes Google actually granted. Previously, leaving Gmail's own consent-screen checkbox unticked — a real, repeatable failure since Gmail is a Google *restricted* scope with its own tickbox — stored an unusable refresh token and reported success, surfacing only later as a misattributed `403 insufficientPermissions`.
  - `gcloud`-assisted end-to-end setup (`gmail auth setup`) is deferred to a follow-up — it would be this project's first shell-out to a third-party CLI beyond `gh`/`claude` and is flagged as possibly ADR-worthy on its own.
- **Multiple Gmail accounts, selected independently of `--profile`** ([#1500](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1500)): `omni-dev gmail` supported exactly one mailbox at a time — re-running `auth login` overwrote the current refresh token, and using a second `--profile` to get a second mailbox meant dragging every other credentialed backend (Atlassian, Datadog, the Claude API key) along for the ride. See [ADR-0066](docs/adrs/adr-0066.md) and [docs/gmail.md#multiple-accounts](docs/gmail.md#multiple-accounts).
  - **A new `gmail` block in `settings.json`** (`GmailSettings`/`GmailAccountSettings`, `src/utils/settings.rs`), a sibling of `env`/`profiles`/`mcp` rather than nested under a profile: `default_account` plus a `accounts` map of named credential bundles. The two-level path walkers backing `Settings::upsert_env_vars_in`/`remove_env_vars_in` generalized into `ensure_object_at`/`object_at_mut` (arbitrary path-segment depth) so the new `upsert_gmail_account`/`remove_gmail_account`/`set_gmail_default_account` helpers reuse the same hardened write path (0700/0600, unknown-field-preserving) with no new file-handling code.
  - **`src/gmail/account.rs`**: `resolve_account` (reads — `search`/`read`/`thread`/`label`/`sync`/`auth status`/`auth logout`/the MCP tools) and `resolve_account_for_write` (creates — `auth login`/`auth import`/`account import-legacy`) implement the resolution precedence: a literal `GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`/`_REFRESH_TOKEN` in the process environment bypasses account resolution outright; an explicit `--account`/`OMNI_DEV_GMAIL_ACCOUNT` selects that account (an unknown name is a hard error for reads, but never blocks a write, since login/import is how an account is created in the first place); no explicit account falls back to the configured default, then the sole configured account, then a hard error if ambiguous; zero named accounts falls through **unchanged** to today's exact legacy resolution — the zero-migration guarantee, pinned by a dedicated regression test.
  - **An `--account NAME` flag** (`src/cli/gmail.rs`, propagated to `OMNI_DEV_GMAIL_ACCOUNT`) selects the account for a Gmail command, mirroring `--profile`/`--instance` in shape but *not* in scope: it's `global = true` on `GmailCommand` rather than the root `Cli`, so it's inherited by every `gmail` subcommand without becoming a CLI-wide reservation of the word "account" — Snowflake's pre-existing, unrelated `snowflake query/cancel/disconnect --account` needed no change. The `no_subcommand_arg_shadows_a_global_arg_id` structural test (`src/cli.rs`) was generalized to accumulate globals per-subtree (not just at the root) and to also check long-flag-string collisions, not only arg-id ones — the latter is clap's actual hard constraint ("Long option names must be unique for each argument"), which an id-only check can miss when a subcommand's field is renamed but its flag string isn't.
  - **`omni-dev gmail account {list, set-default, import-legacy}`** (`src/cli/gmail/account/`): `list` renders name/email/scope/default from `settings.json` only (no network, no secrets); `set-default` validates against configured accounts before writing; `import-legacy [--name NAME=default] [--remove-legacy]` migrates today's legacy credentials into a named account, non-destructively unless `--remove-legacy` is passed (`--name`, not `--account` — `--account` is inherited by every `gmail` subcommand including this one, and clap rejects two args on one command sharing a long flag string). `gmail auth status --all` reports one status block per configured account, backfilling each account's cached (display-only) `email_address` on success; it degenerates to today's single line with zero named accounts.
  - **A one-time shadowing notice, not a silent auto-migration**: the moment a first named account is created while legacy credentials still exist, a no-`--account` invocation stops seeing them. `gmail auth login --account`/`gmail account import-legacy` print a stderr note at exactly that transition, pointing at `import-legacy` (for any other legacy account) or `auth logout` (to clean up).
  - **A new `gmail_account_list` MCP tool**, and an optional `account` parameter on all five existing Gmail tools (`gmail_auth_status`, `gmail_search`, `gmail_message_read`, `gmail_thread_read`, `gmail_label_list`) — an MCP client calls `gmail_account_list` to discover valid names before passing one elsewhere.
- **`gmail auth login` can target a specific Chrome profile, matched to the account's email** ([#1505](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1505)): with several named accounts (#1500) each usually signed into a different local Chrome profile, login always opened whatever profile the OS default browser happened to be on, so switching Google identities on the consent screen was a manual, easy-to-get-wrong step that could land the refresh token on the wrong mailbox entirely. See [ADR-0067](docs/adrs/adr-0067.md) and [docs/gmail.md#browser-profile-targeting](docs/gmail.md#browser-profile-targeting).
  - **Manual escape hatch — `browser_command`** (`GmailAccountSettings`, `src/utils/settings.rs`): an explicit, `{url}`-templated launch command, per account. Its POSIX-style tokenizer moved out of `crate::snowflake` into a shared `crate::utils::browser_command::split_browser_command` on this, its second consumer.
  - **Automatic resolution — `chrome_profile_from_email`**, opt-in per account: a new `src/gmail/chrome_profile.rs` reads Chrome's own `Local State` file to find which local profile is signed into the account's `email_address`, never guessing (zero or multiple matches both fall back) and always failing open to the OS default browser on any resolution failure — never a hard login failure.
  - `email_address` may now be set by hand ahead of the first login (previously write-only, backfilled only by `gmail auth status`); an explicit value is never overwritten by that backfill.
  - Zero migration: an account that sets neither field, or a legacy (non-named) account, keeps opening the OS default browser exactly as before.
- **`omni-dev gmail sync-all`: concurrent multi-account sync, driven by a new `.omni-dev/gmail-sync.yaml`** ([#1504](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1504)): syncing several mailboxes previously meant an external wrapper script looping `gmail sync --account ...` serially, even though each account has its own independent Gmail API quota and nothing in the engine required serialization. See [ADR-0068](docs/adrs/adr-0068.md) and [docs/gmail.md#sync-all-accounts](docs/gmail.md#sync-all-accounts).
  - **A new recognised config file, `.omni-dev/gmail-sync.yaml`** (`src/cli/gmail/sync_all.rs`), located via the existing filename-generic Chain A discovery (`local/` shadow, `--context-dir`/`OMNI_DEV_CONFIG_DIR`, walk-up to repo root): a top-level `concurrency` plus an `accounts` list of `{account, output_dir, query?, extract_attachments?}` entries. `account` is a lookup key into `~/.omni-dev/settings.json`'s `gmail.accounts` map ([ADR-0066](docs/adrs/adr-0066.md)), never a second credential store. Unlike `scopes.yaml`/`commit-rules.yaml`, this file is loaded strictly: a missing file, an empty `accounts` list, malformed YAML, or an unrecognised account name are all hard errors before any client is built, batching every unknown account name into one message rather than failing one task at a time.
  - **A shared fetch-request semaphore, layered underneath each account's existing local `--concurrency`.** `SyncOptions` (`src/cli/gmail/sync/engine.rs`) gains an optional `shared_pool: Option<Arc<Semaphore>>`, acquired immediately before each message fetch in both fan-out paths; `None` for every existing single-account caller, so `gmail sync`'s behavior and test suite are unchanged. `sync-all` builds one semaphore sized by `--concurrency` (CLI flag → `gmail-sync.yaml`'s `concurrency` → the same default as `gmail sync --concurrency`) and shares it across every account's `tokio::spawn`ed sync, so no single account can claim the whole shared budget. Each account still runs its own independent `TokenBucket` against its own Gmail quota — the shared semaphore is a purely local resource cap, orthogonal to quota pacing.
  - **Per-account credential resolution never touches the `--account` env var.** Each spawned task resolves its client via `helpers::create_client_for(Some(&account))` (`src/cli/gmail/helpers.rs`, shipped for #1500) rather than `GmailCommand::execute`'s `std::env::set_var` path, which would race across concurrent tasks. The global `--account`/`OMNI_DEV_GMAIL_ACCOUNT` selector is rejected outright when combined with `sync-all` — it targets one mailbox, while `sync-all` always targets the whole `gmail-sync.yaml` list.
  - **Live per-account progress bars on one shared `indicatif::MultiProgress`.** Every account gets its own listing-spinner + fetch-bar pair (`SyncProgressBars::new_in`, `src/cli/gmail/sync/progress.rs`), all registered on a single `MultiProgress` instance so they render and advance concurrently instead of fighting each other over the same terminal — gated by the same interactive-terminal check (`-o table`, not `--quiet`, a tty `stderr`) `gmail sync` itself uses. Independent of the bars, one summary line still prints per account as soon as that account finishes (not only once every account is done), routed through `MultiProgress::suspend` so it doesn't land mid-redraw, followed by a trailing combined total once every bar has finished; `-o json`/`-o yaml`/`-o yamls`/`-o jsonl` emit one structured record per account plus a `combined_summary`. The process exits non-zero if any account either failed outright or reported per-message errors — one account's failure never suppresses or aborts another's in-progress sync.
- **`omni-dev gmail extract-attachments`: retroactive attachment extraction for already-archived mail, without re-fetching** ([#1510](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1510)): `gmail sync --extract-attachments` (#1487) only ever extracted attachments for messages fetched *during that run* — because presence-on-disk is the archive's idempotence mechanism ([ADR-0064](docs/adrs/adr-0064.md)), turning the flag on had no effect on mail already archived, even under `--full`. [ADR-0065](docs/adrs/adr-0065.md) named this gap explicitly and, until now, the only fix was deleting the affected `.eml` files and re-running `--full --extract-attachments` — wasteful, since extraction is a pure, local, network-free parse of bytes already sitting on disk. See [docs/gmail.md#extract-attachments](docs/gmail.md#extract-attachments).
  - **Purely local and offline**: reads the manifest and `.eml` files already under `--archive-dir` and never resolves a `GmailClient` — dispatched in `GmailCommand::execute`/`GmailSubcommands` (`src/cli/gmail.rs`) alongside `sync-all`'s no-client special case, before credential resolution. `--account` is rejected outright, the same as `sync-all`, since the command has no client/credential concept to scope. Deliberately **not** named `--output-dir` like `sync`'s own flag: this command's primary interaction with the directory is reading an existing archive, so the flag names that instead, even though it also writes new `attachments/` subdirectories into it.
  - **Reuses `gmail sync --extract-attachments`'s extraction path verbatim, against on-disk bytes instead of freshly-fetched ones** (`src/cli/gmail/extract_attachments/engine.rs`): walks every non-deleted manifest record, trusts `attachment_count > 0` as a fast-path filter (the same cheap heuristic scan `sync` always runs, regardless of whether `--extract-attachments` was ever passed), and skips any record whose `attachments/` directory already exists — the same presence-on-disk idempotence `sync` itself relies on, which is what makes the command safe to re-run any time to pick up whatever an earlier run missed. `sync/engine.rs`'s `manifest_path` helper was promoted to `pub(crate)`, and a new `write_attachments_atomically` helper (also shared with `sync`'s own `--extract-attachments` fetch path) publishes a message's whole attachment set via a hidden-directory-then-`rename` swap, so a mid-batch write failure can never leave the `attachments/` directory existing-but-partial — which would otherwise be skipped forever by the idempotence check above.
  - **Per-message failures accumulate into a report; they never abort the batch** (`ExtractAttachmentsReport`, `src/cli/gmail/extract_attachments/report.rs`, mirroring `SyncReport`'s compute-render-decide shape from [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/adr-0064.md) Decision 4): a missing or unreadable `.eml` is recorded as a per-message error and the run continues; a message whose real MIME parse disagrees with the heuristic and finds nothing (the rare case ADR-0065 documents) is a silent no-op, not an error.
  - **`--dry-run` parses every candidate for an accurate count** rather than echoing the heuristic's `attachment_count`, and writes nothing; `-o table|json|yaml|yamls|jsonl` output mirrors `sync`'s own report formats.
  - Two small additive `Manifest` API changes reused by the new engine: `ManifestRecord::internal_date_utc()` (mirrors `Message::internal_date_utc`, since a manifest record's `internal_date` is the same Gmail epoch-millisecond string) and `Manifest::records_not_deleted()` (the full-record counterpart of the existing id-only `ids_not_deleted`).
  - No MCP equivalent — same reasoning as `sync`/`sync-all`: a bulk filesystem operation is a poor fit for a synchronous MCP tool call.
- **`omni-dev gmail render`: turn an archived `.eml` back into readable Markdown, plus `gmail read -o markdown`** ([#1513](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1513)): `gmail sync` archives full-fidelity, byte-exact `.eml` files, but nothing in the codebase turned one back into something readable — `raw_message::extract_headers` never decodes RFC 2047 encoded-word subjects, `attachments.rs`'s real MIME parser only ever extracted attachment files, and `gmail read`'s renderers never printed the body at all. See [docs/gmail.md#render](docs/gmail.md#render).
  - **`src/gmail/render.rs::render_markdown(raw: &[u8]) -> String`**, a sibling of `src/gmail/attachments.rs` rather than an extension of `raw_message.rs`'s line-oriented scanner (the same "needs real MIME parsing" reasoning that module's own doc comment already draws for attachment bytes applies at least as strongly here, plus RFC 2047 decoding that scanner explicitly skips). Renders a header block (Subject/From/To/Cc/Date/Message-Id/In-Reply-To/References, RFC 2047-decoded via `mail-parser`'s own header accessors — no new decoding logic needed), the body (a genuine `text/plain` part preferred; falling back to `text/html` converted via the new `htmd` crate — chosen over a plain HTML-to-text conversion since the output format already is Markdown), and a bullet list of attachment filenames (listed via `attachments::extract_attachments`, never embedded — this is a readable rendering, not an export). Never fails: an unparseable message degrades to a short placeholder rather than erroring, so one bad file can't abort a batch caller.
  - **`omni-dev gmail render <PATH.eml>... [--out-dir DIR] [-o FORMAT]`** (`src/cli/gmail/render.rs`), dispatched in `GmailCommand::execute`/`GmailSubcommands` (`src/cli/gmail.rs`) alongside `sync-all`/`extract-attachments`'s no-client special cases — purely local, no `GmailClient`, no network, `--account` rejected outright. Deliberately takes bare file paths rather than a `Manifest`/`--archive-dir` + message id, so it works on any `.eml` file with no dependency on this tool having synced the mailbox at all (`find archive/messages -name '*.eml' | xargs omni-dev gmail render` already works with no extra flag). Default `-o table` prints each input's rendered Markdown straight to stdout — multiple inputs separated by a `---` thematic break, and no trailing summary line in the all-success case, so `gmail render *.eml > combined.md` stays clean; `--out-dir DIR` instead writes one `.md` file per input (named after its stem) and prints a `Saved to:` line per file; `-o json`/`-o yaml`/`-o yamls`/`-o jsonl` emit one structured record per input via the shared blanket `Vec<T>` `JsonlSerialize` impl, more useful here than one line for the whole batch given callers may render hundreds of archived messages at once. A per-file read/write failure is recorded against that file rather than aborting the batch, though the command still exits non-zero if any file failed.
  - **`gmail read -o markdown`** (`ReadOutputFormat`, `src/cli/gmail/read.rs`) reuses `render_markdown` against a live message, always fetching `format=raw` regardless of `--detail` (rendering needs the full raw MIME structure). Kept as a `read`-local enum extending the shared `OutputFormat` with a `Markdown` variant, rather than adding `Markdown` to the crate-wide `OutputFormat` every other CLI surface's `-o` renders generically via `Serialize` — a `Markdown` variant only makes sense for a MIME message.
  - No MCP equivalent for `gmail render` — same reasoning as `extract-attachments`/`sync`: a bulk, potentially many-file filesystem operation is a poor fit for a synchronous MCP tool call.
- **`--fold-quotes`: collapse deeply-nested quoted reply chains in `gmail render`/`gmail read -o markdown`** ([#1514](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1514)): #1513 rendered a message's `>`-quoted reply history verbatim, deliberately deferring the fact that real synced threads can accumulate 10-20+ levels of quoting by the last reply, drowning the genuinely new content in repeated older quotes.
  - **`fold_quoted_lines`/`quote_depth`** (`src/gmail/render.rs`) count each line's leading `>` depth (tolerant of `>>>`, `> > >`, and mixed spacing) and replace any run nested deeper than one level with a one-line `*(N quoted lines omitted)*` marker, bridging a lone unprefixed blank line between two foldable runs so it doesn't fragment one nested quote block into several tiny markers. A single line-based pass handles both native plain-text quoting and `htmd`'s HTML-`<blockquote>` conversion uniformly, since both already converge on the same repeated-`>`-per-line shape by the time a body reaches this function — no separate HTML-specific path or locale-dependent `"On ... wrote:"` pattern matching needed.
  - **`--fold-quotes`** on both `gmail render` and `gmail read -o markdown` (the latter only relevant combined with `-o markdown`, the reverse of `--detail`'s asymmetry). Off by default: verbatim rendering stays fully information-preserving, and the full text is one re-render away without the flag.
  - No MCP equivalent — `render_markdown` has no MCP surface, same scope as `gmail render` itself.
- **`gmail render --archive-dir PATH --all`: whole-archive batch rendering, `Manifest`-aware** ([#1515](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1515)): #1513 deliberately took only bare `.eml` paths, leaving "render everything I've synced" to a caller-assembled glob (`find archive/messages -name '*.eml' | xargs omni-dev gmail render`) with no way to skip soft-deleted messages or ones already rendered by a prior run — an Open Question explicitly deferred from that issue.
  - **`--archive-dir PATH --all`** (`src/cli/gmail/render.rs`), mutually exclusive with positional `PATH` arguments via a `clap::ArgGroup` (mirroring `CancelCommand`'s `"cancel-target"` group in `src/cli/snowflake.rs` rather than the heavier `sync`/`sync-all` separate-subcommand split, since this only changes how the `.eml` path list is produced, not the execution engine). `--all` is required alongside `--archive-dir` rather than implied by it, reserving room for a future non-`--all` selector.
  - **`resolve_archive_paths`** reads `manifest.jsonl` (via the existing `manifest_path`/`Manifest::load`, already promoted `pub(crate)` for `extract-attachments`) and resolves every non-deleted record to its `.eml` path, feeding the result straight into the *unchanged* `render_paths`/`render_one`/`render_report_text` pipeline — so both input modes get identical output formatting, per-file error resilience, and exit-code behavior for free.
  - **Silently skips a message already rendered into `--out-dir`** by a prior run (checked via the same `md_filename` the write path uses, so the two can't drift), mirroring `gmail extract-attachments`'s presence-on-disk idempotence for `attachments/` dirs — so a large archive can be re-rendered incrementally. No skip is applied without `--out-dir`, since stdout has nothing durable to check a re-run against.
  - No MCP equivalent — same reasoning as bare-path `render` itself.
- **Google Drive settings schema: a `drive` block in `settings.json`** ([#1522](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1522)): the foundation layer of the Google Drive integration ([#1520](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1520), [ADR-0069](docs/adrs/adr-0069.md)) — the named-account store every later child issue's OAuth/client/CLI/MCP code compiles against. No OAuth, no HTTP, no CLI/MCP surface yet.
  - **`DriveSettings`/`DriveAccountSettings`** (`src/utils/settings.rs`), a field-for-field mirror of Gmail's `GmailSettings`/`GmailAccountSettings` and a *sibling* of the `gmail` block, never nested under it — a Drive account and a Gmail account are independent credential axes even when the underlying human is the same person. `chrome_profile_from_email`/`browser_command` are inherited by field per [ADR-0067](docs/adrs/adr-0067.md); an absent `drive` block deserializes to an empty account map, preserving the same backward-compatibility guarantee every other settings block has.
  - **`Settings::{upsert_drive_account, remove_drive_account, set_drive_default_account}`**: ~15-line copies of the Gmail helpers passing `["drive", "accounts", name]` to the already-generalized `ensure_object_at`/`object_at_mut` path walkers — the same hardened (0700/0600, unknown-field-preserving) write path, no new file-handling code.
- **Google Drive OAuth2 login, API client, and named-account resolution** ([#1523](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1523)): the largest piece of the Google Drive integration ([#1520](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1520), [ADR-0069](docs/adrs/adr-0069.md)) — `src/drive/{auth,client,error,account}.rs`, a near-verbatim port of the working, fully-tested `src/gmail/{auth,client,error,account}.rs` (plus a duplicated `src/drive/chrome_profile.rs`), adjusted for Drive's read-only-only design. No CLI (`src/cli/drive.rs`) or MCP (`src/mcp/drive_tools.rs`) surface yet — separate child issues.
  - **The same authorization-code + PKCE loopback flow, single-flight-refresh session, and named-account precedence as Gmail**, minus what Drive doesn't need: a single `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.readonly` scope constant rather than Gmail's `GmailScope` readonly/modify enum, and `ResolvedAccount::{Named, Unconfigured}` rather than Gmail's `{Named, Legacy}` — Drive has no pre-#1500-style single-account credential convention to preserve, so there is nothing "legacy" to fall back to; an unconfigured account with no literal-env bypass fails loudly with a `drive auth login` remedy instead of attempting a network call.
  - **`DriveClient`'s base host is `https://www.googleapis.com` with `/drive/v3/...` path prefixes**, not a dedicated subdomain like Gmail's `gmail.googleapis.com` — recorded in [ADR-0069](docs/adrs/adr-0069.md) so this issue didn't have to re-derive it. Quota-exhaustion retry is confirmed narrower than Gmail's: 403 with reason exactly `userRateLimitExceeded` (not also the bare `rateLimitExceeded` Gmail accepts, which isn't confirmed for Drive against Google's own error-handling docs); plain 429s are already covered by the shared `retry_if` driver regardless.
