- **`omni-dev drive` CLI surface: `auth`, `account`, `search`, `read`** ([#1524](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1524)): the user-facing half of the Google Drive integration ([#1520](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1520)), built on the settings schema ([#1522](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1522)) and OAuth2/client layer ([#1523](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1523)) above. `drive auth login/logout/status[--all]` and `drive account list/set-default` mirror Gmail's shape minus what Drive doesn't need (no `auth import`, no `account import-legacy` — there is no pre-existing single-account credential state to migrate). `drive search QUERY [--limit]` passes the query verbatim to `files.list`'s `q` parameter (Drive's own query language, not Gmail's search syntax) and sends `supportsAllDrives`/`includeItemsFromAllDrives` unconditionally. `drive read ID [--content] [--out-file] [--export-mime-type]` returns metadata by default; `--content` downloads regular files via `alt=media` (capped client-side at 500 MB) or exports Google-native files (Docs → `text/markdown`, Sheets → `text/csv`, Slides → `text/plain` by default; every other Google-native type requires an explicit `--export-mime-type`), and refuses folders/shortcuts with an actionable error rather than returning nothing. MCP tools (`drive_*`) shipped separately in [#1525](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1525) below. See [docs/drive.md](docs/drive.md) and [docs/drive-quickstart.md](docs/drive-quickstart.md).
- **`omni-dev drive rename` and the opt-in `drive.metadata` write scope** ([#1557](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1557)): the Drive integration's first write capability, reversing ADR-0069 §2's "read-only by design." `drive auth login --write` additively requests `drive.metadata` alongside the existing `drive.readonly` (Google's narrowest write scope — `files.update` on `name`/`parents` only, no file-content access), via a new `DriveScope` enum (`ReadOnly` default, `Metadata`) mirroring Gmail's `GmailScope` exactly. `drive rename FILE_ID NEW_NAME [--dry-run] [-o ...]` calls the new `FilesApi::rename`/`DriveClient::patch_json` (`files.update`, PATCH); a 403 `insufficientPermissions` gets an actionable hint pointing at `--write` rather than a bare API error. Renaming only ever touches `name`, never `parents`, so it can never change a file's visibility and needs no safety gate — unlike the security-sensitive `move` this lays the groundwork for (tracked by the same issue, not yet shipped). Every rename attempt is written to the request log as a new `kind: "drivemutation"` record (`RecordKind::DriveMutation`), from inside the rename engine itself rather than the CLI layer, so the "every rename must be logged" invariant holds for any future caller too (e.g. an eventual MCP tool). See [docs/drive.md](docs/drive.md) and [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).
- **`omni-dev drive move`: security-gated batch moves** ([#1557](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1557), [ADR-0070](docs/adrs/adr-0070.md)): unlike rename, moving a file can change who can see it — Drive resolves effective visibility from direct permissions on a file plus permissions inherited from its parent folder chain, and a move changes that chain. `src/drive/visibility.rs` derives the direct/inherited split by subtraction (`permissions.list`'s `permissionDetails[].inherited` flag is only populated for Shared Drive items, not My Drive files): `before − current_parent = direct_on_file`, then `direct_on_file ∪ dest = after`, with `added`/`removed` the set differences — a documented, tested "safe failure direction" limitation for shadowed direct+inherited grants (can only over-block, never under-block). `drive move ID... --to FOLDER_ID` refuses any move that would change visibility **by default**; three independent opt-ins (`--allow-visibility-increase`/`--allow-visibility-decrease`/`--allow-drive-boundary-crossing`) proceed anyway, and a bulk call skips only the unsafe files rather than failing the whole batch. No interactive confirmation anywhere — `--dry-run` plus the opt-in flags are the entire gate, since an MCP caller can't answer a TTY prompt. `src/drive/file_move.rs`'s two-phase `plan()`/`execute()` (adapted from `worktree_push`'s shape) validates the destination is a folder once up front, short-circuits an already-in-folder no-op before any `permissions.list` call, and caches destination/shared-parent permission fetches across a batch. Every outcome — moved, blocked, already-in-folder, or failed — is logged via the same `kind: "drivemutation"` record rename uses, carrying `added_principals`/`removed_principals` for a blocked move: the single most security-relevant event, made auditable even though no API call was made. See [docs/drive.md](docs/drive.md#move).
- **Drive MCP tool surface: `drive_auth_status`/`drive_search`/`drive_file_read`/`drive_account_list`** ([#1525](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1525)): a read-only tool surface mirroring `gmail_tools.rs` one-for-one, wired into `OmniDevServer::new()` via a new `drive_tool_router`. `drive_search` has no `enrich`/`concurrency` split like `gmail_search` — `files.list` already returns full metadata per hit, so there's no separate hydration step. `drive_file_read` supports a `metadata`/`content` format switch, refuses to return binary content inline, and writes content bytes straight to disk when `output_file` is set. Every tool but `drive_account_list` takes the same optional per-call `account` parameter Gmail's tools take. A fifth tool, `drive_dedupe`, was added alongside the CLI `drive dedupe` command ([#1556](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1556) below) to keep the MCP surface at parity. See [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md#drive-5-tools).
- **Content-hash duplicate detection and download verification for Drive** ([#1556](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1556)): `files.list`/`files.get` already return `md5Checksum`/`sha1Checksum`/`sha256Checksum` for binary-content files, but `DriveFile` never requested or modeled them. `omni-dev drive dedupe <query>` (and the matching `drive_dedupe` MCP tool) reuses `FilesApi::search_all` and groups hits by `md5Checksum` (the broadest-coverage checksum field) via a `BTreeMap` for deterministic output — no new fetch mechanism needed, skipping unhashed files (folders, Google-native docs) and singleton groups. `drive read --content --verify` (and a matching `verify` param on `drive_file_read`) recomputes SHA-256 locally via the already-present `sha2` crate and checks it against Drive's reported `sha256Checksum`, rejecting Google-native files up front since Drive never returns a checksum for exported content. See [docs/drive.md](docs/drive.md#duplicate-detection).

- **Deterministic, no-AI scope fixes for `git commit message lint` and `staged`** ([#1564](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1564)): `lint` could only flag an invalid `(scope)`, not fix it — most commonly Dependabot's `chore(deps): ...` failing because a project's scope taxonomy uses the ecosystem-default name instead (e.g. `cargo` for Rust). The fix is fully mechanical: `resolve_scope`/`refine_message_scope` already existed and were proven (twiddle's AI flow force-corrects scopes with them after the fact) — this exposes them as a standalone, no-AI suggestion.
  - **`lint --suggest`/`--fix`**: a new `suggest_scope_fix` (`src/git/lint.rs`) resolves a scope from a commit's changed files for any `unknown-scope`/`missing-scope` issue, either replacing an existing wrong scope (via `refine_message_scope`) or inserting one when missing, preserving breaking-change `!` placement. `--suggest` populates the report's previously always-`None` `suggestion` field; `--fix` applies the resolved messages directly via the same `AmendmentHandler` path `git commit message amend` uses — no confirmation prompt, since only a resolvable scope issue is ever touched. `--allow-pushed` mirrors `amend`/`twiddle`'s flag for `--fix`. `git_lint_commits` gets a matching `suggest` MCP param, read-only like the CLI flag; `--fix`'s mutation stays CLI-only, mirroring how `check --twiddle`'s interactive apply has no MCP equivalent.
  - **`staged --no-ai`**: skips the AI backend entirely and prints a `type(scope): ` skeleton derived from `git diff --cached --name-status` — scope via the same deterministic `resolve_scope` resolution `lint --suggest`/`--fix` use, type via `detect_commit_type_from_message` (promoted from a private `CommitAnalysis` method to a `pub(crate)` free function) with an empty message, falling straight through to the file-pattern heuristics. It's a prefix only, never a synthesized description — unlike scope, type is best-effort/advisory here, not validated against an enumerable list. Always prints and never commits, regardless of `--print-only`, since a bare skeleton is never a complete message. `git_staged_commit` gets a matching `no_ai` MCP param.

### Changed
- **Retired the AI-powered commit-check workflow in favour of the deterministic lint** ([#1472](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1472)): across the last 40 runs, `.github/workflows/commit-check.yml` (`action-works/omni-dev-commit-check@v1` + `claude-haiku-4-5`) caught zero commit-message defects and produced one false positive — an Anthropic billing outage that blocked a PR without saying anything about its commits — while re-judging messages an AI had already written against the same guidelines it was checking them with. `.github/workflows/commit-lint.yml` replaces it with the same reusable action's `command: lint` mode (`action-works/omni-dev-commit-check@v1.4`), which runs the deterministic `omni-dev git commit message lint --strict` added in [#1474](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1474): no API key, no model, no network call beyond fetching the `omni-dev` binary itself. The semantic guideline that "the message must accurately reflect the code change" cannot be checked without a model and is no longer enforced in CI; it is accepted as authoring-time-only until [#1471](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1471) lands a `Stop` hook.
- **Pinned the coverage-check action to `v1.1`** instead of the floating `v1` tag, so the reference stops moving silently whenever `v1` is later retargeted at a new minor release.
- **`gmail auth status --all`/`drive auth status --all` fetch each configured account's status concurrently instead of serially** ([#1535](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1535)): N accounts previously meant N serialized network round-trips; `run_auth_status_for` now returns its formatted report as a `String` (instead of printing live) so results can be collected and joined after running concurrently. Drive's `--all` loop faithfully mirrored a pre-existing Gmail inefficiency, so both got identical treatment.

### Fixed
- **AI calls no longer die when a gateway rejects the `output_config` structured-output field** ([#1561](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1561)): the Anthropic and Bedrock backends request a schema-constrained response via the Messages API `output_config.format` on models the registry flags `supports_structured_output` (#1119). That gate is per-*model*, so it cannot express the per-*endpoint* case a proxy creates: pointing `ANTHROPIC_BEDROCK_BASE_URL` at an internal gateway that validates request bodies strictly and does not pass `output_config` through returned `HTTP 400 {"message":"output_config.format: Extra inputs are not permitted"}`, which is correctly permanent under #1333's classifier — so the run died on the first AI call, before any commit was analysed. `ClaudeClient::send_with_optional_schema` (the single choke point for every schema call site) now recognises that specific rejection — a `400`/`422` naming the field, via the new `ClaudeError::is_structured_output_rejection` — warns with both cures, and re-issues the call on the YAML response path, latching structured output off for the rest of the run so the rejected shape is attempted at most once however many commits are in flight. The retry also strips `JSON_SCHEMA_RESPONSE_OVERRIDE` from the system prompt, so the model is not left instructed to satisfy a schema the request no longer carries. This is a deliberately narrow exception to "a permanent 4xx fails loudly": the retry is a *different* request, not the same one re-issued. A new `OMNI_DEV_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_DISABLE=true` switches the field off up front — for every backend and model, and settable from a `settings.json` `env` bundle or a CI `env:` block — skipping even that one round-trip; `supports_structured_output: false` remains the per-model form. See [docs/troubleshooting.md](docs/troubleshooting.md#gateway-rejects-output_config).
- **`gmail sync --extract-attachments`: case-only filename collisions silently overwrote an attachment** ([#1495](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1495)): `dedupe_filename` (`src/gmail/attachments.rs`) compared sanitised filenames case-*sensitively*, so two attachments in one message whose names differed only by case (e.g. `Report.PDF` and `report.pdf`) both kept their original name instead of the second getting a `-N` suffix. On the case-insensitive filesystems macOS (APFS) and Windows (NTFS) default to, that meant the second file silently overwrote the first on disk — no error, and the manifest's `attachment_count` still reported both. The dedup set is now keyed by the lowercased name (the written filename still keeps its original casing). Found during review of [#1487](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1487)/PR #1493 ([ADR-0065](docs/adrs/adr-0065.md)).
- **`gmail sync`/`sync-all`: a message that vanishes between `history.list` and `messages.get` no longer wedges the account** ([#1509](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1509)): if Gmail permanently deletes a just-listed message before its own fetch runs, `messages.get` returns `404 (reason: notFound)` — previously indistinguishable from any other per-message failure, so it landed in `report.errors` and, per [ADR-0064](docs/adrs/adr-0064.md), withheld the `history_id` watermark save. Since the message can never be fetched, every subsequent run re-discovered and re-failed on the exact same stale history event, wedging that account until Gmail's ~1-week history retention aged the event out on its own. A new `is_message_not_found` classifier (`src/cli/gmail/sync/engine.rs`, mirroring `is_history_not_found`'s reason-checked shape but not its fail-open default) now recognises this specific case and records it as a new `SyncAction::Vanished` instead of a `SyncError` — visible in the report and its summary line (`N vanished`), but no longer blocking the watermark or failing `sync`'s/`sync-all`'s exit code.
- **`Settings::upsert_gmail_account`/`upsert_drive_account` could corrupt the whole settings file when writing a non-string field** (caught during [#1523](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1523)'s review, PR #1528): `vars: &[(&str, &str)]` could only ever write a JSON *string*, so persisting a non-string field like `chrome_profile_from_email: bool` through either helper would write the string `"true"` into a `bool` field — since `Settings` deserializes as one unit, the next `Settings::load()` would hard-fail parsing the *entire* settings file, silently dropping every env var/profile/account until hand-fixed. No call site currently triggered it (both helpers only ever wrote the four string credential fields), but the trap is closed proactively: both now take `&[(&str, serde_json::Value)]`, every existing call site (Gmail's `record_account_email`/`named_account_vars`/`gmail auth import`/tests) was updated to match, and a new regression test per helper proves a `bool` value round-trips through `Settings::load()` intact.
- **`Settings::remove_gmail_account`/`remove_drive_account` left a dangling `default_account` pointer behind** ([#1529](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1529), surfaced during review of PR #1528): removing the account named by `<section>.default_account` deleted `<section>.accounts.<name>` but left the pointer itself untouched, so Gmail's resolver (`src/gmail/account.rs::resolve_default_account`) would hard-error on the next invocation that omitted `--account` instead of falling back to the sole-remaining-account rule — and Drive would inherit the identical gap once [#1523](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1523) wires a removal command onto `remove_drive_account`, with no legacy-credential fallback to soften it (ADR-0069 §3). Both helpers now clear `<section>.default_account` in the same write when it names the account being removed.
- **`GmailSession`/`DriveSession` token refresh could panic on an out-of-range `expires_in`** ([#1531](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1531)): both `refresh_locked` methods computed `Utc::now() + TimeDelta::seconds(response.expires_in)` straight from the OAuth token endpoint's unvalidated `expires_in: i64` field, and both `TimeDelta::seconds` and `DateTime<Utc>`'s `Add<TimeDelta>` panic (not error) on overflow. A misbehaving token endpoint — or a malicious/misconfigured `GMAIL_API_URL`/`DRIVE_API_URL` override — returning an out-of-range `expires_in` could crash the process on the hot path of every credentialed Gmail/Drive call (`access_token()`/`force_refresh()`), not just login. `expires_in` is now clamped to `0..=MAX_EXPIRES_IN_SECONDS` (a new const, 100 years — comfortably inside what the arithmetic can represent and far beyond any real token lifetime) before the computation, identically in `src/gmail/auth.rs` and `src/drive/auth.rs`. Surfaced during review of [#1523](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1523); confirmed byte-for-byte identical in the already-shipped Gmail code, so it affected both integrations equally.
- **`FilesApi::paginate` left `next_page_token`/`incomplete_search` pointing past files it had discarded via truncation** ([#1536](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1536), surfaced during review of [#1524](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1524)): when the accumulated total exceeded the caller's `cap`, `paginate` (`src/drive/files_api.rs`) truncated `result.files` but left `next_page_token`/`incomplete_search` set to whatever the last fetched, untruncated page reported — a cursor pointing past files that were fetched but then discarded, not past what was actually returned. No current consumer reads these fields (`drive search`'s CLI rendering and the MCP `drive_tools.rs::run_search` path only read `.files`), but a future paginating consumer (the `drive_search` MCP tool tracked in [#1525](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1525)) would silently skip files between the truncation point and where the token actually resumes. Both fields are now cleared whenever truncation actually discards fetched files (`result.files.len() > cap`); the exact-cap case, where nothing is discarded, still preserves an accurate cursor. Gmail's structurally-identical `MessagesApi::paginate` has the same latent bug and is intentionally left as-is — #1536 was scoped to Drive only.
- **`sanitize_for_terminal` didn't strip bidirectional-control code points, leaving a Trojan-Source-style visual-spoofing gap** ([#1552](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1552), surfaced during review of [#1537](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1537)): `sanitize_for_terminal` (`src/cli/format.rs`) strips Unicode category `Cc` control characters (C0/C1/DEL) before a server- or writer-supplied string reaches the terminal, which fully closes ANSI/CSI escape injection since every CSI sequence starts with ESC. It left the bidirectional-control code points untouched, so a Drive filename or Gmail subject/sender containing U+202E (RIGHT-TO-LEFT OVERRIDE) survived unchanged — in a terminal that honors bidi controls, this can visually reorder rendered text (e.g. a filename crafted so `evil.exe` displays as `exe.live`), the same technique covered by CVE-2021-42574. `sanitize_for_terminal` now also strips LRE/RLE/PDF/LRO/RLO (U+202A–U+202E), LRI/RLI/FSI/PDI (U+2066–U+2069), and LRM/RLM (U+200E/U+200F) — deliberately only these code points, not the whole Unicode `Cf` category, since the rest of `Cf` (e.g. ZWJ in emoji sequences, joiners in Arabic/Indic text) is legitimate and stripping it would mangle otherwise-valid international text.
- **`.omni-dev/scopes.yaml` coverage gaps: three unscoped subsystems and eleven missing module facades** ([#1468](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1468)): `worktrees`, `transcript` and `coverage` had no scope of their own, and eleven scopes (nine flagged by the issue plus `gmail` and `drive`, both of whose facade gaps landed after the issue was filed) were missing their `src/<mod>.rs` facade pattern — every one of those 51 files silently resolved to the Rust ecosystem `lib` scope's `src/**` catch-all instead of erroring, since `resolve_scope` only reports "no match" when literally nothing matches. Fixed by adding the three missing scopes, the eleven facade patterns, and closing a pre-existing dead pattern (`resources`' `src/resources/**`, since only `src/resources.rs` exists); `omni-dev config scopes lint --project-only` (added in [#1475](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1475)) now reports zero violations against this repo's own tree. STYLE-0007 in [docs/STYLE_GUIDE.md](docs/STYLE_GUIDE.md) gains a fourth clause recording the tree-coverage rule, and `.omni-dev/commit-guidelines.md` documents the `lib`-for-shared-infrastructure convention in prose for the first time (#1469).
- **`DriveCommand`/`GmailCommand::execute` leaked their `--account` env var into any later `execute()` call in the same process** ([#1538](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1538)): both set `DRIVE_ACCOUNT_ENV`/`GMAIL_ACCOUNT_ENV` to propagate `--account` but never restored the prior value. Harmless for a one-shot CLI run, but a latent trap for any future in-process caller invoking `execute()` more than once (a daemon service, batch runner, or test harness). A new `ScopedEnvVar` RAII guard (`src/utils/env.rs`) now scopes both mutations to the call.
- **`git_staged_commit`'s deterministic and `print_only` paths leaked `println!` output onto MCP stdout, corrupting the JSON-RPC stream** ([#1567](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1567)): `run_staged_no_ai` (added in [#1564](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1564) above) and `run_staged_with_client`'s `print_only` branch both printed their message directly instead of only returning it, so an MCP client calling `git_staged_commit` with `no_ai`/`print_only` would see the leaked line interleaved with JSON-RPC frames. The printing moved into `StagedCommand::execute` (CLI-only), mirroring how `LintCommand`/`AmendCommand` already keep their deterministic cores stdout-silent. Two new `mcp_integration_test.rs` regression tests drive the real `omni-dev-mcp` binary through `git_staged_commit` (`no_ai` and `print_only`, the latter via a stubbed Ollama-compatible endpoint) and assert stdout stays pure JSON-RPC end-to-end — a leak is only observable at the real-process level, since the in-process `spawn_server` duplex transport never touches actual stdout.

## [0.40.0] - 2026-08-02

### Added
- **Deterministic, no-AI commit-message lint** ([#1474](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1474)): `check`'s deterministic pre-checks (`run_pre_validation_checks`) only ever recorded scope validity as a free-text *pass* for the AI prompt and silently dropped a *failure* — and its `SCOPE_RE` matched only the undocumented `type!(scope):` breaking-change form, never the documented `type(scope)!:` one, so 31 historical commits lost the check's scope-validity signal entirely ([#1473](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1473)). A new pure `lint_message(message, rules, valid_scopes) -> Vec<CommitIssue>` in `src/git/lint.rs` takes no git handle and does no I/O, so it's exhaustively unit-testable, and both `run_pre_validation_checks` and `refine_message_scope` now delegate to it and its shared `scope_comma_format_ok`/`scope_parts_all_valid` predicates instead of keeping a second, drifting implementation — fixing #1473 as a side effect of unifying on one corrected `parse_subject` parser (canonical `type(scope)!:` plus the legacy `type!(scope):` fallback, built from scratch rather than patched onto the old regex) rather than as a separate pass.
  - Rules cover every guideline section the old pre-checks never touched: type validity (the ten default types in `.omni-dev/commit-guidelines.md`'s `## Types` table), subject length (80 chars by default, overridable via a new optional `.omni-dev/commit-rules.yaml`), lowercase/no-trailing-period subject style, a blank line after the subject when a body is present, and forbidden footers (`Co-Authored-By` by default, e.g. AI attribution lines). Scope *requirement* is config-gated and off by default, matching existing practice rather than the guidelines' stricter text — an absent `commit-rules.yaml` falls back to `CommitRules::default()` for every field.
  - New **`omni-dev git commit message lint [COMMIT_RANGE]`** mirrors `check`'s CLI surface (`COMMIT_RANGE`, `--context-dir`, `--guidelines` accepted-but-unused for interchangeability, `-o/--output`, `--strict`, `--quiet`, `--show-passing`, `--verbose`) so it's a drop-in swap for `check` in scripts, minus every AI-pipeline-only flag (`--concurrency`, `--no-coherence`, `--twiddle`, the deprecated `--format`) since there's no AI client to configure at all. A new `--stdin` lints a single literal message directly — the shape a `commit-msg` git hook needs. It reuses `GitRepository::get_commits_in_range` as-is, so merge commits are already excluded with no new filtering logic, and deliberately diverges from `check` on one point: an empty range exits 0 with a clean empty report rather than `check`'s `exit(3)`, since a deterministic gate with nothing to lint isn't a failure. `run_lint` is the shared non-interactive core, mirroring `run_check`/`run_check_with_client`.
  - New **`git_lint_commits`** MCP tool mirrors `git_check_commits`' shape (`GitLintCommitsParams`, the same `format_check_payload`-style YAML wrapper) and delegates to the same `crate::cli::git::run_lint` the CLI uses, so the two surfaces share one implementation. Adds a `message` parameter (the `--stdin` equivalent, since an MCP caller has no stdin to pipe through) and needs no model/credential parameters at all — its tool description says so explicitly, so an agent can prefer it over `git_check_commits` when it just wants the free, exact signal.
  - A new regression corpus (`tests/lint_regression_corpus_test.rs`) embeds 399 real, non-merge commit subjects from a pinned range of this repo's own history (`a28f9598..466cf0fc`) as literal fixture data — a live `git2` walk at test time would fail under CI's default shallow, depth-1 `actions/checkout` and would drift as `main` moves — and asserts the flagged set is *exactly* the known exceptions, split by rule with a one-line justification each: `subject-length` (>80 chars, the dominant category and what the original issue's measurement predicted), one `blank-line-after-subject` (the issue's own headline example), a handful of `unknown-scope` hits from pre-existing `scopes.yaml` drift (out of scope for #1474; that repair is #1468), and one `format` violation from an external contributor's one-off PR that never followed conventional-commit form. This is what proves the rules encode existing practice rather than a new standard.
- **`omni-dev config scopes usage`** ([#1476](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1476)): a pure reporting command — never gates, always exits 0 — that tallies declared conventional-commit scopes across history against `.omni-dev/scopes.yaml` and reports which are **unknown** (declared in a commit, absent from `scopes.yaml`), **unused** (defined in `scopes.yaml`, never declared), and how many commits are **scope-less**. Answers exactly the questions a drifted `scopes.yaml` repair needs — previously only answerable by hand-rolled, subtly-inconsistent shell pipelines — and complements the structural coverage checks `omni-dev config scopes lint` already runs ([#1475](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1475)): `lint` asks whether every *file* is covered by some scope, `usage` asks whether every *declared* scope in history is a real one and every *defined* one is actually used.
  - New `tally_scope_usage` (`src/git/commit.rs`) is a pure function over already-extracted subject lines and scope definitions, so the bulk of the logic is git-free and unit-testable with string literals; multi-scope subjects like `feat(cli,claude): …` count once for each of `cli` and `claude`, matching `run_pre_validation_checks`'s own comma-splitting.
  - New `GitRepository::get_commits_from_head` (`src/git/repository.rs`) is the whole-history walk this command needs, since the existing `get_commits_in_range` only supports the `"HEAD"` and `"A..B"` forms; a shared `collect_walk` helper was factored out of both entry points rather than duplicating the merge-skipping/reversal logic.
  - New `load_project_scopes_only` (`discovery.rs`) loads `scopes.yaml` without the ecosystem-default merge, so "unused" only ever flags a scope a human actually wrote, never a synthesized `cargo`/`lib`/`core`/`test` default that `merge_ecosystem_scopes` injects at load time.
  - Reads each commit's raw first line rather than `git log --format=%s`, which folds a non-blank second line into the "subject" and had previously inflated a real measurement.
  - Lives under a new `ConfigSubcommands::Scopes` → `omni-dev config scopes usage [COMMIT_RANGE]`. `-n/--max-count` bounds the window to the newest N commits (the default is the *entire* history reachable from `HEAD`, since "ahead of main" — the default every other range-taking command uses — is the wrong default for a taxonomy report, and is mutually exclusive with an explicit `COMMIT_RANGE`); `--context-dir` follows the standard resolution chain; `--project-only` excludes ecosystem defaults from the known-scope set (so they're reported as unknown rather than silently accepted); `-o/--output text|json|yaml` reuses `data::check::OutputFormat`.
- **Claude model-family marker on worktree and repo rows** ([#1448](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1448)): the Worktrees tree's Claude session cues ([#1406](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1406)) already said *how many* sessions a worktree was running and in what state, but not *which model* — telling a Sonnet worktree from an Opus one meant switching to its terminal or editor tab. Worktree rows now carry a trailing `[hsof*]`-style marker — **h**aiku, **s**onnet, **o**pus, **f**able, `*` for anything else — naming every model family running there, and repository rows (previously description-less) show the union across their worktrees. Purely a client-side reclassification of the `model` id the sessions service already sent on every `list`/`subscribe` reply; no daemon or wire change was needed. Documented in [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
  - `editors/vscode/src/sessionCounts.ts`: `classifyModel` matches a raw model id to its family by a case-insensitive substring, so it survives a Bedrock/regional-prefixed id (e.g. `us.anthropic.claude-3-7-sonnet-...` still classifies `s`); an id matching none of the four — including a session whose model the daemon never learned — classifies `*`. `tallyModelsByWorktree` buckets families per worktree using the same inclusion rule as the existing state tally (`tallyByWorktree`): idle still counts, `ended` and unattributable sessions don't, so the marker's scope always matches the `!`/`⚙`/`◦` glyphs it sits beside.
  - `formatModelMarker` renders in a fixed `h`/`s`/`o`/`f`/`*` order regardless of discovery order, only the letters actually present (`[so]` for a Sonnet-and-Opus worktree, never a blank-padded `[hsof*]`), and is empty — not `[]` — for a worktree or repo running no sessions.
  - `unionModelFamilies` gives repo rows a rollup of every child worktree's families, so a repository running Sonnet in one worktree and Opus in another shows `[so]` even though no single worktree does.
  - `extension.ts` folds the new tally into the same per-tick pass that already drives the state glyphs — `setSessionTallies` is replaced by a combined `setSessionState(tallies, models)` — so a tick that changes both never fires the tree twice.
- **Per-row tree icon colours** ([#1428](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1428)): a new **Set Colour…** context-menu action in the VS Code **Worktrees** view tags an individual repository or worktree row with an icon colour, so a tree spanning a dozen repositories reads at a glance instead of being scanned — and so a theme that renders the default green illegibly has a way out. It acts on the whole selection (a repo row is exactly as taggable as a worktree row, so one gesture can tag every worktree of a repository), the colour picker's **Default (theme colour)** entry clears the selected rows again, and a new **omni-dev: Clear All Row Colours** command-palette entry drops every tag at once.
  - **The colour belongs to the row, not to what the row is doing.** `worktreeRowIcon`/`repoRowIcon` (`editors/vscode/src/icons.ts`) resolve the tag as a distinct step from the glyph, so a tagged row keeps its colour whichever icon it is showing — the tick for the worktree open in this window, the dot for one open elsewhere, or the plain branch glyph for one with no window — and the glyph itself never changes; a colour never costs the open-state distinction. Per-*state* colouring was deliberately rejected: in a tree where forty of forty-five rows are all "open in another window", a per-state colour would repaint nearly everything identically and differentiate nothing, and would be unstable for the ~10 s after a daemon restart before every window has re-registered, since a state-keyed colour would visibly follow that transient all-closed window where a row-keyed one does not.
  - **Precedence, first match wins:** the mid-rebase cue ([#1415](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1415), yellow, transient and actionable) → the row's tag → the row's default state colour. A worktree left mid-rebase therefore still shows yellow until it is resolved, and an untagged row renders exactly as it did before the feature existed. The PR-check and Claude-session badges keep their own severity-ranked colour regardless, so a tagged row's icon and badge can legitimately differ — they report different things.
  - **Colours live in VS Code user settings, not the daemon** — the opposite call to the show/hide-closed toggle ([#1301](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1301)) and the per-repo PR-poll flag ([#1376](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1376)), both of which the daemon holds and re-pushes on a snapshot because `context.globalState` is read-once with no cross-window change event. Settings do not have that problem: `onDidChangeConfiguration` fires in every open window on a user-scope change, so tags sync live across windows for free, survive a daemon restart, and ride Settings Sync to other machines — at the cost of a wire field and daemon changes a purely client-side presentation concern has no other reason to need.
  - Hand-editable as `omniDevWorktrees.rowColors`, a map keyed by `nodeId` — `repo:<repo root>` / `wt:<worktree path>`, not a bare path, since a main worktree's `path` *is* its repo's `root` and a path-keyed map could not tell the repo row from its own main worktree row — to a workbench colour id from the `charts.*`/`terminal.ansi*` families plus a muted grey. An unrecognised id leaves the row at its default rather than rendering an invisible icon; keys are paths, so deleting a worktree leaves its entry behind — **Clear All Row Colours** is the escape hatch. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Open GitHub Repository row action** ([#1442](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1442)): every GitHub-shaped action in the Worktrees view was pull-request shaped — **Open Pull Request…**, **Add to Merge Queue**, **Copy PR URL** — so reaching a repository's own page (Actions, Issues, the code browser, a stale `README`) meant leaving the view and typing the URL by hand or going by way of a PR's breadcrumb. A new **Open GitHub Repository** context-menu action opens `https://github.com/<owner>/<name>` in the OS default browser, built straight from the `github` identity the daemon's `tree` snapshot already carries.
  - `editors/vscode/src/github.ts`'s `repoWebUrl`/`repoWebUrlsForNodes` are the pure, `node --test`-ed builders; `repoCommands.ts::openGithubRepository` is the thin `vscode` adapter that resolves the selection via `selectionTargets` and opens each page.
  - It is the **cheapest** action in the view: the URL is a pure function of the snapshot, so unlike every other GitHub action here there is no `gh`, no subprocess, no network and no daemon round-trip at all — hence no progress notification and no confirmation, and it works against any daemon version.
  - Gated on **repository rows only** (`viewItem =~ /^repo\.github/`, the `repoContextValue` prefix) rather than the `/github/` gate the two Open Pull Request actions share, or Copy PR URL's `^(repo|worktree)` — a worktree's repository page is identical to its parent's, so offering it on both would double a menu entry for no new information. It sits in the `0_open` group beside **Open Worktree**, not with the pull-request actions, since it is an *open* action rather than a PR one.
  - A multi-selection still **de-duplicates by URL**: a worktree row caught up in the selection resolves to its parent repository, since VS Code evaluates a `when` clause against the clicked row only. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Copy PR URL row action** ([#1430](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1430)): the Worktrees tree could already *open* a selection's pull requests two ways and *copy* a selection's directories, but not copy its PR URLs — the missing half of gathering the links for the batch of worktrees in front of you and pasting them into a prompt, a stand-up note, or a `gh` one-liner. A new **Copy PR URL** context-menu action puts the selection's PR links on the clipboard as **one line per selected row**, in selection order, next to **Copy Directory**.
  - `editors/vscode/src/prClipboard.ts` is the pure, `node --test`-ed line-building model; `prCommands.ts::copyPullRequestUrls` is the thin adapter, fanning out over the selection, discovering each distinct scope via a `discoverScopes` helper factored out of `selectPullRequests`, and joining outcomes back onto rows by the newly-exposed `prScopeKey`.
  - The block is per **row**, not per discovered PR: a row with an open PR contributes its URL, a row with none contributes a `#`-commented placeholder naming it (`# No PR for <branch> in <absolute path>`), and a **failed** lookup gets its own distinct comment (`# PR lookup failed for …`) rather than an emptiness placeholder — so a transient `gh`/daemon failure can never be pasted as a settled "no PR", and a block can never silently shrink to whatever happened to have a PR. URLs **de-duplicate** across the whole block; placeholders never do, since they name different worktrees.
  - Gated like **Copy Directory** (`viewItem =~ /^(repo|worktree)/`), not like the two Open Pull Request actions' `/github/` gate — a row with no GitHub identity, or a detached worktree, has a truthful answer here (the placeholder). Nothing is confirmed and nothing bails: a clipboard write is not destructive.
  - A selection the daemon's PR polling has already resolved costs **no subprocess and no network**: a badged worktree answers from the snapshot, and repo scopes go through the daemon's shared, TTL-cached `open-prs` op. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **A passive "behind origin/main" indicator in the worktrees tree and CLI** ([#1457](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1457)): the tree view's `↑ahead ↓behind` sync counts only ever reflected a branch's divergence from **its own upstream** (`origin/<branch>`), never from the repository's **remote default branch** — so a feature branch fully in sync with its own upstream could sit dozens of commits behind `origin/main` with no visible signal at all. The only place that number was ever computed was inside **Rebase on main**'s confirm modal, reachable only by actually invoking a rebase (or `omni-dev worktrees rebase --dry-run`). A new `⇊N` glyph on the tree row (with its own tooltip line), and a matching `main-N` fragment in `omni-dev worktrees tree`, now surface that count at rest — no rebase, and no `git fetch`, required.
  - New `folder_main_behind` in the daemon (`src/daemon/services/worktrees.rs`), folded into the existing lazy **`ahead-behind`** op (#1306) as an independently-optional `main_behind` field alongside `ahead`/`behind`. It resolves the default branch the same **local-only, no-fetch** way `worktrees rebase`'s `--onto` default does (`RemoteInfo::detect_main_branch_local`), so the number always agrees with what a rebase would actually target, and reuses the same local-only `graph_ahead_behind` walk the rebase dry-run preview already does — the same staleness contract VS Code's own SCM ahead/behind arrows already live with.
  - Omitted whenever the branch's own upstream **is** already the resolved default branch (the common checked-out-`main`/`master` case), so it never duplicates the existing `↓` count — and, unlike `ahead`/`behind`, a worktree with **no upstream at all** can still report `main_behind`, since that is exactly the case this indicator exists for.
  - `src/cli/worktrees.rs`: `merge_ahead_behind` folds `main_behind` in independently of `ahead`/`behind`, and `sync_summary` appends a trailing `main-N` fragment — so `omni-dev worktrees tree` (table and `-o json`) carries the same signal as the tree view.
  - `editors/vscode/src/tree.ts`: `main_behind?: number` added to `TreeWorktreePayload`/`AheadBehind`, folded through `withAheadBehind`, and rendered by `syncCounts` as `⇊N` plus an explicit tooltip line naming the count and the **Rebase on main** action.
- **`git push --force-with-lease` for worktrees and repos** ([#1443](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1443)): Completes the batch-rebase workflow #1415 started. A rebase rewrites history, so every already-published branch it touches is diverged and a plain `git push` is rejected — the tree view displayed the divergence it had just created with no way to resolve it, leaving you to open a terminal in each worktree. See [ADR-0061](docs/adrs/adr-0061.md).
  - New two-phase **`push` op** on the `worktrees` daemon service, serialized by a `push_lock` and re-planning from scratch on execute like `close`/`merge-queue`/`rebase`. Its plan phase **contacts no remote at all**: classification is against the local `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>`, which is exactly what the lease is checked against, so the plan you confirm and the lease `git` enforces agree by construction — a fetch here would silently *renew* the lease.
  - New **Push (force-with-lease)** row action in the VS Code worktrees tree (`4_git` group), the only batch git action offered on **repo rows** as well as worktree rows — a selected repo expands to its worktrees client-side, with no extra round-trip. Always-modal confirm listing each branch with its classification, force pushes first and labelled, with the action button reading **Force Push** whenever the batch contains one.
  - New **`omni-dev worktrees push [<PATH>…] [--all] [--dry-run] [-y]`**, keeping its own local engine like `worktrees rebase` so a batch push works with no daemon running.
  - **Always `--force-with-lease --force-if-includes`, never `--force`.** A bare `--force-with-lease` leases against the local remote-tracking ref, so any background fetch — VS Code's own `git.autofetch` included — silently renews it and a teammate's unseen commit becomes overwritable. Per `git-push(1)`, `--force-if-includes` is *not* implied and is a documented no-op unless `--force-with-lease` is passed valueless. There is no force escape hatch anywhere: not an engine option, not a CLI flag, not a wire field. A refused lease is reported distinctly from every other rejection and names `git fetch` plus a rebase as the fix.
  - **The repository's remote default branch is never force-pushed**, whichever worktree has it checked out. The gate is the **branch name**, not `is_main`, deliberately inverting [ADR-0060](docs/adrs/adr-0060.md): a rebase rewrites only local history and is `git reflog`-recoverable, while a force-push publishes that rewrite to everyone. A *fast-forward* onto the default branch stays an ordinary push and is allowed. The guard lives in the engine, so it holds for the CLI, the daemon op, and any future caller.
  - A transient `pushing` tree cue mirroring `rebasing` (row spinner plus description), with **no durable half** — a push writes no on-disk state, so a completed push is instead observable as `upstream_sha` moving.
  - New `src/git/worktree_push.rs` engine, host-agnostic like `worktree_rebase.rs`; the primitives both share (selection, worktree enumeration, HEAD reads, the `git` subprocess seam) moved to `src/git/worktree_batch.rs` rather than being duplicated.
- **`omni-dev config scopes lint`** ([#1475](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1475)): `.omni-dev/scopes.yaml` is the canonical commit-scope taxonomy, and nothing checked it against the tree it claims to describe — it had already drifted here ([#1468](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1468): `src/transcript/**`, `src/coverage/**`, `src/worktrees.rs` have no scope) in a way the AI-based commit check structurally cannot detect, since that check validates messages *against* whatever taxonomy it's handed. The new command runs two zero-AI, zero-network structural assertions: every `file_patterns` entry matches at least one tracked file (no dead patterns), and every tracked file under `--root` (repeatable, default `src`) is matched by some scope (no unscoped files). Reuses the existing `globset`-based matchers (`resolve_scope`, `scope_matches_files`) rather than a second matcher, so it inherits their exact semantics — including that `src/foo/**` does not cover a sibling `src/foo.rs`, and that `!`-prefixed negative patterns are never reported dead.
  - **`--project-only`** (on by default; disable with `--no-project-only`) is the load-bearing flag: it excludes the ecosystem-injected default scopes `merge_ecosystem_scopes` adds (e.g. Rust's `lib` = `["src/lib.rs", "src/**"]`), which would otherwise make the "every file is scoped" check vacuously true on exactly the repos that need it most.
  - **`--allow <GLOB>`** (repeatable), unioned with a new optional `allow:` key in `scopes.yaml`, suppresses coverage violations for paths that legitimately belong to no scope. This repo's own `scopes.yaml` gains such a block for `src/utils/**`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/test_support.rs`, `src/bin/**`, and `src/templates/**`.
  - `-o/--output text|json|yaml`, reusing `data::check::OutputFormat`; non-zero exit on any violation. Run today, it fails against this repo's known [#1468](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1468) gaps — finding drift is this command's job, not fixing it.

### Changed
- **"Rebase on main" no longer shows a confirmation modal** ([#1458](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1458), [ADR-0062](docs/adrs/adr-0062.md)): the action used to pause between the daemon's two-phase check and execute with an always-shown modal listing every branch about to be rewritten and its real behind-count — shown even for a single row, per [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md) §1's "the modal is the only place you see the full set" rule as [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/adr-0059.md) applied it to rebase. Risk-gating rebase the way **Close Worktree** does does not hold up: `close`'s `risks` enumerate outcomes a reflog genuinely cannot get back, but every branch a rebase touches is a named ref, and starting a rebase is itself what writes the pre-rebase `HEAD` into the reflog **unconditionally**, before any dialog could appear — a clean fast-forward and a worktree left mid-rebase with a conflict are equally recoverable with ordinary git (`git reset --hard ORIG_HEAD`, or `git rebase --abort` while a conflict is still in progress). [ADR-0062](docs/adrs/adr-0062.md) concludes the honest fix is not a gate that can't distinguish anything but no gate at all: phase 1 (fetch once per repository + classify) now flows straight into phase 2 (re-plan from scratch + execute) with no pause in between, for a single row and a batch alike — the `withProgress` notification goes straight from "Checking…" to "Rebasing N…". **Push (force-with-lease)** keeps its own confirmation unchanged ([ADR-0061](docs/adrs/adr-0061.md)), since publishing a rewrite to a remote is a qualitatively different, harder-to-walk-back risk that a purely local, reflog-recoverable rebase does not share. No change to the daemon, the wire protocol, or the durable `operation`/transient `rebasing` tree cues from ADR-0059 — this is a client-side UI change only. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **ADF schema snapshot bumped to `@atlaskit/adf-schema` 56.1.18** ([#1436](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1436)): Refreshes the pinned upstream snapshot from `56.1.13` to `56.1.18` after the weekly `adf-schema-drift` job detected an upstream release. This is a **version bump only** — the upstream `dist/json-schema/v1/full.json` is **byte-for-byte identical** across these releases (same `full_json_sha256` `75f0809…`), so the vendored `assets/adf-schema/full.json` is unchanged and the regenerated `generated.rs` carries the same `UPSTREAM_ENTRIES` and `UPSTREAM_FULL_JSON_SHA256` as before. The only changes are the provenance constants that pin *which release* the snapshot was taken from: `SCHEMA_VERSION` and `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `src/atlassian/adf_schema.rs`, the mirrored `UPSTREAM_VERSION` / `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `generated.rs`, `assets/adf-schema/provenance.json`, and the JFM spec + README version references. No validation behaviour changes. The upstream tarball SHA-256 (`402cc55a…`) was verified independently against the npm registry.
- **`worktrees rebase` no longer excludes the repository's main working tree** ([#1438](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1438), [ADR-0060](docs/adrs/adr-0060.md)): the main working tree was structurally skipped by `Inspected::classify` and never enumerated by `--all`, inherited from `close`'s deletion guard even though rebase is not destructive to the repository's set of worktrees the way `close` is. The main tree is now classified — and, if eligible, rebased — exactly like any other worktree: still refused if dirty (absent `--autostash`), detached, mid-operation, or unresolvable, never refused for being main. `--all` now targets every worktree of the repository, main included, and an explicit `<PATH>` naming the main tree is no longer discarded. `SkipReason::MainWorkingTree` is removed. The VS Code extension's **Rebase on main** action follows: it is offered on every worktree row (not just linked ones), and no longer pre-filters a selected main tree out of the batch before sending it to the daemon. See [ADR-0060](docs/adrs/adr-0060.md), which supersedes [ADR-0055](docs/adrs/adr-0055.md) §3 on this point.

### Fixed
- **The current-window tick disappeared, and mismatched the open dot's colour, during a rebase** ([#1433](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1433)): a worktree row's icon is the only place the **Worktrees** tree says "you are standing here" — the badge layer's two characters are already claimed by the PR-check and Claude-session providers — but the mid-rebase cue added by [#1415](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1415) took the row icon over entirely, colour included, so the current window's tick vanished for the duration. The durable half is what actually hurt: `operation` is read from `repo.state()` into every snapshot, and `worktrees rebase --keep-conflicts` deliberately leaves a conflicting worktree mid-rebase, so the very row you were working in kept a `$(warning)` triangle instead of a tick for as long as the conflict took to resolve — across daemon restarts, and precisely when finding your own window matters most.
  - `worktreeRowIcon` (`editors/vscode/src/icons.ts`) splits what was one precedence rule into a **glyph rule** and a **colour rule**, decided independently. Glyph, first match wins: the current window (a tick, unconditionally) → the rebase cue (spinner while rebasing, warning triangle for one left unfinished) → the open badge (a dot for a worktree open elsewhere) → the plain branch glyph. Colour, first match wins: the rebase cue (yellow) → the row's tag ([#1428](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1428)) → the row's state (green for an open worktree, else the theme default). A current-window row mid-rebase therefore now renders a **yellow tick**: identity from the glyph, rebase state from the colour.
  - A second, smaller defect folded into the same fix: the current-window tick was `charts.blue` while the open-in-another-window dot was `charts.green`, even though both report the same fact — a live window — and differ only in *which* window, a distinction the glyph already carries. Both are now green; a row tagged via **Set Colour…** ([#1428](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1428)) is unaffected. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md) for the documented glyph/colour precedence.
- **Repo-row model marker could name a family from a hidden worktree** ([#1448](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1448)): the new repo-row model-marker rollup initially unioned *every* child worktree's families unconditionally, so a repository row could show a model family that belonged only to a worktree "hide closed worktrees" was currently hiding. `treeDataProvider.ts` now filters to the same visible set `worktreeNodes()` uses before unioning, via a `visibleWorktrees`/`visibleWorktreePaths` predicate extracted into `tree.ts` — the marker had briefly reimplemented that filter inline with no test coverage, which is exactly the drift that produced the bug.
- **A mid-session `/model` switch never reached the sessions tracker**: found while manually validating the Worktrees tree's new `[hsof*]` model-family marker — switching a wrapped session from Sonnet to Opus left the marker (and `omni-dev sessions list`) stuck showing Sonnet for the rest of the session, in two compounding ways.
  - `StreamTracker::absorb_identity`, the `omni-dev claude-wrap` stream-json parser behind Feed 4 of the sessions service, treated `model` as fill-once identity, the same as `session_id` — captured from the first `system`/`init` line it ever saw and never touched again. But Claude Code re-emits `system`/`init`, model included, at the start of **every turn**, not just session start, specifically so a client can observe a model change; the tracker was simply discarding every later announcement. `model` is no longer fill-once: it now always takes the latest non-empty value a `system`/`init` line carries, while `session_id`/`cwd` stay fill-once, since those are genuinely fixed for a session's lifetime.
  - Also added: `absorb_identity` now reads a `set_model` `control_request` — sent editor → CLI on the wrapped process's **stdin**, which `claude-wrap` already tees but never inspected for this — and updates the model the moment it arrives, with `emit_if_changed` now also firing on a model-only change so it reaches the daemon immediately rather than waiting for the next state transition. **This does not, in practice, make the Claude VS Code extension's own switches instant**, and it is worth being precise about why: its in-chat `/model` command never sends `set_model` at all (it is a local UI action that mutates in-process state and persists to the user's `~/.claude/settings.json`, per Claude Code's own source — no stdio signal at the moment of the switch), and separately the extension respawns a **fresh wrapped process per turn** rather than keeping one alive across a conversation, so there is usually no live process for a between-turn `set_model` to reach anyway. For that caller the fix above (re-reading `system`/`init` every turn) remains the one doing the real work — a switch is still only observed once the next prompt starts a new process. The `set_model` handling is a correctness improvement for whatever *does* send it to a live process (e.g. an external SDK-driven caller using the stream-json control protocol directly), not a guaranteed instant path for this extension.
  - The daemon-side registry (`src/sessions.rs`'s `fill()`) already applied updates correctly — both additions were confined to the wrapper's own local tracker state. New regression tests in `src/sessions/stream.rs` cover both: `a_later_init_confirming_the_already_set_model_does_not_re_emit` for the first, `a_set_model_request_updates_and_reports_the_model_immediately` and `a_set_model_with_no_model_field_is_left_for_the_next_init_to_settle` for the second. No wire-format or CLI change.

## [0.39.0] - 2026-07-26

### Added
- **Reload the VS Code windows of selected worktrees** ([#1417](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1417)): the worktrees tree could already act on a *batch* of selected worktrees' windows — **Close Window**, **Close Worktree**, **Reposition Windows to Match** — but there was no way to **reload** them, so the standard remedy after an extension update, a settings change needing a restart, a wedged language server, or a `git` operation that left the SCM view stale meant focusing each window in turn and running `Developer: Reload Window` by hand. That is precisely the per-window manual sweep the companion exists to remove: the daemon already knows every window open across every VS Code instance, and already has a proven channel for telling *another* window to act on itself — the `close` directive that rides the heartbeat reply ([#1277](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1277)). The worktrees service gains a **`reload`** op and the registry a second directive of the same shape (`reload_pending`, behind its own `Mutex` taken independently of the window map's and of `close_pending`'s, cleared on `unregister`, in-memory so a daemon restart simply drops it). The heartbeat reply carries an additive `reload: true`, taken-and-cleared so it fires exactly once and **omitted when nothing is pending**, so a companion that predates this change sees a byte-identical reply; `close` and `reload` are set and consumed independently, so both can ride one reply, with the companion checking `close` first because closing subsumes reloading. Addressed by **window key** like `reposition` rather than by path like `close` — a reload acts on a *window*, and one tree row is one window, whereas a path can be open in several — which also means there is **no `requester_key`**: the invoking window reloads itself directly rather than waiting a heartbeat for its own directive, so the daemon never needs to know who asked. The one real hazard is self-reload: `workbench.action.reloadWindow` kills the extension host exactly as `closeWindow` does, so the extension runs the others to completion, reports, and only then reloads itself. Deliberately **not** two-phase like `close` — a reload creates, modifies and destroys nothing, and VS Code's hot exit preserves dirty editors, so it follows the `reposition` precedent of firing and reporting; there is correspondingly nothing to undo. Selected worktrees with no open window are silently skipped, and the count is surfaced in the summary so a partially-applied batch is never *quietly* narrowed. A new **`omni-dev worktrees reload <PATH>…`** drives it from the CLI (where, unlike the tree's sweep, a named worktree with no open window is an error rather than a skip), and `omni-dev worktrees heartbeat` now reports both pending directives. Known limitation, inherent to the companion contract and identical to the one `close` already accepts: the directive rides the ~10 s heartbeat, so a cross-window reload lands up to ~10 s later and a batch visibly staggers — which is why the reply and the notification claim only what was **signalled**, never what reloaded (a reloaded window re-registers under the same key, so the daemon has no completion to observe and deliberately does not wait for one). Auditable as a `reload` INFO line in `omni-dev daemon logs` carrying counts and any unknown keys — never a path, which this op never sees. **Adds no capability**: it rides the same `0600` Unix socket, persists nothing, touches neither git nor the OS, and is strictly weaker than the `close` capability a socket writer already has — so no ADR. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Push Claude session state to every window via a sessions `subscribe` op** ([#1414](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1414)): the session cues in the Worktrees tree were the last piece of tree state each window discovered by **polling on its own timer**, while everything else — worktree rows, PR badges, the show-closed toggle — already arrived over the worktrees push stream ([ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md)). Because each window's poll phase is set by whenever that window happened to activate, two windows showing the *same* worktree row could disagree about its cue for up to a full ~10 s tick, with which one was stale being arbitrary. Measured on a live daemon (30 windows, 39 sessions), the daemon was never the bottleneck — a `sessions list` round-trip is **under 1 ms** and every window ticked at exactly 10.00 s with no drift — so the skew was purely an artefact of polling. The sessions service now exposes a **`subscribe`** op alongside `list`: `SessionsRegistry` carries the same `tokio::sync::watch` change-notify the worktrees registry uses, and the daemon's existing `run_stream` loop pushes a fresh snapshot on each change (plus its own `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_STREAM_TICK` re-sample, which is what still catches TTL-driven ageing and fires no event of its own). Two things fall out for free: a window whose tree view is hidden no longer goes blind (it polled *not at all* while hidden), and the 30 windows × 1 round-trip per 10 s spent discovering "nothing changed" is gone. Unlike the worktrees stream this needs **no coalescing snapshot cache** ([#1303](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1303)) — a session's `repo` is enriched at `observe` time, so `list` is pure formatting and the stream's snapshot is literally the same shared payload builder, guaranteeing a one-shot fetch and a pushed frame are byte-identical. The bump is deliberately **narrower than "the serialized payload differs"**: `SessionEntry` carries `last_seen`/`last_event`, which churn on *every* hook event, and the companion's `window` op fires per window per ~10 s — bumping on those would push a full snapshot to every window several times a second with the server's snapshot diff unable to suppress any of it, so the registry bumps only when something a consumer **renders** changes (a session appearing or ending, a state transition, a best-effort field taking a new value, a window report that alters the source join) and lets the rest ride the periodic re-sample. `list` itself never bumps: it is the body of every subscriber's snapshot, so it would feed the stream loop back into itself. **No trust-boundary change** — a read-only stream over the same `0600` Unix socket, carrying exactly what `list` already served.
- **Worktree rebase moves into the daemon, and conflicts are left in place to resolve** ([#1415](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1415)): the batch rebase added in [#1400](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1400) was confined to the CLI on the strength of one claim in [ADR-0055](docs/adrs/adr-0055.md) — that the daemon's minimal launchd environment lacks `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and so could not authenticate a fetch to an SSH remote. That claim was **wrong**: `ps eww` on the resident daemon shows launchd exporting `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` into the per-user session, and a fetch under exactly that reconstructed environment succeeds. What the daemon genuinely lacks is a useful `PATH` — the same problem [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md) §3 already solves for the `code` launcher and `resolve_gh_binary` for `gh` — which is binary resolution, not credentials, and is now closed by a matching **`resolve_git_binary()`** (`OMNI_DEV_GIT_BIN` → well-known absolute paths → bare `git`). The `worktrees` service therefore gains a two-phase **`rebase`** op modelled on `merge-queue`: phase 1 fetches each selected repository's target ref **once** and classifies every worktree — which *is* the "is this worth doing?" gate, skipping the main working tree, a detached `HEAD`, a dirty tree, an operation already in progress, an unresolvable target, and anything already up to date — and phase 2 **re-plans from scratch** before rewriting anything, so a worktree that went dirty between the phases is skipped rather than rebased on the strength of a stale verdict. Concurrent executes are serialized, since linked worktrees share one object database. The headline behaviour change is **`--keep-conflicts`** (new on the CLI, always sent by the tree view): a rebase that hits conflicts now leaves the worktree **mid-rebase**, markers on disk, to be resolved in place and finished with `git rebase --continue` — instead of `git rebase --abort` discarding every hunk that had already applied cleanly and every conflict `rerere` had already resolved. The batch continues either way, and each run is auditable in `omni-dev daemon logs`. This is a real escalation of what the daemon can do — it now rewrites git history and spends the user's ambient credentials, the second op after `merge-queue` to do the latter — so it stays same-user-bounded behind the `0600` socket, refuses the main working tree on the same structural `is_main` guard as `close`, and re-validates everything on execute. Off macOS the premise may not hold (a systemd user unit does not inherit `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` unless it is imported), which degrades honestly through the existing `fetch-failed` reporting, with the local CLI as the fallback; `omni-dev worktrees rebase` keeps its own engine and still works with no daemon running at all. See [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/adr-0059.md).
- **Rebase state on worktree rows** ([#1415](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1415)): the `tree` payload gains two additive fields so a client can show what a rebase is doing. `operation` is the worktree's own in-progress git operation (`rebase`, `merge`, `cherry-pick`, `bisect`, …) read fresh off disk into every snapshot — **durable**, so a conflict left in place keeps showing until it is resolved, across daemon restarts. `rebasing` is a transient in-memory flag meaning the daemon is working on that worktree right now. They are not redundant: a rebase that applies cleanly never writes an on-disk state to report, so without the second a multi-second rebase would look like nothing happening. Both are omitted when unset, so an older client sees a byte-identical snapshot.
- **Reposition worktrees' VS Code windows to match one another** ([#1407](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1407)): with a fan-out of per-issue worktrees open, getting a chosen subset of windows to occupy the same screen slot was manual, per-window dragging, repeated every time the set changed. The daemon's worktrees service gains a **`reposition`** op that moves and resizes each selected worktree's **already-open** VS Code window onto the geometry of the window the command was invoked from — which is the reference and is never itself moved. **Z-order is untouched**: nothing is raised, lowered, or activated, which falls out of the mechanism rather than being maintained, since setting a window's macOS Accessibility position/size attributes does not raise it (so this deliberately does **not** reuse the `open`/focus path, which spawns `code` and lets VS Code raise the window). This has to live in the daemon because a VS Code extension **cannot** read or set its window's OS position or size at all — there is no API and the request is explicitly parked upstream ([microsoft/vscode#28150](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues/28150)) — and the daemon is already a native process inside the user's GUI session that knows every window ([ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md)). The hard part is identification, not permission: the registry stores no OS window id, and its `pid` is the **extension-host** process, while in Electron every window's native `NSWindow` belongs to the single **main** process — so resolution is `ppid(pid)` (one batched `ps`, which also keeps Stable/Insiders/VSCodium apart) to find the owning application, then the worktree's root name matched against each window's `AXTitle` through a uniqueness-requiring exact→suffix→substring cascade. It **refuses to guess**: where no rule isolates a single window the target is reported `ambiguous` and left alone, because moving the wrong window is a worse and less attributable failure than moving none. Every target comes back with its own outcome (`moved`/`unchanged`/`partial`/`no-window`/`not-found`/`ambiguous`/`minimized`/`fullscreen`/`reference`/`failed`), and fullscreen or minimized windows are skipped rather than mangled. Deliberately **not** two-phase like `close`/`merge-queue` — nothing durable is created, modified, or destroyed, so a modal on a routine layout command would cost more than it protects — with reversibility provided instead by a **one-level, in-memory** undo record and a **`reposition-undo`** op. A new **`omni-dev worktrees reposition --reference <PATH> [<PATH>…] [--dry-run] [--undo]`** drives it from the CLI, where `--dry-run` resolves everything and writes nothing: because matching is the fragile part, that dry run is the primary operator tool, and it is trustworthy precisely because it is the same code path as a real run. Auditable as `reposition` INFO lines in `omni-dev daemon logs`. **macOS only** for now (consistent with the tray), and it needs a one-time **Accessibility** grant that the daemon cannot self-grant, that takes effect only after `omni-dev daemon restart`, and that — because omni-dev installs unsigned — is invalidated by every upgrade; the extension surfaces a missing grant with a link to the settings pane rather than an opaque failure. All Accessibility FFI is confined to one `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` module behind a four-method trait, with the matching and planning logic platform-independent and unit-tested against a fake, so a Linux/Windows port would be one additional implementation. Adds no socket capability and persists nothing, but is the project's first **OS** trust surface. See [ADR-0058](docs/adrs/adr-0058.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Authoritative Claude session state via a stream-tee process wrapper** ([#1406](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1406)): the sessions tracker could only ever *infer* what a session was doing — `working`/`idle` was guesswork, and `waiting_for_permission` arrived only if you had installed the `Notification` hook. That is the one state worth knowing about a window you are not looking at, and Claude Code ships no event for it. It does ship the state in its `--output-format stream-json` stdout, which is exactly what the Claude VS Code extension reads, and the extension's supported `claudeCode.claudeProcessWrapper` setting lets omni-dev sit in that stream. A new **`omni-dev claude-wrap -- <cmd> <args…>`** wraps the Claude process transparently — stdio forwarded byte-for-byte, exit status (and `128 + signo`) propagated, `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` relayed — while teeing both directions to a parser that reports the exact state to the daemon. Both directions matter: a `can_use_tool` request travels out on stdout and its answer back on stdin, so one-sided parsing would show a session entering `waiting_for_permission` and never leaving. **`omni-dev sessions install-wrapper`** / **`uninstall-wrapper`** set it up: a `0700` shim beside the daemon socket plus the VS Code user setting pointing at it (the extension spawns the wrapper with no shell, so the setting must name a single executable file); a JSONC settings file that cannot be rewritten fails with the exact line to paste. Being in Claude's launch path makes **fail-open** structural rather than promised — the byte pumps write and flush before they even attempt the tee, the tee drops lines rather than blocking, a missing daemon is a silent no-op, and a terminal launch is `exec`-replaced with no observation at all; the worst case is losing visibility, never Claude failing to launch. **No secret is persisted and no conversation content is ever logged**: only the state, `session_id`, `cwd` and model leave the process, over the daemon's existing `0600` socket. Reporting reuses the existing `observe` op via one new `SessionEvent::StreamState` variant, so there is no new op and no wire change, and a 30s keep-alive makes this the one feed with real process-lifetime liveness — a wrapped session idle at the prompt no longer ages out. See [ADR-0057](docs/adrs/adr-0057.md) and [docs/sessions-service.md](docs/sessions-service.md).
- **Batch-rebase worktrees onto remote main, fetching once per repository** ([#1400](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1400)): keeping a fan-out of per-issue feature worktrees current with `main` was a manual, per-worktree chore (`cd`, `git fetch`, `git rebase origin/main`, repeat), and doing it naively re-fetches the remote for *every* worktree — wasteful (N round-trips) and subtly wrong, because if `origin/main` advances between fetches the worktrees land on different tips and stop sharing a base. New **`omni-dev worktrees rebase [<PATH>...] [--all] [--onto <ref>] [--autostash] [--dry-run] [-y] [-o table|json]`** fetches the remote default branch **exactly once per repository** up front (linked worktrees share one object database, so a single `git fetch` updates the tracking ref every worktree sees), then rebases each selected worktree onto that single pinned snapshot. Selection is explicit — `<PATH>...` or `--all` (every linked worktree of the current repo, never the main working tree, keyed on the structural `is_main` fact as `close` is) — so a bare invocation is a usage error, not a silent mass-rebase. A dirty worktree is skipped with a reason (unless `--autostash`); a detached HEAD or in-progress rebase/merge is skipped; a rebase that hits conflicts is by default `git rebase --abort`-ed so the worktree is **left exactly as it was** and reported (pass `--keep-conflicts` to leave it mid-rebase instead — see [#1415](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1415) above), and the batch continues for the rest (per-worktree `rebased`/`up-to-date`/`skipped`/`conflict`/`fetch-failed`). A rebase rewrites history, so it **confirms by default** ([ADR-0027](docs/adrs/adr-0027.md)); `--dry-run` still fetches (non-destructive) so the behind-count preview is accurate. Per [ADR-0003](docs/adrs/adr-0003.md) the fetch and rebase shell out to the user's `git` (git2 only reads). Unlike every other `worktrees` subcommand this one runs **entirely locally and never contacts the daemon** — since #1415 a deliberate choice rather than the credential necessity originally claimed, so that a batch rebase keeps working with no daemon running and `--all`/`--onto` stay out of the wire protocol. No new dependency and no widened trust boundary. See [ADR-0055](docs/adrs/adr-0055.md), [ADR-0059](docs/adrs/adr-0059.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Batch merge-queue enqueue from the worktrees service** ([#1401](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1401)): the daemon's worktrees service gains a **`merge-queue`** op that enqueues the selected worktrees' pull requests into the repo's **GitHub merge queue** — but only the ones that are genuinely ready to merge, with everything else returned **skipped with a reason** rather than silently dropped or failing the batch. Finishing a worktree ends in merging it, and with a merge queue enabled that means *enqueuing*, which previously meant visiting each PR in the browser one at a time. Eligibility is evaluated **server-side** across seven gates: a clean working tree (`dirty`/`untracked`), a real commit (`no-commits`), fully pushed (`unpushed`/`no-upstream`), an open PR heading the branch (`no-pr`), not a draft (`draft`), not conflicting (`conflicting`), and green checks (`checks-failing`) — plus a `stale` guard refusing to enqueue when the remote PR head is not the commit you are looking at (a detached HEAD and a non-GitHub remote are refused locally too). The three **local git** gates run first, so an ineligible worktree costs **zero** GitHub API calls; the survivors resolve in a **single** `gh api graphql` query (the same aliasing that keeps the badge poll at 1 point), making a whole batch ~1 point plus one mutation per PR actually enqueued. Deliberately stricter than the queue itself requires — the point of a batch action is that you do not inspect each PR, so an enqueue that would predictably bounce is reported as a skip instead (`BLOCKED`/`UNKNOWN` merge states are *not* treated as conflicts; only `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY` are). Two-phase like `close` ([ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md)) and **re-validated on execute**, so a worktree that went dirty, was force-pushed, or had its PR closed between the check and the confirm is skipped at execute time — the UI gating is convenience, never the guard. A PR already in the queue is idempotent success (`already_queued`), not a failure, and a per-PR rejection (queue not enabled on the repo, PR unmergeable, insufficient permissions) lands in `failed[]` without sinking the rest. A new **`omni-dev worktrees merge-queue <PATH>… [--check] [--yes]`** drives it from the CLI — `--check` prints the eligibility report and stops; otherwise it prints the report, confirms interactively unless `--yes`, and executes. Both phases are auditable as `merge-queue check`/`merge-queue enqueue` INFO lines in `omni-dev daemon logs`. Uses a **dedicated** GraphQL query rather than widening the continuously-polled badge fragment (which would bloat the hot path and force a cache-schema change), sharing the check-rollup sub-selection so the CI verdict cannot drift from the badge you see. This is the service's first op to mutate **remote** state, but it creates/modifies/deletes no local file and rides the existing `0600` socket using the user's own `gh` credentials — **no credential enters the daemon** and no new trust boundary. Requires the merge queue to already be enabled on the repo. See [ADR-0056](docs/adrs/adr-0056.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).

### Fixed
