- **Sync the commit-guidelines scope list with `scopes.yaml`, and make the rule self-enforcing** ([#1421](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1421)): `.omni-dev/scopes.yaml` is the single source of truth for commit scopes, and the `## Scopes` list in `.omni-dev/commit-guidelines.md` exists only so the AI prompt has that context inline — but nothing checked the two against each other and the markdown had fallen **nine entries behind**, omitting `browser`, `daemon`, `datadog`, `mcp`, `request-log`, `sessions`, `snowflake`, `vscode` and `website`, which between them cover most of the recent feature work. It never produced a visible failure because both files are injected into the same commit-check prompt and the AI judge happened to resolve the contradiction in favour of the authoritative YAML — so this was **prompt-quality debt rather than a live breakage**, but it was a coin flip being paid for in tokens on every CI run. The list is now regenerated from the YAML in full, with each description copied verbatim, and a note records the four Rust ecosystem default scopes (`cargo`, `core`, `lib`, `test`) that `merge_ecosystem_scopes` adds at load time — valid in commits but previously documented in neither file, which was the same class of contradiction. The more durable half of the fix is [`tests/commit_guidelines_scopes_test.rs`](tests/commit_guidelines_scopes_test.rs), which parses both files and enforces all three clauses of STYLE-0007 — the bullet list matches the YAML name-for-name and description-for-description, and every scope used in the `## Examples` section is one the YAML defines — so a third drift fails the build instead of quietly degrading the prompt.
- **`omni-dev worktrees register --repo` no longer panics on a clap global-flag collision** ([#1420](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1420)): `omni-dev worktrees register --key k1 --repo myrepo --folder /tmp` aborted with `thread 'main' panicked … Mismatch between definition and access of 'repo'` and exit 101 — a clap-internal crash, so there was no message telling the user what to do differently. Two args shared one clap **arg id**: the global `-C/--repo` (a `PathBuf`, the `git -C` analogue) and `RegisterCommand`'s own `--repo` (a `String` holding the repository *name*). clap propagates a `global = true` arg **by id**, not by long name — it skips pushing the global into a subcommand that already defines that id, then copies whatever that subcommand matched back up into the root's `ArgMatches`, so the root ended up holding a `String` under `repo` and the derived `Cli` panicked reading it as a `PathBuf`. The subcommand-local flag is therefore renamed to **`--repo-name`** (the field, and so the id, is renamed too — renaming only the long spelling would have left the collision in place). This is a breaking change to an existing flag, but on a surface with no known caller: `register` is a companion feed op and the real client, the VS Code extension, speaks NDJSON to the control socket rather than shelling out. The op's wire payload is unchanged — the key is still `repo`. One consequence worth knowing: the old spelling no longer errors, it now binds the *global* `-C/--repo` (so `worktrees register --repo X` silently sends no repository name). An audit of the other nine global flags found no second collision, and a new structural test walks the whole command tree to assert no subcommand ever re-uses a global arg id, since the failure mode is silent until the global is read.
- **A Claude session waiting on you no longer goes quiet a few seconds later** ([#1418](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1418)): a worktree row in the Worktrees tree would flash amber (`!` — the session is blocked on you) and then, within 0–5 s, turn green (`⚙` — working) while the session was still sitting at the permission prompt, staying green for the entire wait. The row went silent at exactly the moment it should have been shouting, which is the one thing the session cue exists to tell you about a window you are not looking at. The tree was faithfully rendering what the daemon reported: `SessionState::for_event` was last-writer-wins, and the transcript watcher's growth sighting mapped to `working` **unconditionally** — including over a `waiting_for_*` that a `Notification` hook had just set. The trigger is that Claude flushes the assistant `tool_use` line to `~/.claude/projects/**/<id>.jsonl` **before** the prompt it is asking about can possibly be answered, so the watcher's next ≤5 s scan sees the file grow and downgrades the wait; nothing then restores it, because no further growth happens while you sit at the prompt and the next hook (`PostToolUse`) only fires once you answer. The amber therefore lasted a random 0–5 s — the phase of the watcher tick — and with the stream wrapper ([#1406](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1406)) installed the symptom became a 30 s amber→green→amber flip-flop as its keep-alive re-asserted the truth only to be clobbered again. Growth is now ranked **below** any state a hook reported directly: it still means `working` from every other state, but leaves `waiting_for_input`/`waiting_for_permission` untouched — and `ended` with them, which had the mirror-image defect (a session's last lines land around `SessionEnd`, so the scan that follows usually fell inside the 10 s ended-linger window and revived the entry as a `working` phantom held for the full 5 min session TTL). This restores the precedence [ADR-0052](docs/adrs/adr-0052.md) already claimed — `waiting_for_*` is **reliable** (a hook fires it directly) while growth-implies-working is **best-effort inference**, so the inference must not overwrite the signal — and ports the rule the stream wrapper's tracker has always applied, that an outstanding permission prompt outranks whatever the content messages last implied. It cannot strand a row on amber: any later hook releases it, an authoritative stream state still overrides verbatim ([ADR-0057](docs/adrs/adr-0057.md)), and the session TTL is the backstop regardless. That release does have latency, and it is the deliberate side of the trade: no hook fires at the moment you answer a permission prompt (the prompt comes after `PreToolUse`), so on a hooks-only install the next one is the `PostToolUse` that fires when the approved tool *finishes* — a row stays amber for the duration of a long build or test run you just approved. That is the better failure of the two: a stale "blocked on you" is a nag you can see and dismiss, a stale "working" is the alert you never got. With the stream wrapper installed there is no gap at all, since it reports `working` off the `control_response` the moment you answer. The watcher is unchanged — it should keep reporting growth; only the registry's ranking of that report was wrong. **No wire-format, CLI, or extension change**, so no companion release is needed. See [docs/sessions-service.md](docs/sessions-service.md).
- **Closing a worktree whose directory is already gone now actually prunes its admin metadata** ([#1403](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1403)): when a linked worktree's working directory had been deleted **outside** the daemon — a manual `rm -rf`, an OS cleanup, a parent tree removed some other way — **Close Worktree** logged `linked worktree pruned` and replied `removed: true` while pruning nothing: the main repo's `.git/worktrees/<name>/` admin entry survived, `git worktree list` kept reporting the entry as `prunable`, and the row stayed in the tree view. Clicking again did exactly the same thing, so a half-removed worktree was unremovable from the very UI that had just claimed to remove it, and the audit log agreed with the UI rather than with the repository — the worst shape for a destructive op's log to take. `remove_worktree` handled the four (directory × admin-metadata) combinations asymmetrically: a `!path.exists()` early return conflated "already fully removed", where there is genuinely nothing to do, with "half-removed the other way", where the checkout is gone but the admin entry is still present, and never inspected the main repo at all on that path. It now prunes on that branch too — which first requires **finding** the owning repository with no checkout left to read a `.git` gitlink breadcrumb from, so candidates are resolved two ways: path ancestors (the nested `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/<name>` layout) and the registered windows' own folders (an external worktree that shares no ancestor with its repo), with the prune then using `working_tree(false)` and mirroring the live path's lock check. A new `Removal` outcome distinguishes an actual prune from an already-gone no-op, so the audit lines added in [#1364](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1364) now report **three** distinct outcomes instead of conflating the first two — both remain INFO and both still reply `removed: true`, since the row should disappear either way, while a failure remains WARN and propagates — which is what makes a future "the daemon says pruned but the row is still there" answerable from `omni-dev daemon logs` alone. The phase-1 safety check is unchanged and was never the bug: mapping `!path.exists()` to `removable: true` with an `already-removed` note and no dialog is the right verdict for "there is nothing to lose"; the defect was phase 2 reading that verdict as "there is nothing to do". See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).

## [0.38.0] - 2026-07-24

### Added
- **Declarative coverage-diff ignore-list in repo config** ([#1398](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1398)): `coverage diff` could previously exclude CPU-conditional / run-to-run-nondeterministic files only via the per-invocation `--ignore-filename-regex` flag, which a wrapping action (e.g. omni-dev-coverage-check) may not expose. A persistent, version-controlled ignore-list is now read from `.omni-dev/coverage.yaml` (`diff.ignore-filename-regex`) and **set-unioned** with the CLI flag, so a repo declares these files once instead of threading a flag through every caller. Discovery reuses the standard `.omni-dev/` resolution (new `--context-dir` flag, `OMNI_DEV_CONFIG_DIR`, walk-up, local/XDG/home fallbacks); config patterns share the flag's semantics exactly — excluded from both head and baseline before the diff, applied after `--strip-prefix`, empty patterns are no-ops. Missing/empty config is a no-op; a malformed file or invalid regex from either source fails loudly; unknown keys are ignored for forward-compatibility. MCP callers pick up the repo config automatically via walk-up from `repo_path`. Also documents the previously-undocumented `--ignore-filename-regex` flag. See [docs/coverage.md](docs/coverage.md).
- **Audit logging for the destructive worktree `close` op** ([#1364](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1364)): the daemon's one destructive op (closes a VS Code window and `git2`-prunes a linked worktree) is socket-only — neither a logged CLI invocation nor an outbound HTTP request — and previously emitted no `tracing` line, so a worktree could vanish with no record of when, which target, which window, or whether the delete succeeded. Both phases now emit INFO `tracing` lines visible via `omni-dev daemon logs` (the journal on Linux), with no new surface and no trust-boundary change — only the worktree path and window key are logged, never a secret. Phase 1 (safety check) logs the target, whether a window has it open, and the deletability verdict (`removable`/`is_main` + the blocking risk kinds), so "why did the close prompt or refuse?" is answerable from the log; phase 2 (execute) logs the routing decision (self-close vs. how many cross-window targets were signalled), a WARN if a signalled window never closed (the op aborts, worktree left intact), and the terminal outcome — INFO on a pruned worktree / closed window, WARN on a prune failure. The routing predicate and removal-outcome mapping are split into unit-testable helpers. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **CLI typed parity with the VS Code extension's daemon op surface** ([#1361](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1361)): the companion extension drives the daemon through 11 socket ops but the `omni-dev` CLI had typed commands for only 3 (`worktrees list`/`tree`/`focus`) — the other 8 were reachable only through the generic `omni-dev daemon service <svc> <op> --payload '<json>'` escape hatch, so they had no `--help`/golden-snapshot discoverability, no argument validation, and no help for the two-phase `close` protocol. This adds **8 typed clients** (no new ops, no new authority, everything still rides the existing `0600` control socket): **`omni-dev worktrees close <PATH> [--window-only] [--dry-run] [--yes]`** drives the daemon's destructive two-phase `close` from the CLI — it prints the side-effect-free phase-1 safety report (`removable`/`is_main`/`open` + owning window + `risks`/`info`), refuses a non-removable target such as the main working tree, and confirms interactively (unless `--yes`) before the phase-2 execute; a CLI omits `requester_key`, so the daemon treats it as a cross-window close (all git logic stays daemon-side, [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md)). **`worktrees show-closed [true|false]`** reads (from the `tree` snapshot's `show_closed`) or sets (`set-show-closed`) the cross-window toggle. **`worktrees tree --follow`** (`-f`) streams live snapshots via the daemon's `subscribe` push op, re-rendering on every change until Ctrl-C (a new `DaemonClient::subscribe`/`DaemonSubscription` streaming primitive backs it; honours `-o json` as an NDJSON stream). The companion **feed ops** are exposed for scripted/headless companions and integration testing: **`worktrees register --key K --folder P… [--repo R] [--title T] [--pid N]`**, **`worktrees heartbeat --key K`** (prints `known` and any pending `close`), **`worktrees unregister --key K`**, plus **`sessions window --key K [--folder P…] [--tabs N] [--terminals N]`** and **`sessions window-unregister --key K`**. Every command carries `--socket`; all newly rendered daemon-supplied strings are control-character-sanitized ([#1137](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1137) precedent). No new trust boundary or authority (ADR-0040/0048/0049 unchanged). See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md) and [docs/sessions-service.md](docs/sessions-service.md).
- **Logged `git worktree` wrappers — accidental worktree removal is now recoverable from the log** ([#1392](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1392)): bare `git worktree` operations never pass through omni-dev, so when a worktree was removed by mistake the request log had nothing to recover from — no path, no branch, no commit — and `git worktree remove` deletes the working directory (with `--force`, including unsaved work). New **`omni-dev git worktree {add,remove,list,move,prune,repair}`** subcommands are thin wrappers over the real `git worktree` verbs whose entire value is the `kind: "worktree"` request-log record each writes (tagged `service: "worktree"`, `command: ["git","worktree","<verb>"]`): `remove` captures the worktree's `path`, `branch`, HEAD `commit`, `had_uncommitted`, and `used_force` **before** the removal — enough to re-create the branch (`git branch <branch> <commit>`) and re-attach — and the record is written on failure paths too; `prune` records a JSON `pruned` list of `{path, branch, commit}` from a before/after porcelain diff; `add`/`move` record the resolved branch and paths; `list --output-json` emits `git worktree list --porcelain` as JSON for machine consumption. Git's output, exit behaviour, and argv semantics pass through unchanged (metadata capture is strictly best-effort via `git2` and can never fail the wrapped operation), and argv is scrubbed by the existing redaction before write. The log surface grew to match: `omni-dev log --command 'git worktree remove'`, `--service worktree`, and the MCP `log_search` equivalents return the recovery row; `log count --kind worktree` tallies; and the `--query` mini-language now falls back to the record's free-form `context` map for unknown field names, so `branch:issue-1392` / `path:demo-wt` query the recovery fields directly (HTTP correlation tags included). Existing records and queries are unaffected — the new kind rides the schema's forward-compatible `#[serde(other)]` contract, and older binaries render the records via the generic fallthrough. Distinct from `omni-dev worktrees` (the daemon's VS Code-windows view). Part 2 of the issue (an opt-in shell wrapper so *bare* `git worktree` is also logged) is a follow-up. See [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).
- **Run a subset of daemon services** ([#1318](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1318)): `omni-dev daemon run` gains a `--services <SVC>` flag (and the daemon honors an `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_SERVICES` env var) to host only a chosen subset — e.g. `worktrees` alone, with no Snowflake auth machinery and no browser-bridge TCP planes. An unselected service is never constructed, so its startup work is skipped entirely. Because launchd/systemd `exec` a fixed command with a minimal environment (an env var set in the user's shell never reaches the demand-spawned daemon), `daemon start --services a,b` **bakes the selection into the generated launchd plist / systemd user unit** as a `--services` argument so it survives the service-manager exec and login auto-start. `daemon restart` preserves the running daemon's current subset unless `--services` overrides it (a full daemon still bakes no argument, so its restart stays byte-identical to a fresh `start`). Valid names: `browser-bridge`, `snowflake`, `worktrees`, `sessions`. Selection is resolved at daemon **build** time — the service registry stays append-only, so a runtime enable/disable toggle remains a follow-up. The `!` marks a **library-API break only** — no user-facing CLI change, since the default stays "host everything": `build_default_registry` takes a new `services: &ServiceSelection` parameter, `DaemonRunConfig` gains a public `services` field, and the `install_and_load` launchd/systemd signatures take the selection (technically API-breaking for library consumers).

### Changed
- **Bold/italic/strike around inline code no longer aborts a JIRA/Confluence write — the converter now splits the marks silently** ([#1391](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1391)): JFM like `` **`widget`** `` produced a text run carrying both `strong` and `code`, which ADF forbids (`code` combines only with `link`/`annotation`), so the whole write bounced with the [#1087](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1087)-enriched validation error and the author had to restructure the sentence and retry — this ships the "option 1" follow-up that entry left open, making the constraint invisible. JFM→ADF conversion now drops `strong`/`em`/`strike` from code-marked runs in a document-level pass (`split_emphasis_code_marks`): inline code always parses into its own run, so the siblings produced by the same emphasis span keep their styling — `` **foo `bar` baz** `` becomes bold `foo `, monospace `bar`, bold ` baz` — and the rendered output is identical to the (illegal) combined form, with a `link` on the code run preserved (`code`+`link` is legal). The pass runs after block building, so headings keep their existing [#1005](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1005) behavior (code stripped, bold kept). Deliberately scoped to the marks markdown emphasis syntax leaks: explicit span-syntax combinations (`` [`x`]{underline} ``, `:span[…]{color=…}`) are still preserved byte-faithfully per the issue #554 fidelity contract and still rejected by the mark validator with the enriched `cannot be combined with` error, which also continues to guard raw-ADF input. No CLI-surface change. See [docs/specs/jfm.md](docs/specs/jfm.md).
- **ADF schema snapshot bumped to `@atlaskit/adf-schema` 56.1.13** ([#1385](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1385)): Refreshes the pinned upstream snapshot from `56.1.3` to `56.1.13` after the weekly `adf-schema-drift` job detected an upstream release (the report cited `56.1.8`; upstream had moved to `56.1.13` by refresh time, so the snapshot pins the latest to keep the next drift run green). This is a **version bump only** — and another inert one: the upstream `dist/json-schema/v1/full.json` is **byte-for-byte identical** across these releases (same `full_json_sha256` `75f0809…`), so the vendored `assets/adf-schema/full.json` is unchanged and the regenerated `generated.rs` carries the same `UPSTREAM_ENTRIES` and `UPSTREAM_FULL_JSON_SHA256` as before. The only changes are the provenance constants that pin *which release* the snapshot was taken from: `SCHEMA_VERSION` and `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `src/atlassian/adf_schema.rs`, the mirrored `UPSTREAM_VERSION` / `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `generated.rs`, `assets/adf-schema/provenance.json`, and the JFM spec + README version references. No validation behaviour changes. The upstream tarball SHA-256 (`6d199ff2…`) was verified independently against the npm registry.

## [0.37.0] - 2026-07-22

### Added
- **Ground-truth counting of every GitHub API call** ([#1387](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1387)): omni-dev now counts each `gh` invocation it makes, so we can answer "how many GitHub API calls did *omni-dev* make in the last hour," "which subcommand dominates," and "did a cost-reduction fix actually land" — the local counterpart to `gh api rate_limit`, which is GitHub's *server-side* view of everything the token spent. Every GitHub call already funnels through the `gh` subprocess (the token never enters our process), so a single choke point (`github_metrics::run_gh`) now wraps every Rust `gh` call site and records one `kind: "gh"` request-log line per invocation — carrying the semantic subcommand (`api graphql`, `pr list`, `repo view`, …), scrubbed argv, source (`cli`/`daemon`/`mcp`), exit code, and duration. A new **`omni-dev log count`** subcommand aggregates the request log by record kind and source over a `--since`/`--until` window, with `--json`; **`--kind gh`** narrows to GitHub calls and adds the richer breakdown by category (`api` vs `subcommand` vs local `--version`) and subcommand. The records are also queryable via `omni-dev log --query 'kind:gh'`. The daemon logs a summary of the counters to its own log ~5 seconds after startup, every 10 minutes, and once on shutdown (covering SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP and the `shutdown` op via the shared cancellation token), and surfaces the current count in `daemon status`. Recording reuses the request log's best-effort, exit-code-safe, redacting, `0600` infrastructure and honors `OMNI_DEV_LOG_DISABLE`; no secret is ever written. Phase 1 covers the Rust daemon + CLI + MCP call sites; the VS Code companion extension's `gh pr list` (a separate process) is a planned follow-up. See [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).
- **Per-repository PR-badge polling, off by default and time-boxed** ([#1376](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1376)): the worktrees daemon's PR-badge poller is now opt-in **per GitHub repository** rather than all-or-nothing. Since [#1337](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1337) the poller resolved badges for **every** `(repo, branch)` the tree knew, so with many repos open but only a couple in active work most of GitHub's API budget went to polling repos nobody was looking at (the session that motivated this exhausted its rate limit). Polling now defaults **off**: a new `set-polling` worktrees op (payload `{ owner, name, enabled }`) toggles one repo — covering all its worktrees/branches, keyed by `owner/name` — and a repo the daemon is not polling issues **zero** `gh` (the single filter point is `pr_watch_from_snapshot`, which skips any repo not stamped `polling_enabled: true`). Each repo in the `tree`/`subscribe` snapshot carries `polling_enabled` (omitted when false, so a not-polled repo and a pre-#1376 daemon stay byte-identical on the wire), and disabling a repo drops its badges on the next snapshot because the badge fold skips a not-polled repo. **Enabling is a 15-minute lease, not a permanent flag:** the repo **auto-disables** when the lease elapses (reaped on read, the stale-window-TTL precedent, so the icon greys / badges strip / polling stops within one ~10 s snapshot tick), and re-enabling refreshes it — so an idle repo stops costing budget even if the user forgets to turn it off. Leases are **persisted** with their expiry under the daemon data dir (`worktrees-polling.json`, `0600`), so a restart within the window keeps the remaining lease and drops any that expired while down. **Behavior change:** on upgrade, PR badges go dark until you enable a repo (right-click the repo in the VS Code **Worktrees** view → **Enable PR Polling**; the icon turns green for ~15 minutes). No new trust boundary and no CLI-surface change — the toggle rides the existing `0600` control socket (reachable via `omni-dev daemon service worktrees set-polling --payload …`). See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **GitHub API rate-limit monitor in the daemon** ([#1375](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1375)): the daemon now polls `gh api rate_limit` on a fixed cadence and surfaces per-resource used-percentage (`graphql`, `core`, and `search`) so an approaching budget exhaustion is visible as a *trend* before it rate-limits `gh` machine-wide — the failure mode that previously drained silently until commands started failing with `API rate limit already exceeded`. Querying `/rate_limit` is **exempt** (it spends nothing against any budget, confirmed empirically), so the monitor costs nothing against the very budget it watches and needs no adaptive backoff. It surfaces three ways: a top-level `github rate limit: graphql 82% (4100/5000, resets 06:50Z) · core 3% (27/1000)` line in `omni-dev daemon status` (with a `⚠` when any resource is ≥ ~80%); an additive optional `github_rate_limit` object on the `-o json` status payload (`{ graphql, core, search }`, each `{ used, limit, remaining, percent, reset }`, omitted until the first poll so pre-#1375 clients stay byte-compatible); and a compact `github: graphql 82% · core 3%` line in the macOS tray's Worktrees submenu. The poller also logs a `WARN` (via `omni-dev daemon logs`) the moment a resource *crosses* ~80%, once per crossing rather than every poll. The reading is machine-wide but the poller lives in the worktrees service (the daemon's `gh` consumer); no credential enters the daemon — it shells out to `gh`, following [ADR-0003](docs/adrs/adr-0003.md)/[ADR-0050](docs/adrs/adr-0050.md). Cadence is a new `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_RATE_LIMIT_POLL` whole-seconds knob (default 60). No new trust boundary and no clap-surface change. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Git commit provenance in `daemon status` and `omni-dev --version`** ([#1374](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1374)): two daemons built from different commits of the *same* crate version were previously indistinguishable — both advertised e.g. `v0.36.0` — so confirming that a rebuild+reinstall+`daemon restart` had actually loaded an unreleased fix required manual process/mtime forensics. A new `build.rs` now captures the commit SHA (short + long), commit date, working-tree dirty flag, and a build timestamp at compile time (best-effort: a build outside a git checkout, e.g. a crates.io download or release tarball, simply reports none and every consumer degrades gracefully). `omni-dev daemon status` renders them in the header — `daemon: running (v0.36.0, a6d304fd, built 2026-07-20T…, dirty)` — and in `-o json` as additive sibling fields (`commit`/`commit_long`/`commit_date`/`dirty`/`build_timestamp`), flattened onto the existing `status`/`ping` payload with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` so a daemon built without git metadata stays byte-identical on the wire and older clients ignore the extra keys. `omni-dev --version` (long form) now shows the same provenance while `-V` keeps the bare crate version. The CLI→daemon staleness check (`warn_version_mismatch`) now compares on commit SHA when both sides know theirs, so a rebuilt-but-same-version resident daemon the CLI has outrun is correctly flagged for `daemon restart` — closing the blind spot in the previous crate-version-string equality. No new dependencies and no CLI arg/flag/help-surface change (the `help_all` golden snapshot is unaffected). Known trade-off: the dirty flag can lag until the next commit/branch change, since uncommitted edits have no build-script input to watch.

### Changed
- **Every PR poll doubles as a free GitHub budget reading, and the rate-limit poller idles when nothing is watched** ([#1389](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1389), fix 8): the badge-poll GraphQL query now carries a `rateLimit { limit cost remaining used resetAt }` block — a meta-field GitHub answers **without** adding to the query's own cost — so **(8a)** each poll folds a fresh `graphql` reading into the shared rate-limit cache (merging with, not clobbering, the standalone poller's `core`/`search`), and its `cost` reports the poll's **actual** point price. That verifies the "1 point per poll" assumption at scale instead of taking it on faith: measured `cost: 1` at 37 branches (it rises to ~2 near 100, ~4 near 200), so a large watch set is confirmed cheap rather than suspected expensive — the exact question that turned a recent budget-burn scare into an hour of manual tracing. The poll logs it at debug (`PR poll cost 1 point(s); graphql 123/5000 used, 4877 remaining`). Because the graphql figure now stays current from the PR polls, **(8b)** the standalone `gh api rate_limit` poller only spends a subprocess while a window is registered or a polling lease is active — a fully-idle daemon leaves the last reading in place. The read is exempt (zero budget), so 8b is purely about not waking a subprocess every 60s on an idle machine; 8a is the load-bearing half. No CLI-surface change; `resolve_with` grows a `resolve_with_budget` sibling and the `RateLimitCache` an `observe_graphql` merge. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **"Open Pull Request…" and the badge fallback move off per-window `gh` into the daemon** ([#1389](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1389), fix 7): after #1337 moved *badge resolution* into the daemon, two per-window `gh pr list` paths remained in the VS Code companion — the "Open Pull Request…" discovery, and the degraded badge fallback — so with many windows open they, not the daemon, dominated the GraphQL burn (`gh pr list --json statusCheckRollup` is a GraphQL alias), and they are **not** counted by [#1387](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1387) (a separate process). Two changes close that. **(a)** A new **`open-prs`** worktrees op serves a repo's open PRs from a **shared, TTL-cached** `gh pr list` (default 60 s, `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_OPEN_PR_TTL`) routed through the #1387-counted `run_gh`, so N windows dedupe to **one** counted call per repo instead of one per window; the extension answers a branch already carrying a `pr` badge straight from the snapshot (**zero `gh`**), routes repo-wide lookups through the op, and falls back to its own `gh` only against a daemon too old to serve it. **(b)** The extension's badge fallback is now **gated on `polling_enabled`**: a repo you have not enabled polling for (#1376) resolves no badges daemon-side **and** the extension no longer shells `gh pr list` per window for it — closing the gap where *disabling* polling merely moved a repo's cost from one shared daemon call to one `gh` per window (the profile that burned ~half a GraphQL hourly budget across ~30 windows on two VS Code restarts). The `open-prs` op is socket-only (no CLI-surface change); the pure `github.ts` discovery module is unchanged in spirit — it now takes an injected repo-PR fetcher rather than a raw `gh` runner, keeping it `vscode`-free and unit-tested. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Daemon PR-badge poller burns far less of the shared GitHub budget** ([#1389](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1389)): the worktrees poller's fetch decision was a single equality check (`watched != watch`) rebuilt every ~10 s, so **any** change to the watch set — additions, removals, or *either* SHA moving — forced an immediate `gh api graphql` and reset the backoff. That drove three burn profiles: one pending check anywhere pinned the *global* 10 s cadence (360 calls/hour), every window `register`/`unregister` (including on **close**, which cannot change any surviving badge) triggered a fetch, and a full VS Code restart's ~20 windows re-registering one-by-one cost ~40 calls a single call at the end would have covered. Six local optimizations, no webhook or GraphQL-schema change: **(1)** a pure removal now **never** fetches — a window close, VS Code/daemon shutdown, TTL reap, or lapsing 15-minute lease prunes dead cache entries locally and asks nothing; **(2)** the change-notify wake is **debounced** (new `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_PR_DEBOUNCE`, default 2 s) so a re-registration storm collapses into one fetch on the final set; **(3)** a **local commit** no longer triggers a fetch (only a *push* — a moved upstream — or an added target does), since GitHub has not seen the commit and the badge stays correctly stale via the existing local `is_stale_for` check; **(4)** resolved badges are **persisted** across restarts (`worktrees-pr-cache.json`, `0600`, beside `worktrees-polling.json` — number/URL/check-state/draft, no secret) so a restart serves them instantly and **skips its immediate re-poll** when every target already has a fresh verdict; **(5)** the pending cadence **escalates within pending** (10 s for ~2 min after a push, then toward a 60 s ceiling) so a long CI run or zombie suite stops pinning the fast rate; and **(6)** the poller now **consults the shared rate-limit reading** ([#1375](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1375)) and caps its cadence at ≥ 5 min while any resource is over the ~80% warn threshold — the daemon is the single `gh` choke point for every window, so this is the one place a machine-wide cap can be enforced, making budget exhaustion in this class structurally impossible. Rough aggregate on an active working day: ~900–1000 calls → ~250–300 (~70–75%), with the burst scenarios (window churn, VS Code/daemon restart) cut 85–97%. **[#1387](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1387)'s counting is preserved and non-negotiable:** every remaining call still routes through the single counted `run_gh` choke point, verified by a test asserting the poll's counted-record delta equals the ground-truth number of `gh` subprocesses it spawned (0 when it coalesces/skips, 1 per fetch). The fetch-trigger, escalation, and budget-throttle logic are pure functions with direct unit tests (the `pr_should_fetch`/`next_pr_poll_delay` precedent); no new trust boundary and no CLI-surface change. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Worktree removal is serialized across concurrent `close` executes** ([#1359](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1359)): the VS Code companion's multi-select close now fans out into one `close` op per target rather than issuing them one at a time, because a close is almost entirely a *wait* on the target window's next heartbeat (~10s) and waits on independent windows overlap — so N closes cost one shared interval instead of N stacked ones. That makes it possible for two executes to reach the `git2` worktree prune at once, which the sequential caller had implicitly prevented. `WorktreesService` now holds a `tokio::sync::Mutex` across `remove_worktree`, taken **after** the heartbeat wait so the waits still overlap and only the prune itself serializes — keeping the fan-out safe at the source rather than relying on every caller to stay sequential. The lock is precautionary rather than a fix for an observed corruption: each op enumerates the repo's worktrees and then prunes an entry out of that same `.git/worktrees`, so concurrent ops read a directory a sibling is midway through removing from and `git2` promises nothing about that — but the window is narrow enough that it has not been reproduced, and serializing a directory delete behind a multi-second wait costs nothing measurable. Scope is deliberately global (one lock, all repos) rather than per-repo; concurrent recursive deletes on one disk do not parallelize well anyway. No wire-protocol or CLI-surface change. See [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **ADF schema snapshot bumped to `@atlaskit/adf-schema` 56.1.3** ([#1325](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1325)): Refreshes the pinned upstream snapshot from `56.1.1` to `56.1.3` after the weekly `adf-schema-drift` job detected an upstream release. This is a **version bump only** — and another inert one: the upstream `dist/json-schema/v1/full.json` is **byte-for-byte identical** across these releases (same `full_json_sha256` `75f0809…`), so the vendored `assets/adf-schema/full.json` is unchanged and the regenerated `generated.rs` carries the same `UPSTREAM_ENTRIES` and `UPSTREAM_FULL_JSON_SHA256` as before. The only changes are the provenance constants that pin *which release* the snapshot was taken from: `SCHEMA_VERSION` and `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `src/atlassian/adf_schema.rs`, the mirrored `UPSTREAM_VERSION` / `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `generated.rs`, `assets/adf-schema/provenance.json`, and the JFM spec + README version references. No validation behaviour changes. The upstream tarball SHA-256 (`a40d1c99…`) was verified independently against the npm registry.

### Fixed
- **PR-less branches no longer keep every VS Code window polling `gh pr list` forever** ([#1370](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1370)): the daemon's PR-badge resolver reported only branches *with* an open PR, so a checked branch with none was indistinguishable on the tree snapshot from one an old daemon never checked — and the companion extension's degraded fallback re-fired per window × repo × every 60s indefinitely for every PR-less branch (measured: ~11,000 GraphQL points/hour against the 5,000/hour budget with 41 windows over 39 repos, rate-limiting `gh` for everything sharing the token — silently reintroducing the very cost [#1337](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1337) moved resolution daemon-side to eliminate). The resolver now returns a tri-state `PrResolution` per target and the tree worktree carries an additive `pr_none: true` for every successfully checked branch with no open PR (a never-pushed branch included) — mutually exclusive with `pr`, with *both absent* still meaning "not resolved", so an older extension stays byte-identical per [ADR-0053](docs/adrs/adr-0053.md)'s forward-compat contract and a newer one runs **zero** `gh` against a current daemon. A **failed** poll keeps the last good state — badges *and* negatives — and can never manufacture false negatives (a hard error on GraphQL `errors`, and malformed reply nodes stay *unresolved* rather than negative); the change-notify still bumps only when a resolution actually moves, and a negative never holds the poller's fast cadence. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Shim-exec tests no longer flake with `ETXTBSY` under high parallelism** ([#1348](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1348)): tests that write an executable shim and then `execve` it (the `fake-gh` stubs in `src/pr_status.rs`, and the `claude-cli` version-probe shims in `src/utils/preflight.rs`) could still fail with `Text file busy` (`os error 26`) — it fired on the v0.36.0 release CI and, with `fail-fast` on the Test matrix, turned a two-test flake into a wholly-red release run. The window is a `fork()` from any sibling test landing between our open-for-write of the shim and our `execve` of it: the child inherits the still-open writable FD (`O_CLOEXEC` closes only on `exec`, not on a bare `fork`), and the kernel refuses our exec until the child execs. The real fix is to **retry the exec on `ETXTBSY`** — an artifact of writing the very binary we then run, which never happens to a real `gh`/`claude`, so production keeps failing loudly on it. A new test-only `test_support::shim::retry_on_etxtbsy` helper wraps the `pr_status`/`preflight` execs (the `worktrees` badge poller already retries on its own tick, and the `claude-cli` backend already retries at its spawn boundary via `spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry`). The crate-wide `SHIM_LOCK` is **kept but honestly re-documented**: it never closed the `ETXTBSY` race (the offending `fork()` comes from tests that never take it), but it *does* bound how many shim subprocesses run at once — without it, high parallelism on a many-core host starves timing-sensitive subprocess tests (a freshly-spawned shim scheduled too late to write its state before a short run timeout reaps it). As a small production diagnostic improvement, the `claude-cli` preflight probe now preserves the spawn error as its error-chain source rather than flattening every failure into a fixed message. The Test matrix also gains `fail-fast: false` so one flaky leg no longer cancels the other five. See [#642](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/642).

### Documentation
- **Windows daemon port design — ADR-0054 and the executable plan** ([#1363](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1363)): commits the docs-first design for porting the daemon to Windows, so the implementation can proceed as independently reviewable PRs against an agreed record. [ADR-0054](docs/adrs/adr-0054.md) (Accepted) records the decisions: transport is a per-user named pipe (`\\.\pipe\omni-dev.daemon.<hash16>`, derived from the existing socket path as a cross-platform endpoint key) behind a new `src/daemon/transport.rs` seam — loopback TCP + bearer token, AF_UNIX-on-Windows, and the `interprocess` crate are rejected in Context; single-instance is exclusive first-instance pipe creation (no stale-reclaim — pipes die with their process); activation is detached spawn only (no Task Scheduler, no Windows Service); phase 1 ships on the default DACL with `reject_remote_clients`, with explicit-DACL hardening tracked as a follow-up; and paths get a single `compare_key`/`display_path` policy where `fsPath`, `git2`, and Claude-hook cwds meet. [docs/plan/windows-daemon-port.md](docs/plan/windows-daemon-port.md) carries the executable detail: the Unix-coupling inventory, exact signature changes, the pipe-name spec with a pinned cross-language test vector, companion changes, and a 7-PR roadmap. Existing Windows-port references were repointed from the retired [#1237](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1237) to #1363.
- **Bedrock model discovery guide** ([#1343](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1343)): expands [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md) with a "Discovering available models" section — the least-privilege IAM policy for model enumeration, copy-paste `aws` CLI recipes for listing models, filtering by provider, querying inference profiles, and checking account access, and the three gotchas that produce `AccessDeniedException`/model-not-found confusion (most `modelId`s require `us.*`/`global.*` inference-profile IDs; filtering by `ON_DEMAND` excludes current models; `list-foundation-models` does not report account entitlements). Also clarifies that the static model registry may differ from live regional Bedrock availability and how to verify what your account can actually invoke.
- **README demo image fixed**: the asciicast image reference used `.svg` but the published asset is a `.png`, so the demo rendered as a broken image on crates.io and GitHub.

### CI/CD
- **Dependency bumps**: `actions/setup-node` 6 → 7 ([#1367](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/pull/1367)), `EmbarkStudios/cargo-deny-action` 2.0.20 → 2.1.1 ([#1366](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/pull/1366)), and the `rust-minor-patch` cargo group ([#1368](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/pull/1368)) — routine patch/minor updates (`tokio` 1.53.0, `serde` 1.0.229, `clap` 4.6.2, `anyhow` 1.0.104, `regex` 1.13.1, `syn` 3.0.1, among others). Manifest + lockfile bumps; no source or behaviour changes.

## [0.36.0] - 2026-07-16

### Added
- **Claude Code sessions tracker — the daemon's fourth service** ([#1210](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1210)): no single vantage point can see the Claude Code sessions running across a machine — a hook knows only its own process, a VS Code extension host is sandboxed per window, and the transcript files carry no live state on their own — so there was no way to answer "what are all my Claude sessions doing right now?". A new `crate::sessions` engine (`SessionsRegistry`, mirroring the worktrees engine/adapter split) tracks every session across every terminal and VS Code window, keyed by Claude `session_id`, with a coarse state: `starting`, `working`, `idle`, `waiting_for_permission`, `waiting_for_input`, `ended`. It is fed by **three independent, gracefully-degrading feeds**: (1) Claude Code hooks, via a new `omni-dev sessions hook` sink; (2) an engine-owned **transcript watcher** that scans `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` every 5s for new or growing files — parsing **only presence and size/mtime**, never the version-unstable line schema — which discovers sessions that predate the daemon and covers the hook-silent thinking window; and (3) the VS Code companion, reporting each window's Claude tab/terminal **counts** (never a tab's `session_id` — the Claude extension exposes no API for it), joined to sessions by a `cwd`-prefix match so a session's `source` is tagged `terminal` or `vscode`. New CLI surface (`#[cfg(unix)]`): **`omni-dev sessions {list,hook,install-hooks,uninstall-hooks}`** — `list` is a read-only socket client; `install-hooks`/`uninstall-hooks` idempotently merge or remove a 7-event hook block in `~/.claude/settings.json`, preserving any other hooks you have configured. The `hook` sink is **infallible by design**: it swallows every error and always exits 0, so a broken or absent daemon can never block a Claude turn. A "Claude Sessions" tray submenu adds a `focus:<session_id>` action for VS Code sessions. Reliability is honest about its limits — `waiting_for_*` is exact (a direct `Notification`), while `working`/`idle` is best-effort inference, since Claude ships no state event (upstream [anthropics/claude-code#43058](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/43058) was closed *not planned*); an idle session ages out on a generous 5-minute TTL and re-appears on its next activity. No new trust boundary — ops ride the daemon's existing `0600` Unix socket and **nothing is persisted** (in-memory only); the hooks are opt-in user config. See [docs/sessions-service.md](docs/sessions-service.md) and [ADR-0052](docs/adrs/adr-0052.md).
- **Worktree PR check badges resolved in the daemon and fanned out to every window** ([#1337](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1337)): PR badges were resolved **extension-side** on repo-expand ([ADR-0050](docs/adrs/adr-0050.md), [#1296](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1296) — landed earlier in this same release window, never in a published build). Because the streamed snapshot carries worktree *topology* and not CI, nothing ever re-asked GitHub: while CI ran and no window opened or closed, a badge stayed wrong indefinitely — including a confident green `✓` over a PR whose CI was still running — and every window resolved its own, so cost scaled with window count. A new `src/pr_status.rs` engine (query, `gh` invocation, reducer, cache) plus a background poller on the worktrees service now resolve every `(repo, branch)` pair the tree knows about in **one `gh api graphql` call**, folded onto the tree snapshot so the existing `subscribe` stream fans live CI state to every open window, focused or not. The query aliases `repository()`/`ref()` — neither a GraphQL connection — so it costs **1 point** regardless of how many repos, worktrees, or windows are open (measured 1 up to ~50 branches). Open PRs are read off the `Ref` filtered to `OPEN`, **not** off the `Commit`: on a Commit they resolve to whatever PR introduced it, which on `main` is an unrelated merged PR and would paint a false badge. Cadence adapts — ~10s while a verdict is pending, doubling to a 30-minute ceiling once terminal, and nothing at all while no window is registered — overridable via the new **`OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_PR_POLL`** env knob (whole seconds). Three correctness fixes ride the same issue: a verdict now records the commit it describes and renders **pending** on a mismatch against the worktree's HEAD, so a push no longer leaves the previous head's verdict standing (backed by a new `head_sha` on the snapshot); a PR whose check **suite** is still creating jobs is treated as pending, killing a false tick seen live where a rollup carried 19 green contexts while the suite was still queued; and the poller wakes every base interval on a cheap cache read and re-asks GitHub when the watched state moved, so a commit triggers a fetch on the next wake rather than waiting out a 30-minute backoff. **No credential enters the daemon** — resolution shells out to `gh` per [ADR-0003](docs/adrs/adr-0003.md); what is conceded is narrower: PR data now crosses the `0600` same-user socket. [ADR-0053](docs/adrs/adr-0053.md) records this as a deliberate **reversal of ADR-0050**, which is now marked Superseded. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **The macOS daemon now keeps a durable log, and `daemon run` defaults to info-level logging** ([#1316](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1316)): on the default macOS install a launchd-spawned `omni-dev daemon` sent stdout/stderr to `/dev/null` — the LaunchAgent plist declared no `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` — so every lifecycle line was discarded and a crash or restart left no history to inspect. Both streams are now pointed at the `daemon.log` co-located with the socket, the same sink the off-macOS detached-spawn launcher already appended to, bringing macOS to parity (a systemd user unit still logs to the journal instead — `journalctl --user`); launchd creates the file under its own umask, so the daemon tightens it to `0600` on startup to match the socket/token posture. That alone would still have logged nothing useful: the daemon's lifecycle events (start, stop, the triggering signal) are emitted at **info**, but the tracing subscriber defaulted to `warn` when `RUST_LOG` was unset, filtering them out before they reached *any* sink. The command path is now resolved before tracing init so the long-lived `daemon run` process defaults to an **info** base filter, while short-lived CLI invocations keep the quieter `warn` default; `RUST_LOG` still overrides either. Together these make `omni-dev daemon logs` (see [#1113](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1113) above) actually have something to read. See [ADR-0039](docs/adrs/adr-0039.md).
- **MCP server configuration in `settings.json`** ([#620](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/620)): the `omni-dev-mcp` server now reads an optional `mcp` section from `~/.omni-dev/settings.json` so operators can set defaults once instead of relying on env vars or per-invocation flags. Three fields, all optional: `default_model` supplies the model for the `ai_chat` tool when its own `model` parameter is absent (the parameter still wins; falls back to the model registry default); `log_level` seeds the tracing filter directive (env `RUST_LOG` still wins when set, otherwise this value, otherwise `warn`); and `max_response_bytes` overrides the tool-response truncation cap (default 100 KB, `0` disables truncation). An absent `mcp` block preserves the previous behaviour byte-for-byte. See the [MCP configuration reference](docs/mcp.md#configuration-settingsjson).
- **Daemon operability: log access, per-service CLI control, and a client↔daemon version handshake** ([#1113](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1113)): the daemon was hard to run and recover **off the macOS tray** — the exact situation on Linux/headless. Four gaps close. **Logs**: a new **`omni-dev daemon logs [-n N] [-f]`** reads and tails the daemon's `daemon.log` — the `0600` file the launchd LaunchAgent (`StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath`) and the off-macOS detached-spawn launcher sink the daemon's stdout/stderr to ([#1316](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1316)) — printing a trailing window (default 200 lines) or following appends; it reads the file directly, so it works whether or not a daemon is resident (under a systemd user unit the daemon logs to the journal instead, `journalctl --user`). **Per-service control**: the browser-bridge ops that were reachable only from the tray are now CLI-reachable — a typed **`omni-dev daemon bridge <status|restart|disconnect-tab <ID>|snippet|token|request-command>`** and a generic **`omni-dev daemon service <SERVICE> <OP> [--payload <JSON>]`** passthrough to any service's ops — so bouncing the whole daemon is no longer the only recovery on Linux. **Version handshake**: the built-in `ping`/`status` ops now carry the daemon binary's version (additive, wire-compatible — an older daemon reports none and is treated as "unknown"), and `omni-dev daemon status` shows the resident daemon's version and prints a non-fatal warning when it differs from the CLI you ran, so a stale daemon after a binary upgrade is detected rather than silently driven. **`worktrees focus`**: **`omni-dev worktrees focus <PATH>`** raises a worktree's VS Code window from the CLI (reusing the daemon's existing `open` op and launcher resolution), making the previously tray-only focus action reachable on Linux/headless. Service enable/disable is deferred to a follow-up ([#1318](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1318)); Windows daemon support remains tracked by [#1237](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1237). See [docs/browser-bridge.md](docs/browser-bridge.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **AI backend parity: structured JSON-schema output on the Anthropic and Bedrock backends, non-Anthropic `--beta-header` warn-and-ignore, and a tunable HTTP timeout** ([#1119](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1119)): three backend-parity gaps from the [#1103](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1103) analysis closed. (1) **Structured output** — the direct Anthropic ([`src/claude/ai/claude.rs`](src/claude/ai/claude.rs)) and Bedrock ([`src/claude/ai/bedrock.rs`](src/claude/ai/bedrock.rs)) backends inherited the all-false capability baseline and fell back to the weaker YAML response path, even though OpenAI/Ollama and `claude-cli` already advertised schema support and the Anthropic Messages API supports it natively. Both now request the GA Messages-API `output_config.format` for structured calls — but **model-gated** by a new per-model `supports_structured_output` flag on `ModelSpec` / [`src/templates/models.yaml`](src/templates/models.yaml) (`ModelRegistry::supports_structured_output`), since `output_config` `400`s on older models. Recent models that support it (the default `claude-sonnet-4-6`, plus `opus-4-6`, `sonnet-4-5`, `haiku-4-5`, `opus-4-5`) take the schema path; older/unknown models transparently keep the YAML path. The returned JSON object flows through the unchanged text-extraction path (JSON is valid YAML), and the schema capability drives the same `output_config`-aware system-prompt override the OpenAI path already uses. (2) **`--beta-header`** — beta headers are Anthropic-specific, but the OpenAI/Ollama backends stored the flag without ever transmitting it *and* validated it against the Claude registry, so it could hard-fail (`Model 'X' does not support any beta headers`) for a header that would never be sent. They now warn-and-ignore it (`client.rs::warn_beta_header_ignored`), matching the `claude-cli` backend; validation stays only on the Anthropic backends (direct API + Bedrock) that actually send it — one consistent behaviour for the flag across every non-Anthropic backend. (3) **HTTP timeout** — the HTTP backends' request timeout was a fixed 300s while only the subprocess backend was tunable (`OMNI_DEV_CLAUDE_CLI_TIMEOUT_SECS`); a new `OMNI_DEV_AI_TIMEOUT_SECS` overrides it in whole seconds (a zero or non-numeric value falls back to the default). No CLI-surface change. See [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md).
- **Snowflake query cancel/abort** ([#1225](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1225)): a runaway query held its pooled session hostage up to `SNOWFLAKE_QUERY_TIMEOUT` (default 3600s), and `snowflake sessions` showed the running SQL but offered no way to stop it — the clean-room client had no `queries/v1/abort-request` call at all. A new **`omni-dev snowflake cancel`** subcommand and daemon **`cancel`** op abort the running query **without** evicting the session, so the pooled session is freed promptly (its poll loop returns a cancelled error within one poll interval) instead of waiting out the timeout. Because a query's session is checked out of the pool — and thus unreachable through the pool slot — while it runs, each session now publishes its in-flight `(requestId, sql, session-token)` for the duration of the statement, and the engine captures a cheap `AbortHandle` onto the pool slot at query start; a concurrent `cancel` reads that handle and posts `queries/v1/abort-request` (a fresh `requestId`, the query's original `requestId` in the body, authorized by the running session's own token). Selectors mirror `disconnect` — the `--account`/`--user` pair, `--id` (a numeric pool id from `sessions`), or `--all` — plus an optional `--member <id>` (a member id from `sessions`) that narrows the pair/id forms to one authenticated session's query instead of every busy member in the pool (`--member` is not valid with `--all`). The reply reports how many running statements an abort was issued for and is a no-op when nothing is running. No secret is persisted. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md).
- **Snowflake multi-statement query support** ([#1226](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1226)): `query()` submitted `{"sqlText": sql}` with no `MULTI_STATEMENT_COUNT`, so on the v1 REST protocol (default count `1`) a semicolon-separated script — the common `USE WAREHOUSE …; SELECT …` shape — errored or truncated server-side. The engine now detects a multi-statement submission (a comment-, string-, quoted-identifier- and `$$`-block-aware statement scan that ignores `;` inside those) and submits it with `MULTI_STATEMENT_COUNT = 0` ("any count", so the routing hint need not match the server's parse exactly); the response then carries a comma-separated list of child `resultIds` in place of inline rows, and each child result set is fetched from the `/queries/{id}/result` endpoint (with the same async-poll and external-chunk handling as a single query). Single-statement queries keep the original, cheaper path (no `MULTI_STATEMENT_COUNT`, no child round trips). The whole script is submitted under one request id, so [#1225](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1225)'s `cancel` aborts it as it would a single statement. The `query` reply payload is now uniformly `{ statements: [ { columns, rows }, … ] }` — **one entry per statement**, so a single-statement query is a one-element `statements` array (previously the bare `{ columns, rows }`). `-o json`/`-o yaml` serialize the whole payload; `-o csv`/`-o tsv` render a single table and **error** on a multi-statement result, since a flat delimited format has no multi-table shape. The `snowflake_query` MCP tool and the daemon `query` op reply carry the same `statements` shape. No CLI-surface (flag/subcommand) change. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#multi-statement-scripts).
- **`jira component` CRUD and `confluence restriction` management** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): there was no project-level component management (components could only be set as an issue field) and no Confluence page-restriction management. A new **`jira component`** subcommand adds `list`/`create`/`update`/`delete` over `GET /rest/api/3/project/{key}/components`, `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE /rest/api/3/component/{id}` (delete behind the standard `--force`/`--dry-run` flow, with optional `--move-issues-to`). A new **`confluence restriction`** subcommand adds `get` (read the read/update restrictions), `grant`, and `revoke` (add/remove a `--account-id` user or `--group` for a `--operation read|update`) over `GET /wiki/rest/api/content/{id}/restriction` and the `.../restriction/byOperation/{op}/{user|group}` endpoints. Matching MCP tools are added: **`jira_component_{list,create,update,delete}`** (delete requires `confirm: true`) and **`confluence_restriction_{get,grant,revoke}`**. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`confluence watcher` — manage Confluence page watchers** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): JIRA had watcher add/list/remove but Confluence had no watcher management at all. A new `confluence watcher` subcommand adds `status`, `add`, and `remove` (each targeting a page and an optional `--account-id`, defaulting to the authenticated user), backed by `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /wiki/rest/api/user/watch/content/{id}`. It exposes `status` rather than `list` because Confluence's public API exposes per-user watch state, not an enumerable watcher list. Matching MCP tools **`confluence_watcher_status`**, **`confluence_watcher_add`**, and **`confluence_watcher_remove`** are added. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **`confluence copy` — copy a Confluence page** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): there was no way to duplicate a Confluence page — `create` starts from a body, and `move` relocates the original. `omni-dev atlassian confluence copy <ID> --parent <PARENT_ID> --title <TITLE>` issues `POST /wiki/rest/api/content/{id}/copy` (v1 single-page copy) carrying the source page's attachments, labels, and properties (but not its restrictions), and returns the new page id. A matching **`confluence_copy`** MCP tool is added. (Hierarchy copy — an async long-running task — is out of scope.) See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **`confluence attachment update` — upload a new attachment version** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): Confluence attachments supported upload/list/download/delete but not replacing an existing attachment's binary, so refreshing a file meant deleting and re-uploading it (losing its id and version history). `omni-dev atlassian confluence attachment update <PAGE_ID> <ATTACHMENT_ID> <FILE>` streams the file to the per-attachment `POST /wiki/rest/api/content/{id}/child/attachment/{attachmentId}/data` endpoint (v1, like attachment creation), bumping the attachment's version. Reuses the streaming multipart machinery of `attachment upload` (`--filename` / `--comment` / `--minor-edit`). A matching **`confluence_attachment_update`** MCP tool is added. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **`jira label add` / `jira label remove` — incremental issue labels** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): the only way to change an issue's labels was `jira write --set labels=…` / `jira edit`, both of which send the **whole array** and so silently drop any label not restated — there was no add-one / remove-one operation. A new `jira label` subcommand adds `add <KEY> --labels a,b` and `remove <KEY> --labels x,y`, backed by a new client method that uses JIRA's `update` verb (`PUT /rest/api/3/issue/{key}` with `{"update": {"labels": [{"add": …}, {"remove": …}]}}`) so labels not mentioned are preserved. Matching MCP tools **`jira_label_add`** and **`jira_label_remove`** are added. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`jira sprint delete`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): sprints had create/update but no delete, so a sprint created by mistake could not be removed from the CLI. `omni-dev atlassian jira sprint delete --sprint-id <ID>` issues `DELETE /rest/agile/1.0/sprint/{id}` behind the standard `--force` / `--dry-run` confirmation flow, with a matching **`jira_sprint_delete`** MCP tool (requiring `confirm: true`). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **Confluence comment `edit` / `delete` / `resolve` / `reopen`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): Confluence comments were add-only — the v2 API supports editing, deleting, and resolving/reopening inline comments, but none were exposed, so the missing resolve verb also compounded the inline-comment drift workflow (#1092). `omni-dev atlassian confluence comment edit <ID> --kind footer|inline <file>` fetches the comment's current version and issues `PUT /wiki/api/v2/{footer|inline}-comments/{id}` with `version + 1` and the new ADF body; `comment delete <ID> --kind …` issues the matching `DELETE` behind the standard `--force` / `--dry-run` confirmation flow; and `comment resolve <ID>` / `comment reopen <ID>` toggle an inline comment's resolution by re-sending its current body with the `resolved` flag set (the v2 update contract requires `version` and `body`, so both are fetched and re-sent unchanged). Matching MCP tools **`confluence_comment_edit`**, **`confluence_comment_delete`** (requiring `confirm: true`), **`confluence_comment_resolve`**, and **`confluence_comment_reopen`** are added. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`jira link remote create` / `jira link remote delete`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): remote (external-URL) issue links were read-only — `jira link remote` had only `list` — so links to Confluence pages, PRs, or external trackers could be read but never added or removed from the CLI. `jira link remote create <KEY> --url … --title …` issues `POST /rest/api/3/issue/{key}/remotelink` (with optional `--summary` / `--relationship` / `--global-id`, where reusing a global id updates the link rather than duplicating it) and returns the new link id; `jira link remote delete <KEY> --link-id …` issues `DELETE …/remotelink/{id}` behind the standard `--force` / `--dry-run` confirmation flow. Matching MCP tools **`jira_link_remote_create`** and **`jira_link_remote_delete`** are added (both supporting `dry_run`; delete also requires `confirm: true`). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`jira version release` / `archive` / `rename` / `delete`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): project versions supported only list and create, so a version could be created but never released, archived, renamed, or removed without the JIRA web UI (sprints, by contrast, already had `update`). Four new subcommands back onto a shared `PUT /rest/api/3/version/{id}` (release/archive/rename — each sending only the fields it changes, with dates validated client-side) and `DELETE /rest/api/3/version/{id}` (delete, with optional `--move-fix-issues-to` / `--move-affected-issues-to` to reassign affected issues first, behind the standard `--force` / `--dry-run` confirmation flow). Matching MCP tools **`jira_version_release`**, **`jira_version_archive`**, **`jira_version_rename`**, and **`jira_version_delete`** (the last requiring `confirm: true`) are added, and the pre-existing but previously-undocumented `jira_version_list` / `jira_version_create` tools are now covered in the MCP tool table. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`jira worklog edit` / `jira worklog delete`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): worklogs supported list and add but not edit or delete, so a mistyped duration or an entry logged against the wrong issue could not be corrected without the JIRA web UI. `omni-dev atlassian jira worklog edit <KEY> <WORKLOG_ID>` issues `PUT /rest/api/3/issue/{key}/worklog/{id}` with any subset of `--time-spent` / `--started` / `--comment` (omitted fields keep their current value; supplying none is an error), and `jira worklog delete <KEY> <WORKLOG_ID>` issues `DELETE …/worklog/{id}` behind the standard confirmation flow (`--force`, `--dry-run`). Matching MCP tools **`jira_worklog_update`** and **`jira_worklog_delete`** (the latter requiring `confirm: true`) are added. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`jira comment delete`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): JIRA comments supported add and edit but not delete — the only way to remove a stray or mistaken comment was the JIRA web UI. `omni-dev atlassian jira comment delete <KEY> <COMMENT_ID>` now issues `DELETE /rest/api/3/issue/{key}/comment/{id}`, guarded by the standard destructive-confirmation flow (`--force` to skip the prompt, `--dry-run` to preview without any API call). The MCP surface gains a matching **`jira_comment_delete`** tool that refuses unless `confirm: true` is passed (mirroring `jira_delete`). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`atlassian auth logout`** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): `atlassian auth` exposed only `{login, status}` while `datadog auth` already had `{login, logout, status}`, so removing stored Atlassian credentials meant hand-editing `~/.omni-dev/settings.json`. A new `omni-dev atlassian auth logout` removes the `ATLASSIAN_INSTANCE_URL`, `ATLASSIAN_EMAIL`, and `ATLASSIAN_API_TOKEN` keys from the active profile's `env` map (the base `env` when no profile is selected — issue #1116), leaving every other setting intact, and reports whether anything was removed. Mirrors the datadog logout exactly (the generic `Settings::remove_env_vars_in` primitive already existed) and is idempotent when no credentials are configured. Like `login`/`logout` on both providers it is CLI-only — no MCP tool, since credential mutation is a local-machine interactive operation (only the read-only `status` is on the MCP surface). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).

### Changed
- **BREAKING — the Snowflake `query` reply is now one result set per statement** ([#1226](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1226)): the daemon `query` op and the `snowflake_query` MCP tool now reply with `{ statements: [ { columns, rows }, … ] }` — **one entry per statement** — instead of a bare `{ columns, rows }`. A single-statement query is therefore a one-element `statements` array, and downstream callers must read `statements[0]` where they previously read the payload directly. This falls out of the multi-statement support described under Added above: a semicolon-separated script now runs as one submission and every statement's result set is returned in order, which the old single-table shape could not express. `-o json` / `-o yaml` serialize the whole payload; `-o csv` / `-o tsv` render a single table and **error** on a multi-statement result, since a flat delimited format has no multi-table shape. No CLI-surface (flag/subcommand) change. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#multi-statement-scripts).
- **Model registry refreshed to the current Claude lineup; `claude` default is now `claude-sonnet-5`** ([#1334](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1334)): [`src/templates/models.yaml`](src/templates/models.yaml) had drifted — its newest entries were `claude-opus-4-6`/`claude-sonnet-4-6`, four current models were absent, and several present entries carried stale limits. The drift was invisible in normal use because `--model`/`OMNI_DEV_MODEL` is passed straight to the API without a registry check, so an unknown identifier surfaced only as a 404 (see [#1333](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1333)) — but the registry is not decorative: it gates structured output (`ModelRegistry::supports_structured_output` → the Messages-API `output_config.format` path) and sizes requests, so a missing or stale entry silently degraded both. **Added** `claude-fable-5`, `claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-opus-4-7`, and `claude-sonnet-5` (all 1M context / 128K output / structured-output capable); Sonnet 5 records the **sticker** rate ($3/$15 per MTok) because the schema cannot express its time-bounded introductory price ($2/$10 through 2026-08-31). **Corrected** the 4.6 entries, which described 1M context as a `context-1m-2025-08-07` beta-header upgrade over a 200K base — the current generation exposes 1M **natively** at standard pricing, so `input_context` is now `1000000` outright and Sonnet 4.6's output cap is corrected 64000 → 128000. The 4.6 `beta_headers` block is deliberately **retained** (now a no-op for limits): `validate_beta_header` hard-errors on any header absent from the registry, so removing it would newly reject `--beta-header anthropic-beta:context-1m-2025-08-07` from callers who still pass it. **Changed** the `claude` provider's `default_model` from `claude-sonnet-4-6` to `claude-sonnet-5` — the model every invocation gets without `--model`; Sonnet 5 is the direct successor to the previous default, so the cost profile is unchanged at sticker rates. The `backend.rs` / `claude_cli.rs` / `resolve.rs` model tables that live *outside* the registry were refreshed to match (`opus` → `claude-opus-4-8`, `sonnet` → `claude-sonnet-5`). **Added** an optional `retires: YYYY-MM-DD` field to `ModelSpec`, populated where a date is announced (Opus 4.1 → 2026-08-05; the retired 3.x entries carry past dates) — the boolean `legacy` could not distinguish "older but fine" from "stops working on a known date"; it is data only, surfaced verbatim by `omni-dev config models show`, with absence meaning "no published date" rather than "not retiring" (Opus 4 / Sonnet 4 are deprecated with retirement still TBD). Schema stays `version: "1"` — an optional field is backwards-compatible in both directions.
- **Worktrees daemon: relax and env-tune the tree/tray refresh intervals** ([#1305](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1305)): two periodic timers drive the worktrees service's `git2` enumeration — the push-`subscribe` re-sample (`STREAM_TICK`, 3 s) and the tray menu recompute (`MENU_REFRESH_INTERVAL`, 2 s) — both aggressive for a git-heavy full-tree walk over dozens of worktrees. With the per-window coalescing ([#1303](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1303)) and lazy tree ahead/behind ([#1306](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1306)) already landed, profiling a ~10-window daemon showed the residual idle CPU was **almost entirely the 2 s tray refresh** — an independent per-window walk that reads neither the coalescing cache nor the lazy path and still computes `ahead`/`behind` inline. Both defaults are raised to **10 s** and made overridable via new whole-seconds env knobs **`OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_STREAM_TICK`** and **`OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_MENU_REFRESH`** (blank/non-numeric/`0` → default; parsed by a shared helper), cutting the dominant enumeration rate ~5×. No user action gets slower: a window open/close or show-closed toggle still pushes promptly via the change-notify, and the tray menu still opens instantly from its cache — only *passively observed* on-disk git state (branch, `ahead`/`behind`, dirty status) surfaces within the interval rather than every 2–3 s, and a lower value restores tighter freshness. The coalescing snapshot cache stays sized to `STREAM_TICK` by reading the same resolver, so the two can never drift. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md#tuning-the-refresh-cadence).
- **`--instance` is now a global flag that overrides the Atlassian instance for every command** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): the `--instance` override existed only on `jira create`, so a multi-site user could target a specific tenant only when creating an issue — every other JIRA/Confluence command was locked to the configured `ATLASSIAN_INSTANCE_URL`. `--instance <URL>` is now a **global** flag (like `--model` / `--profile`): it is propagated to a new `OMNI_DEV_ATLASSIAN_INSTANCE` env var that `atlassian::auth::load_credentials` reads, so every JIRA and Confluence command honours it (email and API token still come from the environment/settings), and — being global — it can appear before or after the subcommand. The per-command `jira create --instance` copy is removed, but `omni-dev atlassian jira create --instance …` keeps working through the global flag, so the change is transparent for that path. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).

### Fixed
- **A worktree's ahead/behind counts now refresh after a push or fetch** ([#1344](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1344)): the tree's `↑ahead ↓behind` counts never updated once a worktree was pushed — a fully-pushed worktree kept reporting `↑1 ↓0` indefinitely, with no cadence and no TTL correcting it, and only collapsing and re-expanding the repo fixed the display. A push moves `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>`, **not** local HEAD, and the snapshot's worktree object carried nothing else a push touches (`path`, `branch`, `head_sha`, `is_main`, `open`, and `window_key` all serialise byte-identically across one), so the `subscribe` stream's delta check dropped the frame as unchanged, no window re-rendered, `getChildren` was never re-entered, and the counts that [#1306](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1306) had made lazy were never re-fetched. A fetch has the same shape, so a worktree falling *behind* likewise kept reporting `↓0`. The snapshot now carries the upstream ref's OID alongside `head_sha` — a config lookup plus a refs read, no revwalk, so it clears the same every-worktree-every-tick cost bar that made ahead/behind lazy in the first place — and a branch with no upstream omits the field via `skip_serializing_if`, keeping older clients byte-identical on the wire. The same signal is fed to the PR badge poller ([#1337](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1337)), which watched only `(target, head_sha)` and so could not see a push — the very event that *starts* the CI run a badge reports — leaving its badge on `●` until the backoff elapsed. Verified against a live daemon on an isolated socket. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Closing a worktree no longer fails with "Directory not empty" while a language server winds down** ([#1315](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1315)): the VS Code companion's **Close Worktree** action deleted the worktree with libgit2's combined `git_worktree_prune(working_tree: true)`, a bottom-up walk that `unlink`s each child then `rmdir`s the parent. A just-closed window's language server or build tool (Metals/Bloop, `rust-analyzer`/`cargo`) can still be flushing into `target/`/`.metals/` **after** the extension's `deactivate()` fires — VS Code tears the extension host down *before* child processes quiesce — so a file created between libgit2's enumeration and its final `rmdir` made the removal fail with `ENOTEMPTY` (`could not rmdir '…/target/': Directory not empty`), and the user had to retry manually.
