Worse, libgit2 deletes the **admin metadata first**, so the failed working-tree rmdir left a *half-removed orphan*: the directory on disk with a dangling `.git` gitlink, no longer tracked by git and unreachable from the tree view — and a naive prune-retry could not recover it, since its admin gitdir was already gone. `remove_worktree` ([src/daemon/services/worktrees.rs](src/daemon/services/worktrees.rs)) now deletes the checked-out directory **itself first** — retrying past the transient `ENOTEMPTY`/`EEXIST`/`EBUSY` race with a short backoff (~2.75s total across four retries) so the winding-down process has time to finish — and only then prunes the admin metadata, so a transient failure leaves the worktree fully tracked and cleanly retryable instead of orphaned. A pre-existing orphan from the old ordering is now detected and its leftover directory cleaned up directly. Non-transient errors (permission denied, a locked worktree — still refused up front) surface immediately with the original message. A test reproduces the race with a background thread that repopulates `target/` as the removal runs. No CLI-surface change. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Undated model aliases silently fell back to a 4096-token output cap** ([#1334](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1334)): Anthropic publishes an undated alias alongside each dated identifier (`claude-haiku-4-5` for `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) and documents the alias as the preferred form, but only the dated identifiers had registry entries. Identifier resolution is exact-match followed by a narrow normalization (region prefix, `anthropic.` prefix, `-v<N>:<M>` suffix) — none of which maps an alias onto its dated entry — so an alias **missed the registry entirely and inherited the `claude` provider's fallback limits**: 4096 max output tokens instead of Haiku 4.5's real 64000, and no structured-output support. This was reachable by default, not just via `--model`: `resolve_defaults` in [`src/cli/ai/claude/cli/model/resolve.rs`](src/cli/ai/claude/cli/model/resolve.rs) resolved the `haiku` alias to exactly `claude-haiku-4-5`, so the shipped default hit the miss. Alias entries are added for `claude-haiku-4-5`, `claude-sonnet-4-5`, and `claude-opus-4-5`, fixing every caller at once rather than patching the one call site; a regression test asserts each alias resolves to the same limits as its dated form.
- **`git branch create pr` no longer hides an AI failure behind the PR template or exits `0` on a permanent one** ([#1333](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1333)): the command caught **every** error from the AI call, logged it at `debug!` (invisible at default verbosity), and fell back to the raw PR template — the comment said `(silently)` outright. With `--auto-apply` against an existing PR, that template then **overwrote a real, populated description** while the command printed `✅ Pull request updated successfully!` and exited `0`, so a hard failure was indistinguishable from success and the loss was irreversible from the tool's side. A `--model claude-sonnet-4-8` typo (no such model) reproduced it: a 404 from the API, a clobbered PR body, exit `0`. Four changes, and the fallback is now only ever a *degradation of last resort for a genuinely retryable failure*. (1) **Nothing is silent** — a fallback emits a `WARN` plus a stderr notice naming the underlying error, and says the description is not AI-generated (`STYLE-0018`); the MCP path, which previously swallowed the error with no logging at all, shares the same helper (stderr only, since the MCP server owns stdout for JSON-RPC). (2) **Permanent failures fail** — all four HTTP backends funnelled non-2xx responses through one choke point that formatted the status into a string and discarded it, making a 404 indistinguishable from a 503. A new status-carrying `ClaudeError::ApiHttpError { status, body }` preserves it, and `ClaudeError::is_transient()` classifies: a non-retryable 4xx (400/401/403/404) can never succeed as-issued and now exits non-zero, while 408/429/5xx, network errors, timeouts, and **anything not positively identified as permanent** stay transient and keep the previous fall-back behaviour. The `claude-cli` backend's existing status mapping is aligned onto the same variant, so every backend classifies identically. (3) **A populated description is never clobbered** — on the non-interactive path (`--auto-apply`), falling back against an existing PR whose body is neither empty nor the unfilled template now refuses rather than overwriting; an interactive run is unaffected, since the user sees the content and explicitly chooses to update. (4) **Bad models are caught locally** — preflight validated credentials but not the model, reporting `✓ credentials verified (model: claude-sonnet-4-8)` for an impossible model, because `resolve_model` returns `String` and is infallible by construction. It now rejects an unknown model **before any network call**, listing the known identifiers; scope is deliberately the direct Anthropic and Bedrock backends only, since Ollama has no registry entries, OpenAI accepts unknown-but-well-shaped ids by design, and `claude-cli` resolves its own aliases (`haiku`/`sonnet`/`opus`). Legacy models still resolve but are not suggested, and a model newer than this build's catalog can be added to `~/.omni-dev/models.yaml`. Also fixed: the amendment and check retry loops (`git twiddle`, `git check`) retried *any* error, so a bad model name was retried three times with `warning: AI request failed, retrying...` before failing; they now break immediately on a permanent error. No CLI-surface change. See [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md).
- **Unsupported *inline* ADF nodes now round-trip losslessly** ([#1117](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1117)): block-level unknown ADF nodes already survived a JFM round trip as ` ```adf-unsupported ` fenced blocks carrying the full node JSON (ADR-0029), but an unknown *inline* node was emitted as a lossy `<!-- unsupported inline: {type} -->` HTML comment that preserved only the type string and had no reader — so a read → edit → write dropped the node's `attrs`/`content`/`marks`/`text` entirely *and* left the literal comment behind as plain text (strictly worse than lossless). Unknown inline nodes are now written as an `:adf-unsupported[]{json="…"}` directive carrying the complete node JSON (escaped via the standard `format_kv` quoting, so embedded braces/brackets/quotes don't break attribute parsing) and read straight back into the original `AdfNode` by `try_dispatch_inline_directive` — the inline mirror of the block fence. A malformed or missing payload degrades to plain text, exactly as the block reader degrades to a normal code block. Converter-only; no CLI or MCP surface change. See [ADR-0029](docs/adrs/adr-0029.md).

### Documentation
- **Workspace Trust workaround documented; ADR-0051 records why the launcher won't pre-trust** ([#1297](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1297)): daemon-opened worktree windows (the tray focus action, the tree view's double-click, and the `open` op — all `code <folder>`) start in VS Code **Restricted Mode** whenever the target is a folder VS Code has never seen, and the launcher cannot influence that — trust is decided by the workbench purely from the folder path against a per-user trusted-folders list, independent of *how* the folder is opened. [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md) gains a **Workspace Trust** section recommending the one-time, robust workaround (trust the worktree **parent** folder, e.g. `~/wrk/work-trees`, once — after which every subfolder window, including every daemon-spawned one, opens trusted), and [ADR-0051](docs/adrs/adr-0051.md) records the decision **not** to add any launcher-side trust bypass. The undocumented `code --disable-workspace-trust <folder>` flag is session-only and a no-op once a VS Code instance is already running (the daemon's normal state — `code <folder>` forwards the open to the resident process, which never received the switch; verified empirically on VS Code 1.128.0, where a fresh folder opened this way with other windows open still landed in Restricted Mode), so shipping it even behind an opt-in env would advertise a fix that does nothing; seeding VS Code's internal trust store (`state.vscdb` key `content.trust.model.key`) only sticks while the editor is fully quit and is version-fragile. Tracks upstream [microsoft/vscode#291933](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/issues/291933). Documentation only.

## [0.35.0] - 2026-07-13

### Added
- **Worktrees daemon backs the show/hide-closed toggle so it syncs across windows** ([#1301](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1301)): the VS Code companion's show/hide-closed toggle lived in per-window `context.globalState` — read once at activation with no cross-window change event — so a flip in one window never re-rendered another, and a newly-opened window could race the persisted value and fall back to the default. The toggle's source of truth moves into the daemon's `WorktreesRegistry` (a lock-free `AtomicBool`, default `show_closed = true`), so the existing push `subscribe` stream carries it to every window for reliable new-window initialization and live cross-window sync. A new `set-show-closed` op flips it (bumping the change-notify so subscribers re-push), and both the one-shot `tree` op and the `subscribe` stream now share one `tree_snapshot` helper returning `{ repos, show_closed }`, so a manual fetch and the live stream agree byte-for-byte. Like the rest of the registry it is in-memory: a daemon restart resets it to the default, which the next snapshot propagates to every window. Wire-compatible — the toggle rides the existing snapshot frame. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).

### Fixed
- **Worktrees daemon: coalesce the per-window tree computation into one shared build** ([#1303](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1303)): every open VS Code window holds a persistent `subscribe` stream, and the daemon re-sampled each one independently on its ~3 s tick and on every registry change — so with N windows the *identical* global worktree tree (a full `git2` enumeration: discover + HEAD + ahead/behind revwalk over every repo and worktree) was rebuilt N times per tick. With ~20 windows this burned ~1.6 cores sustained. The worktrees service now builds the snapshot through a shared, single-flight cache: it computes **once per tick (or change) and fans the byte-identical result out to every subscriber**, so daemon CPU scales with the worktree count rather than `windows × worktrees`. Freshness is unchanged — a `register`/`unregister`/`show_closed` flip bumps a change-generation that forces an immediate rebuild, and a short TTL (the stream tick) still surfaces on-disk branch/commit changes within one tick. The one-shot `tree` op (a manual refresh) deliberately bypasses the cache and computes fresh. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).

### CI/CD
- **Exclude VS Code extension tags from the crate release CI to fix a tag-push race** ([#1289](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1289)): the bare `v*` tag filter in `ci.yml` matched both crate release tags (`v1.0.0`) and extension tags (`vscode-v1.0.0`), so the crate's full CI suite ran on extension releases and its Coverage job 404'd fetching the not-yet-uploaded binary from the in-flight crate release. Adds a `!vscode-*` exclusion to the tag filter (mirroring the `release.yml` fix in [#1288](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1288)) and skips the Coverage job on tag pushes, since validation already occurred on `main` (Coverage is only meaningful for PRs and `main` pushes).

## [0.34.0] - 2026-07-11

### Added
- **`transition execute` can supply transition-screen fields, resolution, and comment** ([#1115](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1115)): the transition body was hardcoded to `{"transition":{"id":…}}`, so any workflow transition whose screen required input — the classic "Resolve" needing a `resolution`, or a mandatory comment/assignee on the transition screen — was rejected by JIRA or left the field unset, blocking a very common automation path (auto-resolving issues from CI/scripts). `omni-dev atlassian jira transition execute` now accepts **`--set-field "NAME=VALUE"`** (repeatable; same YAML-scalar semantics as `jira write --set-field`, resolved against the transition's own screen fields), **`--resolution NAME`** (sent as `{"name": …}`), and **`--comment`** (JFM markdown). The comment is delivered **in the transition itself** (`update.comment`, atomic) when the transition screen accepts a comment — the only way to satisfy a mandatory-comment screen — and otherwise falls back to a separate comment post, so screenless transitions are unaffected. Screen-field metadata is fetched via `expand=transitions.fields` and resolved through the same machinery `jira write` uses. The MCP **`jira_transition`** tool mirrors this with new `resolution` and `custom_fields` params, and its existing `comment` now uses the same screen-aware routing. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **Per-invocation USD cost on `AiClient` responses** ([#818](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/818)): AI calls reported latency but never a dollar cost, because the `AiClient` response surface exposed only a bare `String` — so downstream telemetry consumers (starting with `voice reflect`'s `reflections.log` in the separate `omni-voice` repo) had to record `cost_usd=unknown` even though the `claude-cli` backend already logged `total_cost_usd` and the direct Anthropic backend returns token usage. A new `AiClient::send_request_with_metrics` returns an `AiResponse { text, metrics: InvocationMetrics { cost_usd: Option<f64> } }`, decoupling per-call telemetry from the payload so more fields (token counts, cache hits) can be added later without touching every backend's return type. It is an additive **default** trait method that delegates to `send_request_with_options` and reports `cost_usd: None`, so `send_request`/`send_request_with_options` and the `bedrock`/`openai` backends are unchanged. The direct **Anthropic** backend ([`src/claude/ai/claude.rs`](src/claude/ai/claude.rs)) computes cost from the response's `usage.input_tokens`/`output_tokens` × a new per-model price table, and the **`claude-cli`** backend ([`src/claude/ai/claude_cli.rs`](src/claude/ai/claude_cli.rs)) surfaces the `total_cost_usd` it already parses from the subprocess envelope. The price table adds optional `input_token_price`/`output_token_price` (USD per million tokens) to `ModelSpec` and [`src/templates/models.yaml`](src/templates/models.yaml) for the Claude family; unpriced or unknown models (including the OpenAI/Gemini entries) fall back to `None` with a `tracing::warn!` so unpriced usage is noticed rather than silently zeroed. Per-backend cost for `bedrock`/`openai` and the `omni-voice` `reflections.log` wiring ([rust-works/omni-voice#11](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-voice/issues/11)) are follow-ups the trait surface now unblocks. See [ADR-0028](docs/adrs/adr-0028.md).
- **`SNOWFLAKE_BROWSER_COMMAND` to target a specific browser or Chrome profile for SSO** ([#1229](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1229)): the client already supported a custom external-browser launch command (`BrowserLaunch::Command` with `{url}` substitution), but nothing populated it — the engine always resolved external-browser SSO to `BrowserLaunch::Auto` (the OS default handler), so the SSO popup could land in the wrong browser or the wrong Chrome profile with no way to redirect it. A new `SNOWFLAKE_BROWSER_COMMAND` env/`settings.json` key, when set, supplies a single command line with a `{url}` placeholder (or the URL is appended as a trailing argument), threaded through `SnowflakeEngineConfig::from_env_and_settings` → `resolve_auth_method` into every pooled session. The value is split with a self-contained POSIX-style tokenizer (single quotes, double quotes, backslash escapes — no new crate dependency) so a program path or argument value may contain spaces (`Google Chrome.app`, `--profile-directory="Profile 1"`); a present-but-malformed value (unterminated quote, or one tokenizing to no words) is a hard error at daemon startup rather than a silent fall back to the default handler. Engine-global and read once at startup, consistent with the other config-only settings; unset or blank keeps `Auto`, so existing setups are byte-for-byte unchanged. Applies only to external-browser SSO — the non-interactive methods (PAT / key-pair JWT) open no browser and ignore it. No CLI-surface change. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#external-browser-sso-default).
- **"Close Worktree" / "Close Window" from the VS Code worktrees tree** ([#1277](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1277)): the worktrees tree view ([#1268](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1268), [ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md)) could open a worktree's window but never close one, so tidying up a finished worktree meant switching to a terminal for `git worktree remove`. A new **`close`** op on the `worktrees` daemon service — socket-only, symmetric to `open`, and the service's first **destructive** capability ([ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md)) — closes a worktree's VS Code window and, for a **linked** worktree, deletes it. Right-clicking a leaf offers **Close Worktree** (linked) or **Close Window** (the main working tree, never deleted). The op is two-phase: a side-effect-free **safety check** reports what a removal would lose — modified tracked files, untracked files (ignoring `.gitignore`d), an in-progress rebase/merge/cherry-pick, and commits reachable only from a detached HEAD — so the extension shows a modal confirm **only** when data is actually at risk (or a multi-root window's other folders would close); a clean worktree deletes with no prompt. Unpushed commits on a named branch never block: removal keeps the branch. Deletability keys **solely on the structural `is_main` fact**, never the branch name — a linked worktree checked out on the default branch is fully deletable and its branch survives — and the daemon refuses to remove the main working tree even if a malformed client asks (a `git2`-enforced guard, not just UI gating). Removal is `git2` prune (`valid`+`working_tree`, no shell-out — avoiding the launcher's `PATH` problem — and it refuses a locked worktree rather than forcing past the lock), and it is idempotent for an already-gone worktree. A worktree open in **another** window is closed cross-window by signalling that window through an additive `close` field on its next `heartbeat` reply (the only channel the daemon has to a window it can reply to but never call, riding the `known:false → re-register` precedent, wire-compatible), then waiting bounded for it to unregister before deleting. In the companion, `worktreeContextValue` now encodes `is_main` as `.main`/`.linked` to route the two menu commands, and the heartbeat handler closes this window on the `close` directive. Socket-only, so no CLI-surface or `insta` snapshot change. See [ADR-0049](docs/adrs/adr-0049.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Worktrees daemon service gains repo/worktree enumeration, socket-driven `open`, and push subscriptions** ([#1265](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1265), [#1266](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1266), [#1267](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1267)): the worktrees service ([ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md)) modelled only the *union of currently-open windows* via `register`/`heartbeat`/`unregister`, so it could neither surface a repo's closed/linked worktrees, launch an editor on demand for a socket client, nor push live changes — a tree UI could at best poll a flat open-window list. Three ops close the gap and lay the daemon-side foundation for the tree view ([ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md)). **`tree`** ([#1265](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1265)) derives the distinct repositories from open-window folders (each open folder → its git common dir → repo root) and enumerates **all** worktrees per repo — open or not — via `git2`, enriching each with branch/ahead/behind/`is_main` and GitHub identity from `origin`, then joining open windows back on by canonicalised path; all git I/O runs on a blocking thread, off the registry lock. It is also exposed as a read-only **`omni-dev worktrees tree [-o table|json]`** CLI subcommand (repo-grouped rows, `*` marks the main working tree). **`open`** ([#1266](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1266)) lets a socket client focus-or-open an arbitrary worktree folder in VS Code (`code <path>` uniformly focuses an already-open window or opens a new one), reusing the tray's launcher resolution and an absolute-existing-directory guard (which also blocks a `-`-leading path from being parsed as an argument-injection flag) — a deliberate, socket-ownership-and-`0600`-bounded escalation of the previously tray-only focus action. **`subscribe`** ([#1267](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1267)) is the control socket's first streaming path: a new `ServiceStream` trait plus an optional `DaemonService::subscribe`, driven by `run_stream()`, which sends an initial snapshot then re-samples on a `tokio::sync::watch` change-notify (bumped by `register`/`unregister`/reap **after** the registry lock is released) and a 3 s safety tick, pushing only frames that differ from the last sent (automatic deduplication). Fully backward-compatible — clients that never send `subscribe` keep the classic one-request-one-reply exchange. See [ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Live worktrees tree view in the VS Code companion** ([#1268](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1268)): the companion extension previously only *reported* its own window to the daemon and had no UI of its own. It now renders a dedicated activity-bar tree view of every repository and git worktree open across all VS Code windows, fed by the daemon's push `subscribe` stream ([ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md)) with exponential-backoff reconnection (`subscription.ts`), so the view stays live and self-healing across daemon restarts. A vscode-free tree model (`tree.ts`) and a thin `TreeDataProvider` adapter map daemon snapshots to icons/labels/tooltips; double-clicking a worktree opens it through the daemon's `open` op (manual double-click detection, since the TreeView API has no native double-click event), and a title-bar button forces a manual `tree` refresh. The extension is bumped 0.1.0 → 0.2.0. See [ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Current-window badge in the worktrees tree view** ([#1274](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1274)): the tree view marked a worktree as merely open-or-not, so with several concurrent windows a user could not tell at a glance which worktree they were *actively* working in. A three-way badge now distinguishes a **blue tick** (the worktree open in *this* window), a **green dot** (open in *another* window), and the branch glyph (no live window). The window's own `windowKey`, assigned at activation, is threaded into `WorktreesTreeDataProvider` and compared against each worktree's registry `window_key` — a pure client-side consumer of a field the `tree` payload already carries, so no daemon or protocol change is needed. `worktreeContextValue()` returns `worktree.current` for the current window's worktree to gate window-specific context-menu items. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Provider inference recognises OpenAI and Gemini model identifiers** ([#1119](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1119)): an unknown model from OpenAI or Gemini — one not yet in the embedded catalog — fell through `infer_provider()` to the conservative hard-coded universal fallback (4096/8192 tokens), discarding provider-specific capability. `infer_provider()` in [`src/claude/model_config.rs`](src/claude/model_config.rs) now detects OpenAI models via `gpt-`, `chatgpt-`, and the `o<N>`-reasoning shape (a dedicated `is_openai_reasoning_id()` helper matches `o1`/`o3`/`o4-mini`/… without false positives on other `o`-leading ids) and Gemini models via the `gemini-` prefix, so an unrecognised model resolves to its provider's defaults — OpenAI 16384/128000, Gemini 8192/1048576 — instead of the universal fallback. Purely additive: known models and Claude inference are unchanged.
- **Companion VS Code extension is now publishable to the VS Code Marketplace and Open VSX** ([#1279](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1279), [#1280](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1280), [#1282](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1282), [#1287](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1287)): the companion extension shipped in 0.33.0 with Marketplace/Open VSX publishing left as an explicit deferred follow-up ([#1111](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1111) — until now, install the CI-built `.vsix` by hand); that follow-up is closed. A dedicated `vscode-extension-release.yml` workflow, triggered **exclusively** by `vscode-v*` tags (never a routine `main` push), verifies the publisher secrets are present and that the pushed tag matches `package.json`, re-runs the full typecheck/build/test/package, and publishes the *same* `.vsix` bytes to both the VS Code Marketplace (`vsce`) and Open VSX (`ovsx`) ([#1279](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1279)). A 128×128 raster PNG gallery icon (`media/icon.png`, blue-to-indigo gradient, with an editable `media/icon.svg` source excluded from the package) satisfies the Marketplace's raster-only `"icon"` requirement ([#1280](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1280)). The extension gains its own Keep-a-Changelog `editors/vscode/CHANGELOG.md` — which the Marketplace and Open VSX render as the listing's Changelog tab — plus release docs, and versions **independently** of the crate (currently 0.2.0) ([#1282](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1282)). Release hardening then excludes `vscode-*` tags from the crate `release.yml` `v*` trigger (GitHub's `*` glob matched `vscode-v*`, spuriously firing a `cargo publish`) and makes Open VSX optional — only `VSCE_PAT` is required, and a missing `OVSX_PAT` skips that publish with a `::notice::` rather than failing the run ([#1287](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1287)). See [editors/vscode/CHANGELOG.md](editors/vscode/CHANGELOG.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).

### Changed
- **ADF schema snapshot bumped to `@atlaskit/adf-schema` 56.1.1** ([#1231](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1231)): Refreshes the pinned upstream snapshot from `56.0.9` to `56.1.1` after the weekly `adf-schema-drift` job detected an upstream release. This is a **version bump only** — and another inert one: the upstream `dist/json-schema/v1/full.json` is **byte-for-byte identical** across all of these releases (same `full_json_sha256`), so the vendored `assets/adf-schema/full.json` is unchanged and the regenerated `generated.rs` carries the same `UPSTREAM_ENTRIES` and `UPSTREAM_FULL_JSON_SHA256` as before. The only changes are the provenance constants that pin *which release* the snapshot was taken from: `SCHEMA_VERSION` and `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `src/atlassian/adf_schema.rs`, the mirrored `UPSTREAM_VERSION` / `UPSTREAM_TARBALL_SHA256` in `generated.rs`, `assets/adf-schema/provenance.json`, and the JFM spec + README version references. No validation behaviour changes. (The drift job flagged `56.0.15`; `56.1.1` was the current `latest` at refresh time and is also content-identical, so the snapshot is pinned to it to keep the drift job green.)

### Documentation
- **ADR-0048 records the worktrees tree view and its daemon push subscription** ([#1269](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1269)): adds [ADR-0048](docs/adrs/adr-0048.md), documenting the four coordinated design decisions behind the tree view — the `tree` op that inverts the open-window registry into a grouped repo/worktree view, the `open` focus/spawn op (with its absolute-existing-directory guard and threat model), the control socket's first streaming path (`subscribe` with a `tokio::sync::watch` change-notify + 3 s tick + diff-before-push coalescing), and the companion's activity-bar tree UI (three-way open-badge, double-click timer, exponential-backoff reconnect) — and substantially expands [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md) to match: the new ops in the wire-contract table, push-subscription framing/reconnect semantics, the three-way badge iconography, a revised data-flow diagram, and an expanded security section covering the `open` spawn capability and the `subscribe` stream. Documentation only.

## [0.33.0] - 2026-07-08

### Added
- **Companion VS Code extension for the worktrees service** ([#1111](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1111)): the daemon's worktrees service (engine, `omni-dev worktrees list`, tray submenu, git enrichment) shipped fully but had **no data producer** — the registry only fills via the `register`/`heartbeat`/`unregister` ops that, per [ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md), are driven exclusively by a "first-party companion VS Code extension (separate deliverable)" that did not exist, so the feature was inert end-to-end (`worktrees list` and the tray were always empty). That extension now ships in [`editors/vscode/`](editors/vscode/): a thin TypeScript per-window reporter that, over the daemon's `0600` Unix control socket (newline-delimited JSON), `register`s the window's open folders under a fresh per-`activate()` UUID, `heartbeat`s every ~10s (re-registering when a restarted in-memory daemon replies `known: false`), re-registers on workspace-folder changes, and `unregister`s on deactivation — and no-ops silently when the daemon is not running. It recomputes the daemon's socket path (`<data_dir>/omni-dev/daemon.sock`, matching the Rust `dirs` crate on macOS and Linux) and reports only raw folder paths, leaving branch/ahead-behind enrichment to the daemon (#1186) so the reporter stays thin. It is kept fully isolated from the Rust build: single-crate `cargo` never descends into `editors/`, which is added to `Cargo.toml`'s `exclude` so `cargo publish` never ships it, and a new path-filtered `vscode-extension.yml` workflow type-checks, unit-tests, builds (esbuild), and packages a `.vsix` artifact without touching any Rust job. Publishing to the VS Code Marketplace / Open VSX is a deferred follow-up (it needs a publisher account and CI secrets); until then, install the CI-built `.vsix` with `code --install-extension`. See [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md) and [ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md).
- **JIRA attachment upload and delete — the last read-only CRUD gap closed** ([#1114](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1114)): JIRA attachments were pull-only — the client had only `get_attachments` and the CLI/MCP surface exposed only `jira attachment download` / `images`, while Confluence already had the full upload/list/download/delete set. You could copy a file *off* an issue but never push a log/screenshot/artifact *onto* one, nor remove a stale attachment — a routine "attach evidence to a ticket" automation was impossible. Two verbs close it, reusing the existing multipart plumbing. **`jira attachment upload <ISSUE> <FILE>...`** accepts one or more files and streams each from disk (`ReaderStream`, never fully buffered) as repeated `file` parts of a single multipart `POST /rest/api/3/issue/{key}/attachments` with the `X-Atlassian-Token: no-check` XSRF header, printing metadata for every created attachment. **`jira attachment delete <ATTACHMENT_ID>`** issues `DELETE /rest/api/3/attachment/{id}` (permanent — JIRA has no trash/purge) behind the same destructive guard the Confluence delete uses: an interactive `[y/N]` confirm, `--dry-run` to preview without an API call, and `--force` to skip the prompt. Matching MCP tools ship too: **`jira_attachment_upload`** (takes `file_paths`, returns the created attachments as YAML) and **`jira_attachment_delete`** (guarded by `confirm: true`, mirroring the sibling `jira_delete` tool rather than deleting silently). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md#jira-attachments) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **Non-interactive Snowflake auth: programmatic access tokens and key-pair JWT** ([#1108](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1108)): external-browser SSO was the *only* implemented Snowflake auth method, so the daemon could not authenticate headless — no CI, servers, or display-less containers (the v2 client that would have brought these was declined in [#1003](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1003), leaving the capability gap untracked). Two non-interactive methods now sit alongside SSO on the same clean-room **v1** REST client, selected by a new `SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR` env var (resolved from the daemon's environment, then `settings.json`, once at startup): **`programmatic_access_token`** (alias `pat`) presents a PAT from `SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN` in place of a password (Snowflake requires the user to be covered by a network policy to use one), and **`snowflake_jwt`** (aliases `keypair`/`key_pair`/`jwt`) signs a short-lived RS256 JWT locally from an unencrypted PKCS#8 private key at `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH` (or inline `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY`), authenticating against the public key registered via `ALTER USER … SET RSA_PUBLIC_KEY`. Unset or `externalbrowser` keeps the interactive default byte-for-byte unchanged. All three finish through one shared `session/v1/login-request` path and yield the same session tokens, so the bounded session pool, transparent token renewal, and the keep-alive heartbeat are auth-method-agnostic and untouched. JWT signing uses `aws-lc-rs` (already in the tree via rustls — deliberately **not** the `rsa` crate, the RUSTSEC-2023-0071 reason `snowflake-connector-rs` was dropped); passphrase-encrypted keys are not yet supported, and setting `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSPHRASE` fails fast with decrypt guidance. No secret is persisted (in-memory only, wrapped so it stays out of logs); no CLI-surface or daemon wire-protocol change. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#authentication).
- **`SNOWFLAKE_HOST` override for PrivateLink and custom API hosts** ([#1224](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1224)): the Snowflake API host was always derived as `<account>.snowflakecomputing.com`, so AWS/Azure PrivateLink endpoints and gov/custom hosts were unreachable — the client already honoured a `host` override (`SnowflakeClientConfig.host` / `api_host()`) but nothing populated it. A new `SNOWFLAKE_HOST` env/`settings.json` key, when set, is used verbatim as the API host, resolving through `SnowflakeEngineConfig::from_env_and_settings` (via a `host_override_from` helper that trims and treats blank as unset) and threading into every pooled session in `create_session_with_base`. Engine-global and read once at daemon startup, consistent with the other config-only settings; unset keeps the derived host unchanged. No CLI-surface change. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#configuration).
- **`snowflake disconnect --id` / `--all` over the socket and CLI** ([#1228](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1228)): the engine already had `disconnect_all()` and `disconnect_by_id()`, but the daemon socket op and `omni-dev snowflake disconnect` exposed only the exact `{account, user}` pair form — the bulk and by-id variants were reachable only from the macOS tray, so non-macOS operators had to restart the daemon to bulk-evict sessions. The `disconnect` op/subcommand is overloaded with two more mutually-exclusive selectors: `--id <ID>` (a single pooled session) and `--all` (every session), guarded by a required clap `ArgGroup` so exactly one selector is given (account/user still require each other for the pair form). The socket reply now carries a `count` alongside `disconnected`, so existing callers of the by-id/pair reply stay wire-compatible. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md).
- **CSV/TSV output and `--out-file` for `snowflake query`** ([#1227](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1227)): `snowflake query` gains `csv` and `tsv` output formats — RFC 4180-compliant field quoting, so embedded quotes, commas, and newlines are escaped — alongside the existing JSON and YAML, plus an `--out-file <PATH>` flag that writes results to a file instead of stdout. Column ordering is preserved and duplicate column names are disambiguated with a numeric suffix (`SELECT 1, 1` yields a `_2` column), and every format now ends with a trailing newline for consistency. This makes query results directly consumable by spreadsheets and data pipelines while leaving the JSON/YAML workflows unchanged. See [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md).
- **`coverage diff --ignore-filename-regex` to exclude files from the delta** ([#1220](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1220)): a new repeatable (and comma-separated) `--ignore-filename-regex` flag on `coverage diff` excludes files from the coverage-delta computation, matching the semantics of `cargo llvm-cov --ignore-filename-regex`. Patterns are compiled into a `RegexSet` with upfront syntax validation and applied — after strip-prefix normalisation to repo-relative paths — **symmetrically** to both the head and baseline reports before the diff, so the two share the same denominator and `--fail-under-patch` gates plus indirect-change lists stay valid even when the baseline predates the exclusion rule. Wired through to the MCP `coverage_diff` tool as `ignore_filename_regex` (`serde(default)`). See [docs/coverage.md](docs/coverage.md) and [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **Worktrees service names the parent repo and marks linked worktrees** ([#1249](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1249)): `worktrees list`, `daemon status`, and the tray "Worktrees" submenu now identify which repository each open window belongs to. `GitStatus` gains `main_repo` (the parent repository name, derived from git's common dir — handling both the normal `.git`-subdirectory and bare `.git`-suffixed layouts) and an `is_worktree` flag, computed even for unborn/detached HEAD so a worktree always names its parent. A linked worktree now displays its parent repo name (not its folder basename) and is visually distinguished with a fork glyph (`⑂`), where a normal checkout uses `·`; the CLI table and tray labels prefer the daemon-computed `main_repo` over the companion-reported name. The tray also collapses to one clickable line per window (the live stats label doubles as the focus action) instead of a stats row plus a separate focus row. Both new fields are `#[serde(skip_serializing_if)]`, so the wire protocol stays backward-compatible. See [ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Cross-service request-log correlation for daemon-served requests** ([#1198](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1198)): when the CLI or MCP calls through the daemon (Snowflake queries, browser-bridge requests), the HTTP records the daemon spawned were logged under the *daemon's* own `invocation_id`, so `omni-dev log --id <cli-invocation>` could not surface both halves of one logical operation. The originating client's `invocation_id` is now threaded across the control socket (a new optional `origin_invocation_id` on `DaemonEnvelope`, set via `with_origin()`) and the browser bridge (a new `X-Omni-Bridge-Origin` header), and `request_log::scope_origin_id()` overrides the `invocation_id` for the duration of the daemon-side work — so daemon-triggered HTTP records now correlate back to the caller while still reading `via_daemon: true` (and preserving `source`). The origin id is non-secret (not a token). Backward compatible: older clients omit the field and the daemon skips correlation. Closes the follow-up split out of [#1122](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1122). See [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).

### Changed
- **Upgrade `rmcp` from 1.7.0 to 2.1.0** ([#1222](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1222)): bump the MCP SDK (`rmcp` + `rmcp-macros`) to 2.1.0 and migrate to its new API surface across all ten MCP tool modules and the resource handlers — `Content` → `ContentBlock`, the removed `RawContent` / `RawResource` / `RawResourceTemplate` intermediaries replaced by direct variants, and the `Resource::new()` builder with chainable `with_*()` methods. Internal migration only; the advertised tool/resource surface is unchanged (manifest + lockfile bump).
- **Bump the `rust-minor-patch` dependency group** ([#1221](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1221)): routine dependabot update of `clap_complete` (4.6.5 → 4.6.7) and `rand` (0.10.1 → 0.10.2). Manifest + lockfile bump; no source or behaviour changes.

### Fixed
- **Worktrees tray no longer pegs a core or stalls daemon shutdown** ([#1249](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1249)): the per-worktree git enrichment added in [#1186](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1186) ran on the macOS GUI thread on every tray `menu()` call, so its `git2` I/O pegged a core and could stall daemon shutdown. `WorktreesService` now holds the registry behind an `Arc` and caches a menu snapshot rebuilt by a background tokio task every 2s; `menu()` serves the cached snapshot (falling back to an inline compute before the first refresh or outside a tokio runtime), the tray event loop throttles service polling to at most once per second, and `shutdown()` cancels and joins the refresh task. The service is a no-op under unit tests (which run without a runtime). See [ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md).

### Documentation
- **Architecture decision records backfilled and amended for the current design** ([#1211](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1211), [#1212](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1212), [#1213](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1213), [#1214](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1214), [#1215](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1215), [#1216](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1216), [#1218](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1218), [#1219](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1219)): a batch of ADRs recording already-shipped behaviour — ADR-0041 (pushed-commit amendment guard), ADR-0042 (the request-log subsystem), ADR-0043 (default-on credential redaction), ADR-0044 (unified AI backend and model resolution), ADR-0045 (isolated named credential profiles), ADR-0046 (unified `-o`/`--output` format convention), and ADR-0047 (remote-first fail-closed base-branch resolution) — plus amendments to ADR-0027 (narrowing the MCP dry-run scope for create/write tools) and ADR-0039 / ADR-0040 (formalising the service engine/adapter split). The `dry_run` helper-scope documentation is also clarified ([#1238](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1238)), and stale git-worktree convention guidance is removed. Documentation only.

### CI/CD
- **Path-split gate to accelerate docs-only PRs** ([#609](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/609)): a new `gate` job runs `dorny/paths-filter` on pull requests and, for docs-only / skills-only changes (`*.md`, `docs/**`, `.claude/skills/**`; anything else fails safe to full CI), skips the heavy jobs while still publishing every required status context — so a docs PR merges without waiting on the full matrix and without bypassing branch protection (a skipped *step* inside a passing job reports success, not a skipped *conclusion*). Non-PR events (main pushes, release tags) always take the full path. Centralises the decision in one gate rather than a separate `ci-skip.yml` that would silently rot as the matrix or branch-protection rules change. See [docs/ci-path-split.md](docs/ci-path-split.md) ([#598](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/598)).

## [0.32.0] - 2026-07-06

### Added
- **Per-origin `--allow-origin` allowlist for the browser bridge** ([#1185](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1185)): `browser bridge serve --allow-origin` (and the daemon's `--bridge-allow-origin`) is now **repeatable** and scoped per connecting tab instead of a single global value. Each value is a bare `ORIGIN` (a tab on that origin may reach it) or a `CONNECT=OUTBOUND` mapping (a tab connecting from `CONNECT` may reach `OUTBOUND`); repeats under one connecting origin accumulate its outbound set. The WebSocket-upgrade gate now admits any connecting origin that is a configured **key**, and the per-request outbound-URL check reads the outbound set bound to the **tab a request is routed to** — so a Grafana tab and a Facebook tab each carry only their own grant and neither can borrow the other's scope. Default-closed is unchanged: with no `--allow-origin`, the gate stays open (the session token is the gate) and outbound is relative-URLs-only. The per-request `request --allow-origin` override still wins for a single request. Trust boundary unchanged; see [ADR-0036](docs/adrs/adr-0036.md) and [docs/browser-bridge.md](docs/browser-bridge.md#outbound-request-scope-default-closed).
- **MCP tools for the newer subsystems — Snowflake, request log, transcript, coverage, git amend, browser bridge — plus `jira_search` convenience filters** ([#1120](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1120)): the MCP server exposed 91 tools for the older subsystems (git/jira/confluence/atlassian/ai/config/datadog) but **zero** for the newer ones — the combined router in [`src/mcp/server.rs`](src/mcp/server.rs) stopped at eight families, so an agent could not run a Snowflake query, search its own request log, fetch a transcript, diff coverage, apply commit-message amendments, or drive an authenticated bridge request even though each already existed as a CLI command with a reusable, non-printing core. Ten tools across five new routers close the gap, each delegating to that same core (MCP is a transport, not a fork): **`snowflake_query`/`snowflake_sessions`/`snowflake_disconnect`** send `query`/`sessions`/`disconnect` ops over the daemon's Unix control socket (so they require a running daemon, and first-time `(account, user)` auth opens external-browser SSO on the daemon host); **`log_search`** reuses the CLI filter matrix + renderers (`oneline`/`json`/`full`), capturing output instead of streaming (`--follow` has no MCP form); **`transcript_youtube_fetch`/`info`/`list_langs`** call the `TranscriptSource` trait (the mutating `sync` is omitted); **`coverage_diff`** reuses `DiffCommand::run`, returning the rendered report + patch percentage + gate result and never failing the call (reports `below_gate` instead); **`git_amend_commits`** is the deterministic apply-messages-from-YAML counterpart to `git_twiddle_commits`, taking the amendments as an inline YAML string (a new `AmendmentFile::from_yaml_str` + non-printing `run_amend` core) and refusing already-pushed commits unless `allow_pushed` is set; **`browser_bridge_request`** drives one non-streaming request through a running authenticated tab via the shared `BridgeClient::send` (not read-only — a non-GET method can mutate remote state; `serve`/`harvest` remain CLI-only). Separately, the MCP **`jira_search`** tool now accepts the `project`/`assignee`/`status` convenience filters the CLI has (with `jql` made optional) by reusing an extracted `build_jql_from_filters` shared with the CLI; its `fields` parameter remains the documented informational no-op it already was. The `sync`, `serve`, `harvest`, and daemon-lifecycle surfaces stay intentionally CLI-only. See [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md#tool-catalog).
- **`daemon start` auto-starts at login on Linux via a systemd user unit** ([#1174](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1174)): `omni-dev daemon start` gains login persistence on Linux, mirroring the macOS launchd LaunchAgent (the deferred second half of [#1112](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1112), whose detach fix already landed). On a host with a systemd **user** manager it now writes a socket-activated `omni-dev-daemon.socket` + `omni-dev-daemon.service` pair under `~/.config/systemd/user/` and runs `systemctl --user enable --now omni-dev-daemon.socket`: systemd owns the control socket (created `0600` via `SocketMode`) and demand-spawns `daemon run` on the first client connect, and `WantedBy=sockets.target` re-activates it at login. `daemon run` adopts the inherited descriptor via a hand-rolled `sd_listen_fds` implementation ([`src/daemon/systemd.rs`](src/daemon/systemd.rs); reads `LISTEN_FDS`/`LISTEN_PID`, no libsystemd dependency, and sets `FD_CLOEXEC` so the socket is not leaked into child processes), reusing the same socket-activation shutdown invariant as macOS (the manager owns the inode, so shutdown does not unlink it). `daemon stop` stops and disables the socket unit — the analogue of launchd's `bootout` — so it stays down. When no user manager is available (containers, non-systemd distros, or the new `OMNI_DEV_DAEMON_DISABLE_SYSTEMD=1` escape hatch) `start` falls back to the existing detached `setsid` spawn with no login auto-start, exactly as before. One Linux-specific wrinkle: because systemd re-arms the socket the instant the service goes inactive, `daemon restart` no longer polls the daemon *down* (a probe `connect` would re-activate it) — it shuts down and lets the relaunch plus readiness ping drive a clean handoff. See [ADR-0039](docs/adrs/adr-0039.md) and [docs/browser-bridge.md](docs/browser-bridge.md#running-under-the-daemon).
- **Global `--model` / `--beta-header` flags and `--ai-backend openai|ollama|bedrock`** ([#1118](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1118)): `--model` and `--beta-header` are now global flags on the root command (propagated as `OMNI_DEV_MODEL` / `OMNI_DEV_BETA_HEADER`), so the documented invocation shape `omni-dev --model claude-opus-4-6 git commit message twiddle …` — which clap previously rejected outright — parses, and the old post-subcommand placement keeps working via clap's `global = true`. The per-subcommand copies on `ai chat`, `git commit message twiddle|check|staged`, and `git branch create pr` are removed (both flag positions still parse; MCP tools keep their explicit `model` parameters, which outrank the env). `--ai-backend` is extended from `{default, claude-cli}` to `{default, claude-cli, openai, ollama, bedrock}`, so the three backends that were previously reachable only through `USE_OPENAI`/`USE_OLLAMA`/`CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK` env vars are now first-class flag values. `OMNI_DEV_MODEL` slots in above the per-family model vars and, like every resolver input, is settable via `~/.omni-dev/settings.json` env bundles/profiles. See [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md#dispatch-order-and-model-selection).
- **`--set-field` accepts comma-separated values for array-typed fields** ([#1172](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1172)): array-typed JIRA fields (labels, components, fix versions, multi-selects) previously required YAML flow syntax — `--set-field 'Labels=a,b,c'` YAML-parsed to a scalar string and was rejected with `expected a sequence for array field 'Labels'`, and the error never named the `[a, b, c]` form users needed, so most looped through several syntaxes before landing on it. The shared field resolver now treats a scalar on an array-typed field (schema `type: array`) as comma-separated shorthand: split on `,`, each element whitespace-trimmed, empty elements dropped — so `Labels=a,b,c`, `Labels= a , b ,c`, and `Labels=[a, b, c]` all send the identical wire payload, and a lone scalar (`Labels=urgent`) becomes a one-element array. Element shaping and `allowedValues` validation are unchanged (labels stay bare strings, components/versions `{"name": …}`, options `{"value": …}`); sequence elements are never split, so `Labels=["a,b"]` remains the escape hatch for a literal comma inside one element, and `Labels=""` clears the field. Scalar (non-array) fields are untouched — a comma in `Summary=Fix bug, add test` stays literal. Because the split lives in the shared resolver, it covers CLI `jira write --set-field` / `jira create --set-field` and the MCP `jira_edit`/`jira_create` field maps alike; `--set-field` remains **replace**, not append. CLI `--dry-run` still prints the raw pre-conversion value (it deliberately makes no network call, so field schemas are unknown at that point). See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md#jira-reading-and-writing-issues).
- **Per-commit `in_main_branches` is now populated, and amend refuses already-pushed commits** ([#1105](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1105)): two coupled gaps from the #1103 functionality-gap analysis are closed. First, the documented `commits[].in_main_branches` field ("array of remote main branches that contain this commit") had been a permanent empty stub since its introduction — every commit, including ones on `origin/main`, reported `[]`, so any consumer (human or AI) inferred every commit was unpushed. A new [`src/git/main_branches.rs`](src/git/main_branches.rs) module resolves each remote's main-branch tip **locally and offline** (symbolic `refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD`, else the `main`/`master`/`develop` common-name fallback; remotes that resolve neither are skipped conservatively rather than guessed) and checks containment per commit via the git2 commit graph — tips are computed once per invocation, not per commit. Second, that containment now gates the amend path: `validate_commit_amendable` (the chokepoint all three amend surfaces — `amend`, `twiddle`, and `check --twiddle` — funnel through) refuses to amend a commit contained in a remote main branch, naming the short hash, the containing branches, and the escape hatch. A new `--allow-pushed` flag on `amend` and `twiddle` overrides the refusal (with a printed warning); the MCP `git_twiddle_commits` tool deliberately does **not** expose the override — an AI caller silently rewriting published history is exactly the failure mode the guard exists for, so overriding requires a human running the CLI flag. (`CommitInfo::from_git_commit` gains a `main_tips` parameter — technically API-breaking for library consumers.) See the [user guide](docs/user-guide.md#safety-already-pushed-commits).
- **MCP `jira_edit` tool — set arbitrary fields on an existing issue by name** ([#1157](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1157)): once a ticket was created, changing labels or custom fields (Acceptance Criteria, Story Points, …) from MCP forced a fallback to raw `curl -X PUT /rest/api/3/issue/{key}` with a hand-authored payload — `jira_write`'s `fields` escape hatch takes only canonical ids with already-API-shaped values. The new `jira_edit` tool takes an issue key plus a `{field-name-or-id: value}` map, resolves names against the issue's edit screen (`editmeta`), and coerces each value to the API shape with the same shared resolver the CLI uses: select/option strings become `{"value": …}`, multi-selects arrays of those, labels plain string arrays, number/date scalars pass through, issue-link fields (e.g. `Parent`) wrap an issue key as `{"key": …}`, and rich-text fields accept JFM markdown (auto-converted to ADF; `""` clears) or a raw ADF document object (`{"type": "doc", …}`, validated and forwarded as-is). Supports `dry_run: true` (previews the exact `PUT` method/path/body like the other mutating tools) and returns `{status: ok, key, updated_fields}` as YAML. Unlike `jira_write`'s pass-through hatch, an `editmeta` failure is fatal — resolution is the tool's contract. Mirrors the CLI's existing `omni-dev atlassian jira write --set-field`; no new CLI subcommand (the CLI `jira edit` name belongs to the interactive editor). See [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md).
- **`*_path` file alternatives to inline bodies on write-side MCP tools** ([#1093](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1093)): an AI caller pays an O(size) generation cost to emit a large body inline through a tool call — the model writes every byte through its output stream — so writing a 70 KB Confluence page could take minutes just to *emit the tool call*, versus seconds for the same bytes read from disk. This is the write-side mirror of `confluence_read`/`jira_read`'s `output_file` (which already solved the symmetric read-side cost). Every text body a write tool accepts inline now has an optional sibling parameter the server reads from a filesystem path instead: `confluence_write`/`jira_write` gain `content_path`; `confluence_create` gains `content_path` + `document_path`; `jira_create` gains `description_path` + `document_path`; `confluence_comment_add`/`confluence_comment_add_inline` gain `content_path`; `jira_comment`/`jira_comment_edit` gain `body_path`; and `ai_chat` gains `message_path`. The inline and path forms are **mutually exclusive** (supplying both errors before any network call), and where the body is required exactly one must be given. Binary uploads (`confluence_attachment_upload`) already stream from disk via `file_path`, so no inline-base64 path was ever introduced. A shared [`src/mcp/content_input.rs`](src/mcp/content_input.rs) helper (`resolve_content_input`/`require_content_input`) centralises the either/or resolution and mutual-exclusion check, and each tool's description now says to prefer the path form when the body is already on disk. A diff-style edit tool (the issue's second capability) is intentionally deferred as separate, larger work. See [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md#content_path-on-write-tools).
- **Account-ID → user lookup for JIRA and Confluence** ([#1091](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1091)): author, assignee, and reporter fields come back as opaque `accountId`s, and the only existing resolver went the other way (display name → id via user search). New `omni-dev atlassian jira user get` / `confluence user get` commands and MCP tools `jira_user_get` / `confluence_user_get` resolve the reverse via `GET /rest/api/3/user?accountId=` (JIRA) and `GET /wiki/rest/api/user?accountId=` (Confluence). Lookups are **batch and failure-tolerant**: an unknown, anonymised, or permission-denied id yields a stub record carrying an `error` field (HTTP status + body snippet) rather than failing the whole batch, while a `401` stays a hard error. The read-side tools whose output surfaces bare account ids (`jira_read`, `jira_comment`, `jira_changelog`, `confluence_read`, `confluence_history`, `confluence_comment`) now cross-reference the new lookup in their descriptions. Table and JSON output for user records. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **Inline-comment drift auditing and re-anchoring for Confluence** ([#1092](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1092)): Confluence anchors an inline comment to a run of text via annotation marks, and when that text is later edited it keeps the marks on whatever original characters survive — leaving comments *torn* across fragments, *drifted* onto unrelated text, or silently dropped. New `confluence comment audit` classifies every inline comment on a page as `Ok`/`Torn`/`MarkLost`/`Drifted` (read-only), and `confluence comment reanchor` moves a comment's anchor to a new run and writes the page back in one ADF update — including anchors split across formatting runs, with 1-based match indexing to disambiguate repeated text. Both ship as CLI commands (confirmation prompts + `--dry-run`) and MCP tools (`confluence_comment_audit` / `confluence_comment_reanchor`); the re-anchor logic operates entirely on typed ADF (never JFM) so it never disturbs surrounding marks, and validates the result before writing. `confluence read` also gains a `--version` flag to fetch a historical snapshot. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **Request-log growth bounds: size-capped rotation and `omni-dev log prune`** ([#1121](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1121)): the append-only `log.jsonl` had no built-in cap. Two opt-in mechanisms now bound it. **Automatic rotation** (unix-only, env-gated): `OMNI_DEV_LOG_MAX_SIZE` (e.g. `10mb`) rotates before an append that would exceed the cap, shifting numbered files (`log.jsonl.1`, `.2`, …) and keeping at most `OMNI_DEV_LOG_KEEP_FILES` (default 3); writers serialize the check-rotate-append on a stable `log.jsonl.lock`, and a rotation failure degrades to a plain append (best-effort). **Explicit pruning**: the new `omni-dev log prune` subcommand takes `--older-than <DUR>` (`7d`, `24h`) and/or `--max-size <SIZE>` (at least one required), rewrites the file atomically via a same-directory temp file + rename (preserving `0600`, so concurrent readers never see a half-written file), conservatively keeps records with missing/unparseable timestamps and always retains the most recent record, and previews with `--dry-run`. The `-f/--follow` reader already tolerates rotation and truncation. See [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).
- **Binary request bodies for the browser bridge** ([#1123](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1123)): `browser bridge request` could send only UTF-8 text bodies, so uploading an image, protobuf, or gzip payload through an authenticated tab was impossible — the symmetric gap to the binary *response* support the bridge already had. A new `--body-file` flag (mutually exclusive with `--body`) reads a file as raw bytes and base64-encodes them on the wire; the in-page snippet decodes back to a byte array before `fetch()`, so bytes reach the upstream server byte-exact. An optional `encoding` field on the `ControlRequest`/`Command` wire types carries the tag under `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = …)]`, so a client that omits it stays byte-identical on the wire (absent = UTF-8 text) and the trust boundary is unchanged; the transparent proxy never tags an encoding, so its behaviour is untouched. See [docs/browser-bridge.md](docs/browser-bridge.md).
- **Per-worktree git enrichment in the worktrees service** ([#1186](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1186)): `worktrees list`, `daemon status`, and the tray "Worktrees" submenu now show each window's **live** git state — current branch and ahead/behind counts (`main (+2 -1)`) — instead of the companion-reported title, keeping the companion extension thin. The daemon's service adapter computes a `GitStatus` (via `git2`) on every read from each window's primary folder, off the registry lock (on a blocking thread for the control socket, inline for tray rendering), so values are always current and add no error path: a folder that is not a repo, is on a detached HEAD, or has no upstream still appears with only the fields it can supply. Every enrichment field is `#[serde(skip_serializing_if)]`, so the wire protocol stays backward-compatible (older clients ignore the new optional keys). See [ADR-0040](docs/adrs/adr-0040.md) and [docs/worktrees-service.md](docs/worktrees-service.md).
- **Provider-less `omni-dev transcript fetch <url>` auto-detect** ([#1187](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1187)): a new `transcript fetch <url>` path probes every registered transcript source in priority order and dispatches to the first that recognises the locator, so callers no longer need to know which provider subcommand to use. Detection is **pure string inspection** — an unrecognised locator fails fast with no network call. It mirrors the per-source fetch subcommands' arguments (`--lang`, `--format`, `--auto`, `--translate`, plus an output destination); a new `transcript::detect` module iterates each source's static `matches` method and constructs the first match as `Box<dyn TranscriptSource>`, and registration order sets priority. See [docs/transcript.md](docs/transcript.md).

### Changed
- **Unified machine-readable output selection on `-o`/`--output`; reserved `--out-file` for file destinations** ([#1125](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1125)): "how do I get JSON?" had four competing answers, and the `-o`/`--output` short flag meant *output format* on list commands but *output file* on `jira read` / `confluence read` / `confluence attachment download` — so `omni-dev atlassian jira read PROJ-1 -o json` silently wrote the issue to a file literally named `json`. Every format selector is now `-o`/`--output <enum>` with a per-command value set: the `--format` flag on `snowflake query`, `coverage diff`, `git commit message check` (also upgraded from a loosely-parsed string that silently fell back to `text` on an invalid value to a validated `ValueEnum` that errors), `log`, and `transcript youtube fetch`; the short-less `--output` on `transcript youtube info` / `list-langs` (now also accepts `-o`); and the boolean `--json` on `daemon status`, `snowflake sessions`, and `worktrees list` (now `-o`/`--output table|json`). File destinations move to a dedicated `--out-file <PATH>` with **no `-o` short**, so `-o` never means "file" again (`jira read PROJ-1 -o json` now errors instead of writing a file). Every old spelling keeps working as a hidden alias that prints `warning: --X is deprecated; use --Y instead` to stderr and will be removed in a future major. **One breaking edge:** on `transcript youtube fetch` the `--output` name is repurposed from the output *file* to the output *format*, so `transcript youtube fetch <id> --output out.srt` now errors (invalid format value) — use `--out-file out.srt`. `confluence compare run` keeps its deliberate `yaml` default; `transcript youtube sync` keeps `--format` (it already has an `--out` directory flag that would collide with `-o`). Unchanged: content-representation `--format jfm|adf` on jira/confluence read/create/comment, `confluence compare section --format`, `--output-dir`, and `ai claude history sync --output-format`. See [docs/user-guide.md](docs/user-guide.md).
- **`--ai-backend` (any value, including `default`) decisively overrides the legacy `USE_*` env soup; unknown `OMNI_DEV_AI_BACKEND` values are now a hard error** ([#1118](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1118)): `--ai-backend default` previously only *removed* `OMNI_DEV_AI_BACKEND`, so with `USE_OLLAMA=true` exported there was no flag that could force the direct Anthropic API. The flag now always sets `OMNI_DEV_AI_BACKEND` to the chosen value, and when that variable is set it decides the backend outright — the legacy `USE_OLLAMA` → `USE_OPENAI` → `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK` order applies only when it is unset. A set-but-unrecognized value (e.g. a typo like `claudecli`) previously fell through *silently* to the `USE_*`/default chain and dispatched a different backend with different credentials; it now fails fast with an error listing the valid values.
- **claude-cli sandbox-weakened warnings name where the escape hatch was enabled** ([#1143](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1143)): the per-invocation `WARN` for the tool-access and MCP-access escape hatches previously said only *that* the sandbox was weakened — an intentional one-off `--claude-cli-allow-tools` looked identical to a forgotten `export OMNI_DEV_CLAUDE_CLI_ALLOW_TOOLS=true` in `.zshrc` or a sticky `settings.json` `env` entry. Each warning now ends with an `Enabled via …` clause naming the source: command-line flag, process environment variable (e.g. a shell export), or the base/profile `env` map in `$HOME/.omni-dev/settings.json` — so the sticky cases point at the exact setting to remove. Built on a new sourced resolution seam in [`src/utils/settings.rs`](src/utils/settings.rs) (`EnvValueSource`, `Settings::resolve_with_source`, `get_env_var_sourced`) plus a flag-provenance registry fed by `Cli::propagate_global_flags`, available for any future warning that should name its source. Warning text and docs only — hatch semantics, precedence, and defaults are unchanged. See [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md#escape-hatch-tool-access) and [ADR-0028](docs/adrs/adr-0028.md).

- **MCP description quality now has an automated backstop; two sibling-tool gaps closed** ([#1072](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1072)): the v0.31.0 audit brought every MCP tool/parameter description up to the [STYLE-0029](docs/STYLE_GUIDE.md) bar, but nothing prevented the next new tool from silently regressing it — the only test spot-checked three tools. A new `all_tools_advertise_descriptions_and_param_schemas` test in [tests/mcp_integration_test.rs](tests/mcp_integration_test.rs) now asserts that *every* advertised tool **and** every top-level parameter ships a non-empty description (the checklist's "present" floor; prose quality still needs review). It also closes the two remaining "use X vs Y" gaps the audit missed: `jira_board_issues`↔`jira_sprint_issues` and `confluence_read`↔`confluence_download` now cross-reference each other so an agent picks the right one. MCP tool-description text only — no CLI `--help` or `docs/mcp.md` changes.
- **Bump `quick-xml` from 0.40.1 to 0.41.0** ([#1095](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1095)): routine update of the XML parser used on the Atlassian/ADF paths. Manifest + lockfile bump; no source or behaviour changes.

### Refactored
- **Shared CLI output renderer extracted to `src/cli/format.rs`** ([#1125](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1125)): the `OutputFormat` enum plus the `write_output`/`output_as` machinery and the `JsonlSerialize` trait — previously duplicated verbatim between [`src/cli/atlassian/format.rs`](src/cli/atlassian/format.rs) and [`src/cli/datadog/format.rs`](src/cli/datadog/format.rs) (the latter carried a standing TODO to extract it) — now live in one shared `crate::cli::format` module. Atlassian keeps only its collection-wrapper `JsonlSerialize` impls; Datadog re-exports the shared module. A new shared `TableOrJson` enum backs the three commands migrated off boolean `--json`. No behaviour change.
- **settings.json env-map writes share one home** ([#1153](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1153)): `atlassian::auth::save_credentials` inlined the whole "read settings.json → ensure `env` object → merge keys → write pretty" sequence that `datadog::auth` had already factored into private helpers. Those helpers are promoted to [`src/utils/settings.rs`](src/utils/settings.rs) as `Settings::upsert_env_vars` / `Settings::remove_env_vars` (operating on a generic JSON value, so unknown fields and profiles are preserved verbatim), and both auth modules are rewritten over them — giving the write side the same single home the read side already has in `Settings::get_env_var`. The Atlassian module also gains the Datadog-style `save_credentials_to` path-injection seam, so its save test no longer redirects `HOME` (STYLE-0028 / issue #1030). No behaviour change beyond the permission hardening below.
- **Shared HTTP response-handling helpers for the Atlassian and Datadog clients** ([#1151](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1151)): 50+ inline "check status → extract error body → deserialize JSON" blocks copied across the Atlassian client and the Datadog `*_api.rs` modules are replaced by a small set of helpers — Atlassian's `ensure_success` / `parse_json` and Datadog's `api_url` / `parse_response` / `get_parsed` — so response validation and error diagnostics are consistent and future changes land in one place. No behaviour change.
- **HTTP 429 retry consolidated into one shared driver** ([#1152](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1152)): six open-coded rate-limit retry loops (GET/PUT/POST/DELETE in Atlassian, GET/POST in Datadog, plus the Claude AI path) are replaced by a single `retry_429` driver in a new `utils::http`, centralising retry scheduling, backoff, and rate-limit header handling (`Retry-After` and `X-RateLimit-Reset`) — which also brings `X-RateLimit-Reset` awareness to the Atlassian client for the first time. A companion `request_log::record_http_result` helper collapses the per-call `match Ok/Err` around `record_http`, and the loop's previously-unreachable `unreachable!()` tail is rewritten as a counted `loop` so every line is covered. Callers are unchanged; the refactor is internal.
- **Worktrees engine split from its daemon adapter** ([#1154](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1154)): the worktrees registry logic is extracted into a standalone `crate::worktrees` module (`WorktreesRegistry`, `RegisterRequest`, `WindowEntry`), mirroring the engine/adapter split the browser bridge and Snowflake services already use. The registry now owns the `Mutex<HashMap>`, TTL reaping, entry cap/eviction, and the core `register`/`heartbeat`/`unregister`/`list`/`first_folder` ops, while `WorktreesService` shrinks to adapter duties — routing control-socket ops, rendering the tray menu/status, and driving the VS Code launcher. No behaviour change.
- **Last `mod.rs` module roots migrated to named files (STYLE-0004)** ([#1155](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1155)): the remaining 19 `mod.rs` roots across the daemon, snowflake, browser, atlassian, cli, and coverage subsystems are renamed to the `foo.rs`-beside-`foo/` layout — **no `mod.rs` file remains under `src/`**. Pure file renames (none of the modules use path-relative macros like `#[path]` / `include!`, so Rust resolves them identically), with a stale `mod.rs` pointer in `CLAUDE.md` refreshed.
- **JIRA and Confluence wire types split into dedicated DTO modules** ([#1156](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1156)): ~2700 lines of REST wire types are lifted out of the monolithic `client.rs` / `confluence_api.rs` into new `src/atlassian/jira_types.rs` (issues, users, comments, search, edit/create-meta, Agile board/sprint/dev-status) and `src/atlassian/confluence_types.rs` (comments, attachments, spaces, users, labels, versions, search), leaving those modules to focus on HTTP transport. All types stay exported through their original modules via re-exports, so no downstream import breaks. No behaviour change.

### Fixed
- **Request log: transcript HTTP now recorded, AI backends split by service, and exit-code/duration/`--until` filters added** ([#1122](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1122)): four coverage/filter gaps from the [#1103](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1103) analysis are closed. **(1)** The transcript/YouTube transport made live `reqwest` calls that no `record_http` hook ever saw — every InnerTube `/player`·`/browse` POST, the timedtext GET, the watch-page bootstrap, and the channel resolve/RSS GETs now record a `service: "transcript"` HTTP line (via a shared `record_yt_http` helper mirroring the datadog hook), so `omni-dev log --service transcript` finally shows them. **(2)** All AI backends previously logged as `service: "claude"`, making Bedrock/OpenAI/Ollama traffic indistinguishable and leaving the OpenAI/Ollama context probes unrecorded entirely; `record_ai_http` now takes a `service` + `method`, so the direct Anthropic API tags `anthropic`, and `bedrock`/`openai`/`ollama` (chosen per `is_ollama`) each tag distinctly — the LM Studio (`GET /api/v0/models`) and Ollama (`POST /api/show`) probes included. **(3)** `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `elapsed_ms`, `hostname`, `system_user`, `cwd`, and `auth_principal` were on every record but reachable only through `--grep`; they're now first-class `--query` fields (with aliases `exit`/`duration`·`dur`/`elapsed`/`host`/`user`/`principal`), and the numeric ones — plus `status` — accept comparison operators (`elapsed:>1000`, `exit_code:>0`, `status:>=400`), so "slow requests" and "failed runs" are expressible without piping to `jq`. **(4)** `--since` was relative-only; it and the new `--until` flag now each accept a relative window, a `YYYY-MM-DD` date, or an RFC3339 timestamp, giving a bounded historical window (`--since 2026-07-01 --until 2026-07-02`). A docs caveat also notes that GitHub traffic issued via the `gh` subprocess runs out of process and is not captured. Cross-socket daemon correlation (a `via_daemon` request carrying the daemon's `invocation_id` instead of the originating CLI's) is split out as a tracked follow-up ([#1198](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1198)). See [docs/log.md](docs/log.md).
- **`CLAUDE_MODEL` / `CLAUDE_CODE_MODEL` are honoured by every Claude-family backend, via one shared resolver** ([#1118](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1118)): [docs/ai-backends.md](docs/ai-backends.md) promised every backend resolved `--model` → `CLAUDE_MODEL` → `CLAUDE_CODE_MODEL` → `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` → provider var → default, but only the `claude-cli` backend implemented the chain — the direct Claude API and Bedrock read only `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, so `export CLAUDE_MODEL=claude-opus-4-6` (a spelling the docs blessed) silently fell through to the default model. Backend, model, and beta-header selection now live in one shared resolver ([`src/claude/backend.rs`](src/claude/backend.rs)) used by both the client factory and preflight, ending the byte-for-byte duplicated switch ADR-0028 required to be updated in lock-step. The chain is deliberately **scoped per family** (and the docs updated to match): Claude-family backends (direct API, Bedrock, claude-cli) read `CLAUDE_MODEL` → `CLAUDE_CODE_MODEL` → `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`; OpenAI reads `OPENAI_MODEL` and Ollama `OLLAMA_MODEL` only — so a Claude model id can never leak into a non-Claude backend. Resolution now also skips *empty* values ("first non-empty value", as documented), and the OpenAI branch's dead `gpt-5` registry-miss fallback literal is corrected to the registry default `gpt-5-mini`.
- **`--profile` now applies to `snowflake query` defaults** ([#1110](https://github.com/rust-works/omni-dev/issues/1110)): `omni-dev --profile work snowflake query …` silently ignored the profile — the `SNOWFLAKE_*` defaults were resolved **once at daemon startup** from the daemon's own environment, while the thin CLI client forwarded only its raw flags (usually absent) over the control socket, so `--profile` set `OMNI_DEV_PROFILE` in a process the resolution never ran in. Following the documented multi-account advice therefore queried the startup profile's tenant with no error. The CLI now resolves the six identity/context defaults (`SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT`/`USER`/`WAREHOUSE`/`ROLE`/`DATABASE`/`SCHEMA`) **client-side** via the same profile-aware env-then-`settings.json` chain the Atlassian/Datadog commands use (`QueryRequest::fill_defaults_from`), and sends the resolved values with the query request; precedence is explicit flag > client env > active profile (or base) `settings.json` env > daemon startup defaults. The wire protocol is unchanged (existing fields, now populated; unset fields are omitted from the payload), so bare socket clients that resolve nothing keep the daemon-startup fallback. Pool size and timeouts remain daemon-startup (config-only) settings. [docs/snowflake-service.md](docs/snowflake-service.md#configuration) now states where each setting resolves. Also reported as #1138 in the #1101 security review.
